Counting surfaces on Calabi-Yau 4-folds I: foundations
Younghan Bae, Martijn Kool, and Hyeonjun Park

Abstract
. This is the first part in a series of papers on counting surfaces on Calabi-Yau 4-folds. Besides the Hilbert scheme of 2-dimensional subschemes, we introduce two types of moduli spaces of stable pairs. We show that all three moduli spaces are related by GIT wall-crossing and parametrize stable objects in the bounded derived category. We construct reduced Oh-Thomas virtual cycles on the moduli spaces via Kiem-Li cosection localization and prove that they are deformation invariant along Hodge loci. As an application, we show that the variational Hodge conjecture holds for any family of Calabi-Yau 4-folds supporting a non-zero reduced virtual cycle.



1.
Introduction

1.1.
Background

Counting curves on a space
X
is one of the central topics in modern enumerative geometry. It is a rich subject with links to many other fields of mathematics as well as physics (string theory).
In
[
Kon
]
Kontsevich introduced the concept of
hidden smoothness
of moduli spaces of stable maps in order to define algebraic Gromov-Witten (GW) invariants of
X
. The mathematical foundation of its virtual cycles was established by Li-Tian
[
LT
]
and Behrend-Fantechi
[
BF
]
. When
X
is a
3-fold
, an alternative enumeration can be carried out using Grothendieck’s Hilbert schemes. This change of perspective represents the difference between viewing curves on
X
as being parametrized or cut out by equations. In
[
Th
]
, Thomas then defined virtual cycles on Hilbert schemes of a 3-fold
X
, which yields Donaldson-Thomas (DT) invariants
1
1

1

[
Th
]
defines DT invariants for stable sheaves of any rank on Fano and Calabi-Yau
3
-folds.
of
X
. A remarkable connection between GW and DT invariants for 3-folds was discovered by Maulik-Nekrasov-Okounkov-Pandharipande
[
MNOP
]
. This correspondence was further refined by Pandharipande-Thomas via stable pair (PT) invariants
[
PT1
,
PT2
,
PT3
]
. We refer the reader to
[
PT4
]
for an overview.

It was recently found that a similar relation between curve counting invariants exists on
Calabi-Yau 4-folds

X
. The structure of GW invariants of
X
was studied by Klemm and Pandharipande
[
KP
]
. On the sheaf side, the obstruction theories are not 2-term, and a new discovery was needed. Inspired by work of Donaldson and Thomas
[
DT
]
, Cao and Leung
[
CL
]
defined such a virtual cycle in special cases. The general case was subsequently established by Borisov and Joyce
[
BJ
]
using methods of derived differential geometry. The algebraic counterpart was obtained by Oh and Thomas
[
OT1
]
. Both constructions
[
BJ
,
OT1
]
deeply rely on the derived structure of the moduli space — the shifted symplectic structures of Pantev-Toën-Vaquié-Vezzosi
[
PTVV
]
and the derived Darboux theorem of Brav-Bussi-Joyce
[
BBJ
]
.
Another feature of the theory is that it requires orientations.
The existence of orientations was shown by Cao-Gross-Joyce
[
CGJ
]
.
An explicit GW-PT correspondence on Calabi-Yau 4-folds was conjectured by Cao, Maulik and Toda
[
CMT1
,
CMT2
,
CT3
]
. Virtual counts of points and curves on Calabi-Yau 4-folds recently attracted quite some attention. In physics, Nekrasov-Piazzalunga’s Magnificent Four conjecture
[
Nek2
,
NP
]

provides a formula for counting points on the Calabi-Yau 4-fold
\mathbb{C}^{4}
. For the case of counting points on
X
we refer to
[
CK1
,
Par1
,
Boj
,
KR
]
and for the case of counting curves on
X
we refer to
[
CK2
,
CKM
,
CT1
,
CT2
,
Par1
]
.

The next natural step in enumerative geometry is counting higher-dimensional objects, such as surfaces on Calabi-Yau 4-folds — the topic of this paper. It is not clear at present how to define virtual cycles on the stable map side (e.g., see Alexeev
[
Ale
]
for the moduli space and Jiang
[
Jia
]
for some recent developments).
On the sheaf side, a natural starting point is the virtual cycle of
[
BJ
,
OT1
]
on Hilbert schemes of 2-dimensional subschemes. However, we will see that for enumeration of surfaces, this virtual cycle generally does
not
suffice and has to be modified.

1.2.
Counting surfaces

The aim of this project is to count surfaces on Calabi-Yau 4-folds
X
via sheaf theory. This goal leads to two immediate challenges.

(A)

The Hilbert scheme parametrizes subschemes which are not pure dimensional, i.e. 2-dimensional subschemes may have 0- and 1-dimensional components. Informally: Hilbert schemes allow for free roaming curves and points thereby obscuring the contribution of genuine surfaces.

(B)

A Hodge class of type
(2,2)
on
X
generally does not remain of type
(2,2)
as the complex structure of
X
deforms. When this happens, the virtual cycle vanishes.

The main purpose of this paper is to resolve (A) and (B).

Inspired by Pandharipande-Thomas’s work on curves on 3-folds
[
PT1
]
, we settle (A) by constructing moduli spaces of stable pairs on
X
. We find that for 2-dimensional pairs, there is more structure than for 1-dimensional pairs, and we obtain
two
types which we call
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
and
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pairs. The stable pair moduli spaces give smaller compactifications — roughly speaking,
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs prevent free roaming points on
X
and
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pairs prevent free roaming points
and
curves on
X
. The Hilbert scheme and two stable pair moduli spaces each have desirable features and should be studied together.

We overcome (B) by constructing reduced virtual cycles using the cosection localization method of Kiem and Li
[
KL
]
.
This method was generalized to the case of
isotropic cosections
by Kiem and the third-named author
[
KiPa2
]
.
Instead, in this paper we use a variant, namely
non-degenerate cosections
.
For a class
\gamma\in H^{2}(X,\Omega^{2}_{X})\cap H^{4}(X,\mathbb{Q})
, virtual cycles of moduli spaces parametrizing 2-dimensional pairs in class
\gamma
often vanish.
Similar vanishing for curve counting theories on surfaces/3-folds led to
reduced theories
e.g.
[
MPT
,
KT1
]
(we refer the reader to the introductions of these papers for more references). From the perspective of curves on surfaces/3-folds
X
, reducing is usually needed when
X
has a non-zero holomorphic 2-form. However, for compact Calabi-Yau 3-folds satisfying
h^{0,1}(X)=h^{0,2}(X)=0
, it is of no concern. As we will see, even on compact Calabi-Yau 4-folds satisfying
h^{0,1}(X)=h^{0,2}(X)=h^{0,3}(X)=0
reducing is necessary for most interesting surface classes
\gamma
.

1.3.
Moduli spaces of stable pairs

There are three types of stability conditions on
2
-dimensional pairs.

Definition 1.1

(Definition
2.1
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety of dimension
n\geq 2
.
Let
F
be a 2-dimensional coherent sheaf on
X
,
s:\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F
a section, and
Q:=\operatorname{coker}(s)
. The pair
(F,s)
is called

(i)

\mathrm{DT}(=\mathrm{PT}_{-1})
-stable
if
s
is surjective,

(ii)

\mathrm{PT}_{0}
-stable
if
T_{0}(F)=0
and
\dim(Q)\leq 0
,

(iii)

\mathrm{PT}_{1}
-stable
if
T_{1}(F)=0
and
\dim(Q)\leq 1
.

Here
T_{d}(F)
denotes the maximal
d
-dimensional subsheaf of
F
.

The
\mathrm{DT}
-stable pairs correspond to ideal sheaves.
The
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
-stable pairs are natural generalizations of Pandharipande-Thomas’s stable pairs
[
PT1
]
, which are a special case of Le Potier’s coherent systems
[
Pot1
,
Pot2
]
.
The underlying sheaf of a
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
-stable pair is usually
not
pure and this stability condition appears to be new.
We introduce
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
-stability as a bridge between
\mathrm{DT}
- and
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
-stability.

Suppose
(F,s)
is a
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair on
X
with cokernel
Q
and
smooth
scheme-theoretic support
S
, which generally has 0-, 1-, and 2-dimensional connected components.
For
q=0
,
S
has no 0-dimensional connected components and
Q
is supported on the 1-dimensional connected components of
S
. For
q=1
,
S
has no 0- or 1-dimensional components and the pair
(F,s)
can be equivalently described by a nested triple
Z\subset C\subset S
, where
C
is an effective divisor and
Z
is a 0-dimensional subscheme (Corollary
2.13
). We give various characterizations of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs with
singular
support in Section
2
.

Example 1.2

(Proposition
2.6
)
.

Suppose
(F,s)
is a pair which is
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
- and
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
-stable, i.e.,
F
is pure and
Q
is 0-dimensional.
Then
Q
is a quotient sheaf of the 0-dimensional sheaf
\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X})
, which is supported on the locus where
S
is not Cohen-Macaulay.

Our first main result (Section
3
) is the construction of projective moduli spaces of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
-stable pairs via geometric invariant theory (GIT).

Theorem 1.3

(Theorem
3.10
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety and
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
. Then, for each
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
, there exists a fine moduli space
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
-stable pairs on
X
with
\operatorname{ch}(F)=v
as a projective scheme.
Moreover, there exists a projective scheme
\mathcal{M}
, an action of
\mathrm{PGL}_{N}
on
\mathcal{M}
for some
N
, and an
\mathrm{SL}_{N}
-equivariant ample line bundle
\mathcal{L}^{(q)}\in\mathrm{Pic}^{\mathrm{SL}_{N}}(\mathcal{M})_{\mathbb{Q}}
for each
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
such that

\curly P^{(q)}_{v}(X)=[\mathcal{M}^{st}(\mathcal{L}^{(q)})/\mathrm{PGL}_{N}]=[\mathcal{M}^{ss}(\mathcal{L}^{(q)})/\mathrm{PGL}_{N}].

In particular,
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
is the GIT quotient
\mathcal{M}/\!/_{\mathcal{L}^{(q)}}\mathrm{SL}_{N}
.

Theorem
1.3
shows that
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair moduli spaces are related by GIT wall-crossing.
Since the underlying sheaves of
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs are not
pure
, we needed finer arguments than the standard ones for pure sheaves.
This approach is inspired by an analogous result of Stoppa-Thomas on 1-dimensional pairs
[
ST
]
.

Following
[
PT1
]
, we show that the moduli space of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs consists of open components of the moduli space of perfect complexes. Denote by
{\curly Perf}(X)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}^{\mathrm{spl}}
the moduli space of simple perfect complexes on
X
with trivial determinant.

Theorem 1.4

(Theorem
3.19
, Proposition
3.28
)
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety of dimension
n\geq 4
,
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
, and
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
.
The canonical map

(1)

\curly P^{(q)}_{v}(X)\to{\curly Perf}(X)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}^{\mathrm{spl}},\quad(F,s)\mapsto I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s}F]

is an open embedding.
Moreover, there is a polynomial Bridgeland stability condition
\rho^{(q)}
such that the image of (
1
) is the locus of
\rho^{(q)}
-stable objects.

For
q=1
, Theorem
1.4
was also shown by Gholampour-Jiang-Lo
[
GJL
]
.
The connection to Bayer’s polynomial Bridgeland stability
[
Bay
]
is inspired by
[
GJL
]
.

1.4.
Reduced virtual cycles

Let
X
be a
Calabi-Yau
4
-fold
, i.e., a smooth projective variety of dimension
4
with trivial canonical bundle. In particular,
X
could be a hyperkähler
4
-fold or an abelian
4
-fold.
Fix a trivialization
\omega:\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{\cong}\Omega^{4}_{X}
.

Throughout this section, let
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
and let
\curly P:={\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
be the moduli space of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs
(F,s)
on
X
with Chern character

\operatorname{ch}(F)=v=(0,0,\gamma,\beta,n-\gamma\cdot\mathrm{td}_{2}(X))\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q}).

By Theorem
1.4
, we have an Oh-Thomas virtual cycle
[
OT1
]

\left[\curly P\right]^{\mathrm{vir}}\in A_{\mathrm{vd}}\left(\curly P\right),\qquad\mathrm{vd}=n-\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{2},

which maps to Borisov-Joyce’s virtual cycle
[
BJ
]
under the cycle class map by
[
OT2
]
.
2
2

2

Here
A_{*}(-)
denotes the Chow group with rational coefficients. We also use the convention that
A_{i}(-)=0
for
i\in\mathbb{Q}\setminus\mathbb{Z}
.

The Oh-Thomas virtual cycle is constructed from the
canonical 3-term
symmetric obstruction theory

\phi:\mathbb{E}:=R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})_{0}[3]\to\mathbb{L}_{\curly P},\qquad\mathsf{SD}:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[2]\cong\mathbb{E},

of Huybrechts-Thomas
[
HT
]
and
an
orientation

o:\mathcal{O}_{\curly P}\xrightarrow{\cong}\det(\mathbb{E})

of Cao-Gross-Joyce
[
CGJ
]
.
Then the intrinsic normal cone is
isotropic
by the derived Darboux theorem of Brav-Bussi-Joyce
[
BBJ
]
, which is necessary to define the virtual cycle. We refer to Section
4
for the precise statements.

For a generic surface class
\gamma
, the Oh-Thomas virtual cycle
[\curly P]^{\mathrm{vir}}
vanishes due to the following two heuristics:

(a)

When deforming
X
, the codimension of the Hodge locus of
\gamma
in the base is often positive.
Therefore deformation invariance of virtual cycles implies the vanishing.

(b)

There often exist non-degenerate cosections given by semi-regularity maps.
They give trivial factors
\mathcal{O}
of
\mathbb{E}
which imply the vanishing.

We present a motivating example which reveals the above two phenomena.

Example 1.5
.

Let
X\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{5}
be a smooth sextic hypersurface which contains a plane
S\subseteq X
. Let
v=\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{S})\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
and
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
.

•

The virtual dimension is
-\frac{19}{2}
.

•

The codimension of the Hodge locus of
\gamma=v_{2}=[S]
is
19

[
CGG
]
.

•

There are
19
non-degenerate cosections.

We first discuss the codimension of the Hodge locus in (a).
By Bloch
[
Blo
]
(see Proposition
4.9
), the obstruction to deforming the Hodge class
\gamma\in H^{2}(X,\Omega^{2}_{X})\subseteq H^{4}(X,\mathbb{C})
is governed by the symmetric bilinear form

\mathsf{B}_{\gamma}:H^{1}(X,T_{X})\otimes H^{1}(X,T_{X})\to\mathbb{C},\quad\xi_{1}\otimes\xi_{2}\mapsto\int_{X}\iota_{\xi_{1}}\iota_{\xi_{2}}\gamma\cup\omega\,,

where
\iota
denotes the contraction map. In particular, its rank

\rho_{\gamma}:=\operatorname{rk}(\mathsf{B}_{\gamma})

is the codimension of the Hodge locus of
\gamma
.
3
3

3

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a smooth proper morphism of smooth analytic spaces such that the fibre
\mathcal{X}_{0}
over a point
0\in\mathcal{B}
is
X^{\mathrm{an}}
and
\mathcal{B}
is contractible. Then there exists a unique lift
\widetilde{\gamma}
of
\gamma\in H^{4}(X,\mathbb{Q})
as a section of the local system
R^{4}f_{*}\mathbb{Q}
. The
Hodge locus
of
\gamma
is defined as the subset
\mathrm{Hd}_{\gamma}:=\{b\in\mathcal{B}:\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}\in H^{2,2}(\mathcal{X}_{b})\}
and has a natural analytic scheme structure (see
[
Voi2
]
). If the Kodaira-Spencer map
\mathsf{KS}:T_{\mathcal{B},0}\to H^{1}(X,T_{X})
is an isomorphism, then
\rho_{\gamma}=\dim(T_{\mathcal{B},0})-\dim(T_{\mathrm{Hd}_{\gamma},0})
by
[
Blo
]
.
We will not use the Hodge locus in this paper except for heuristic arguments.

We now discuss the cosections in (b).
By Buchweitz-Flenner
[
BuFl1
]
, a perfect complex
[I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}]\in\curly P
gives rise to a
semi-regularity map

(2)

\mathsf{sr}:\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})_{0}\to H^{3}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})\cong H^{1}(X,T_{X})^{\vee},\quad e\mapsto\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{At}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\circ e),

where
\operatorname{At}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
is the Atiyah class.
This semi-regularity map relates the obstructions of deforming the perfect complex
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
and the Hodge class
\gamma=\operatorname{ch}_{2}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
via the commutative triangle

(3)

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 27.59097pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-27.59097pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 51.59097pt\raise-32.64001pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{H^{1}(X,T_{X})^{\vee}}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

The symmetry of the above triangle given by
\mathsf{ob}=\mathsf{sr}^{\vee}
is a special feature of Calabi-Yau 4-folds
X
and
(2,2)
-classes
\gamma\in H^{2}(X,\Omega^{2}_{X})
.
The semi-regularity maps
\mathsf{sr}
can be extended to cosections on the moduli space,

\mathsf{SR}:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[1]=R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})_{0}[2]\to H^{1}(X,T_{X})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\curly P}.

Then
\mathsf{SR}^{2}:=\mathsf{SR}\circ\mathsf{SR}^{\vee}=\mathsf{B}_{\gamma}\otimes 1_{\curly P}
, which can be shown by extending the triangle (
3
). See Section
4
for details.

Choose a non-degenerate subspace
V\subseteq H^{1}(X,T_{X})
with respect to the bilinear form
\mathsf{B}_{\gamma}
. Then
V\cong V^{\vee}
.
Assume that
V
is
maximal
, i.e.,
\dim(V)=\rho_{\gamma}
.

(a’)

From the perspective of Hodge theory,
V
is a transversal slice to the Hodge locus of
\gamma
. Heuristically, we want to constrain virtual cycles to be deformation invariant only on the Hodge locus of
\gamma
.

(b’)

From the perspective of cosections,
V
induces
non-degenerate
cosections

\Sigma:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[1]\xrightarrow{\mathsf{SR}}H^{1}(X,T_{X})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\curly P}\to V^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\curly P}.

Here non-degeneracy means that the square
\Sigma^{2}:=\Sigma\circ\Sigma^{\vee}
is an isomorphism.
In particular, we have a direct sum decomposition

\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}\oplus(V\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\curly P})[1]

and we want to remove the trivial factors from the obstruction theory.

The second main result in this paper achieves (a’) and (b’).

Theorem 1.6

(Theorem
4.5
, Proposition
4.19
)
.

Let
X
be a Calabi-Yau 4-fold,
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
, and
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
. For any maximal non-degenerate subspace
V\subseteq H^{1}(X,T_{X})
with respect to
\mathsf{B}_{\gamma}
, the composition

\phi^{\mathrm{red}}:\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}\xrightarrow{(1,0)}\mathbb{E}\xrightarrow{\phi}\mathbb{L}_{{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)}

is a symmetric obstruction theory such that the reduced closed substack of the intrinsic normal cone is isotropic (see Section
4
for the precise definition).
Consequently, given orientations of
\mathbb{E}
and
H^{1}(X,T_{X})_{\gamma}:=H^{1}(X,T_{X})/\ker(\mathsf{B}_{\gamma})
, there exists a canonical reduced virtual cycle

\left[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)\right]^{\mathrm{red}}\in A_{\mathrm{rvd}}\left({\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)\right),\qquad\mathrm{rvd}=n-\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\rho_{\gamma},

which is independent of the choice of
V
.
4
4

4

The subspace
V
depends on a choice, but
H^{1}(X,T_{X})_{\gamma}
is canonically defined. These spaces are identified via the map
V\subset H^{1}(X,T_{X})\to H^{1}(X,T_{X})_{\gamma}
.

We prove that
\phi^{\mathrm{red}}
is an obstruction theory via the
algebraic twistor family

[
KT1
]
associated to the transversal slice
V
and prove the isotropic condition via a variation of the
cosection localization
in
[
KL
,
KiPa2
]
for the non-degenerate cosections
\Sigma
. In contrast to the
isotropic
cosections in
[
KiPa2
]
, the
non-degenerate
cosections considered in this paper allow us to handle cases where the virtual dimension
n-\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{2}
is not an integer, but the reduced virtual dimension
n-\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\rho_{\gamma}
is an integer, which actually occurs quite often (e.g. Example
1.5
).

We note that the algebraic twistor method alone does not produce a reduced virtual cycle
since the isotropic condition for
\phi
does not imply it for
\phi^{\mathrm{red}}
.
The cosection localization method alone produces the reduced virtual cycle as a short-cut, but does not give a reduced obstruction theory.
Combining the two methods enables us to directly apply all the known tools for handling Oh-Thomas virtual cycles (torus localization
[
OT1
]
, cosection localization
[
KiPa2
]
, virtual pullback
[
Par1
]
) to the reduced virtual cycle.
We will indeed apply these tools in the sequels to this paper.

We observe that the reduced virtual cycle is closely related to semi-regularity.
In
[
BuFl1
]
, a perfect complex
[I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}]\in\curly P
is called
semi-regular
if the semi-regularity map
\mathsf{sr}
in (
2
) is injective.

Theorem 1.7

(Theorem
4.6
)
.

Let
X
be a Calabi-Yau 4-fold,
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
, and
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
.
For any
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair
[(F,s)]\in{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
, the associated complex
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
is semi-regular if and only if the moduli space
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
is smooth of dimension
\mathrm{rvd}=n-\frac{1}{2}\gamma^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\rho_{\gamma}
at
[I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}]
.

As a direct corollary of Theorem
1.7
, if all
[(F,s)]\in{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
are semi-regular, the reduced virtual cycle equals the fundamental cycle (for a certain choice of orientations).
Thus, heuristically, we view the semi-regular case as the
ideal situation
. Put differently, we view the reduced virtual cycle as the correct
virtual generalization
of the fundamental cycle in the semi-regular case.

For Calabi-Yau 4-folds, Theorem
1.7
generalizes the results of Bloch
[
Blo
]
and Buchweitz-Flenner
[
BuFl1
]
. Indeed, it is shown in loc. cit. that semi-regularity implies smoothness, whereas Theorem
1.7
also provides a formula for the dimension and a converse.

Example 1.8
.

We revisit Example
1.5
.
All planes
S\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
in a smooth sextic
4
-fold
X
are semi-regular and the moduli space
{\curly P}^{(-1)}_{v}(X)\cong{\curly P}^{(0)}_{v}(X)\cong{\curly P}^{(1)}_{v}(X)
, with
v=\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
, consists of isolated reduced points (Corollary
4.28
).
Hence the reduced virtual dimension is zero and the contribution of any plane
S\subset X
to the reduced virtual cycle is
\pm[\{S\}]
.

More generally, we give a simple description of the semi-regularity map for local complete intersection surfaces. This identification is important for computing
\rho_{\gamma}
and the reduced virtual dimension.
Determining
\rho_{\gamma}
is an interesting new aspect of the surface counting theory. For example,
\rho_{\gamma}
is only sub-additive in
\gamma
, e.g.,
\rho_{d\gamma}=\rho_{\gamma}
for any
d\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

Proposition 1.9

(Proposition
4.24
)
.

Let
i:S\hookrightarrow X
be a local complete intersection surface inside a Calabi-Yau 4-fold
X
. Then there exists an isomorphism

\theta_{S}:H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(I_{S/X},I_{S/X})_{0}

preserving non-degenerate symmetric bilinear forms induced by Serre duality. Under this isomorphism, the sheaf theoretic obstruction and semi-regularity maps (
3
) match with the ones from the deformation theory of subschemes.

We identify the dual of the semi-regularity map (
2
) with the obstruction map
\operatorname{ob}_{S/X}:H^{1}(X,T_{X})\to H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})
for Hilbert schemes, which is easier to determine. This leads to the following corollary:

Corollary 1.10

(Corollary
4.28
,
4.30
)
.

(1)

Let
X\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}
be a smooth complete intersection Calabi-Yau 4-fold. Suppose
S\subset X
is a complete intersection surface in
\mathbb{P}^{N}
. Then
I_{S/X}
is semi-regular. Moreover if
v=\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
and
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
, then
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
is smooth of dimension
\mathrm{rvd}=h^{0}(N_{S/X})
at
I_{S/X}
.

(2)

Let
X
be a Calabi-Yau 4-fold with non-degenerate holomorphic
2
-form. Suppose
i:S\hookrightarrow X
is a smooth lagrangian surface and
v=\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
. Then
\mathrm{rvd}=h^{1,0}(S)-\frac{1}{2}\dim\operatorname{coker}(i^{*})
where
i^{*}:H^{1}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})\to H^{1}(S,\Omega_{S}^{1})
.

For Corollary
1.10
(1) and
X
a sextic 4-fold, we show that
\mathrm{rvd}
is often zero, but can be positive in some cases.
For Corollary
1.10
(2), we find that lagrangian planes are semi-regular with
\mathrm{rvd}=0
, but we also consider Schoen surfaces
[
Sch
]
embedded in their Albanese 4-fold which are semi-regular with
\mathrm{rvd}=4
.
Furthermore, for Corollary
1.10
(2), we also consider non-semi-regular cases on hyperkähler 4-folds with lagrangian fibration and the Hilbert square
K3^{[2]}
.
Finally, we show that
\rho_{\gamma}
vanishes when
h^{2,0}(X)=0
and the class
\gamma
is a product of two
(1,1)
-classes. In this case, non-reduced virtual cycles contain non-trivial information.
See Section
4.6
for a precise list of examples.

1.5.
Deformation invariance

Let

f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}

be a
family of Calabi-Yau 4-folds
, i.e., a smooth projective morphism of relative dimension
4
to a smooth connected affine scheme
\mathcal{B}
such that
\omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
and with connected fibres. Consider a horizontal section

\widetilde{v}\in\bigoplus_{p=2}^{4}F^{p}H^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}),

where
F^{p}H^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
is the
p
th filtered piece of the Hodge bundle
H^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
.
Then
\widetilde{v}_{b}\in H^{*}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathbb{C})
is a locally constant family of cohomology classes on the fibres
\mathcal{X}_{b}:=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\{b\}
over
b\in\mathcal{B}
.
The component
\widetilde{v}_{2}
induces a section
\widetilde{\gamma}
of
R^{2}f_{*}\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}
.
The bilinear forms
\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}
on
H^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{b},T_{\mathcal{X}_{b}})
for
b\in\mathcal{B}
extend to a bilinear form
\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
on the vector bundle
R^{1}f_{*}(T_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})
.

Let
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
be the moduli space of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
-stable pairs on the fibres of
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
. This relative moduli space fits into the fibre diagram

\textstyle{{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}_{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{b\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{b}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}}

for each
b\in\mathcal{B}
. We have a relative obstruction theory
\phi_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}:\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\to\mathbb{L}_{{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})/\mathcal{B}}
.

Our third main result (Section
5
) is the deformation invariance of the reduced virtual cycle in Theorem
1.6
.

Theorem 1.11

(Theorem
5.1
)
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a family of Calabi-Yau 4-folds.
Let
\widetilde{v}\in\bigoplus_{p}F^{p}H^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
be a horizontal section,
\widetilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{B},R^{2}f_{*}\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})
the induced section, and
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
.
Assume that the function
b\in\mathcal{B}\mapsto\rho_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}
is constant. Given orientations

\mathcal{O}_{{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})}\cong\det(\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}),\qquad\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}\cong\det(R^{1}f_{*}(T_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})/\ker(\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}})),

there exists a cycle class

\left[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\right]^{\mathrm{red}}\in A_{\mathrm{rvd}+\dim(\mathcal{B})}\left({\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\right)

such that, for all
b\in\mathcal{B}
, we have

\left[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}_{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})\right]^{\mathrm{red}}=i_{b}^{!}\left[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\right]^{\mathrm{red}}\in A_{\mathrm{rvd}}\left({\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}_{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})\right)

for the induced orientation.
Here
i_{b}^{!}
denotes the refined Gysin pullback.

In general, reduced virtual cycles given by cosection localization
[
KL
]
are deformation invariant only under an additional assumption. In Theorem
1.11
, this assumption follows from the fact that the Hodge locus of
\widetilde{\gamma}
in
\mathcal{B}
is
\mathcal{B}
itself.

The existence of a relative orientation is required in Theorem
1.11
. We conjecture (Conjecture
5.7
) that a relative orientation exists after a finite étale base change of the base
\mathcal{B}
and we prove its topological counterpart (Corollary
C.2
). The existence of a relative orientation is crucial for deformation invariance of numerical invariants, but is
not
needed in the main application below.

Variational Hodge Conjecture

The Hodge conjecture predicts that for any smooth projective variety
X
, all rational
(p,p)
-classes on
X
are algebraic.
In
[
Gro1
]
Grothendieck introduced a variant of the Hodge conjecture.

Conjecture 1.12

(variational Hodge conjecture)
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety and
\gamma
an algebraic
(p,p)
-class on
X
. For any smooth projective morphism
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
to a smooth connected scheme
\mathcal{B}
and a horizontal section
\widetilde{v}_{p}
of
F^{p}\mathcal{H}^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})

such that
\mathcal{X}_{0}\cong X
and
(\widetilde{v}_{p})_{0}=\gamma
for some closed point
0\in\mathcal{B}
, the cohomology classes
(\widetilde{v}_{p})_{b}
are algebraic for all closed points
b\in\mathcal{B}
.

Our fourth main result is an affirmative answer to Conjecture
1.12
when there exists a non-zero reduced virtual cycle.

Theorem 1.13

(Theorem
5.13
)
.

Let
X
be a Calabi-Yau 4-fold and let
\gamma
be a
(2,2)
-class on
X
.
If for some
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
with
v_{2}=\gamma
,
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
, and choice of orientation on
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
we have

[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)]^{\mathrm{red}}\neq 0\in A_{\mathrm{rvd}}({\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X))

then Conjecture
1.12
holds for
X
and
\gamma
.

We prove Theorem
1.13
via the deformation invariance in Theorem
1.11
. Here we do not require the two technical assumptions in Theorem
1.11
. The function
b\in\mathcal{B}\mapsto\rho_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}\in\mathbb{Z}
is lower semi-continuous and the reduced virtual cycle vanishes if
\rho_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}
drops at
0\in\mathcal{B}
(Proposition
5.5
).
The assumption on orientations can be removed by working with virtual
cycles
instead of
invariants
.

We view Theorem
1.13
as a
virtual
generalization of a result of Buchweitz-Flenner
[
BuFl1
]
(see also
[
Blo
]
) for Calabi-Yau 4-folds. In fact,
[
BuFl1
]
implies that Conjecture
1.12
holds if there exists a semi-regular
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F]
on
X
with
\operatorname{ch}_{2}(F)=\gamma
.
Theorem
1.13
recovers the result of
[
BuFl1
]
for Calabi-Yau 4-folds, because the reduced virtual cycle equals the fundamental cycle near a semi-regular point (Theorem
4.6
).

This application to the variational Hodge conjecture shows that it can be useful to work with the virtual cycle instead of invariants, because one can restrict to each connected component of the moduli space.

Example 1.14

(Example
5.16
)
.

We give a non-semi-regular application of Theorem
1.13
. Let
X=S_{1}\times S_{2}
be a product of two K3 surfaces. Let
\beta_{1}\in H^{1,1}(S_{1})
,
\beta_{2}\in H^{1,1}(S_{2})
be two irreducible effective classes with
\beta_{1}^{2}\geq 2
and
\beta_{2}^{2}=0
.
Let
\gamma=\beta_{1}\cup\beta_{2}
.
Then, for any
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
, we have
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)=|\mathcal{O}_{S_{1}}(\beta_{1})|\times\mathbb{P}^{1}
for some
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
with
v_{2}=\gamma
. In this case, the reduced virtual cycle is
\pm h^{0}(\mathcal{O}_{S_{1}}(\beta_{1}))\cdot[\mathrm{pt}\times\mathbb{P}^{1}]
. Therefore Conjecture
1.12
holds for such
\gamma
.
This is a non-semi-regular case since the reduced virtual cycle differs from the fundamental cycle.

1.6.
Moduli stack of semi-stable sheaves

In Appendix
B
, we generalize the construction of reduced virtual cycles to
Artin
stacks (which are global quotient stacks). In particular, we consider the moduli stack
\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)
of Gieseker semi-stable sheaves on
X
(with respect to a polarization
H
) with Chern character
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
, rigidified by the action of
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
The Chern character
v
is arbitrary so this includes moduli of
2
-dimensional
torsion
sheaves and moduli of
higher rank
sheaves.

Theorem 1.15

(Theorem
B.6
)
.

Let
(X,H)
be a polarized Calabi-Yau 4-fold and
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
.
For any choice of orientation on
\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)
, there exists a canonical reduced virtual cycle

[\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)]^{\mathrm{red}}\in A_{1-\frac{1}{2}\chi(v,v)+\frac{1}{2}\rho_{\gamma}}(\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X))

where
\gamma:=v_{2}
and
\chi(v,v):=\int_{X}v^{\vee}\cdot v\cdot\mathrm{td}(X)
.
Moreover, if
[\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)]^{\mathrm{red}}\neq 0
, then Conjecture
1.12
holds for
X
and
\gamma
.

We construct the reduced virtual cycle as a class in Kresch’s Chow group
[
Kre1
]
by extending Oh-Thomas’s construction
[
OT1
]
and Kiem-Li’s cosection localization
[
KL
]
to Artin stacks (which are global quotients).
If there exist strictly semi-stable sheaves, this virtual cycle does not provide numerical invariants since there is no push-forward map for
\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)\to\operatorname{Spec}\,(\mathbb{C})
. Nonetheless, this virtual cycle is sufficient for applications to the variational Hodge conjecture.

There is a “fixed determinant version” of Theorem
1.15
under the assumption
v_{0}\neq 0
and
v_{1}=0
(Theorem
B.10
).

1.7.
Derived algebraic geometry

In Appendix
A
, we revisit Theorem
1.6
via derived algebraic geometry.
The main result is the following:

Theorem 1.16

(Corollary
A.15
)
.

In the situation of Theorem
1.6
, there exists a
(-2)
-shifted symplectic derived enhancement of
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
inducing the reduced symmetric obstruction theory
\phi^{\mathrm{red}}
.

We first introduce general reduction methods via
(-1)
-shifted
closed
/
exact
1-forms (Theorem
A.1
).
In order to apply this to the moduli space
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
, we show that the semi-regularity maps can be enhanced to
(-1)
-shifted closed
1
-forms (Proposition
A.13
).
This is achieved by generalizing the integration map of
[
PTVV
]
(Theorem
A.6
).

As a byproduct, we obtain a new proof of Bloch’s conjecture for surfaces on Calabi-Yau
4
-folds (Remark
A.18
). The general case of Bloch’s conjecture was shown by Pridham
[
Pri
]
and Bandiera-Lepri-Manetti
[
BLM
]
(and by Iacono-Manetti
[
IM
]
when the normal bundle is extendable).

1.8.
Relations to physics

Finding connections between surface counting on Calabi-Yau 4-folds and string theory is an interesting direction. We briefly point out relations to two (disjoint) parts of the physics literature:

•

In M-/F-theory, the complex moduli of a Calabi-Yau 4-fold
X
are fixed by fixing a rational 4-cycle class called the
G_{4}
-flux.
Then Gukov-Vafa-Witten
[
GVW
]
introduced an associated superpotential giving rise to a symmetric bilinear form which (up to change of coordinates) coincides with
\mathsf{B}_{\gamma}
. Explicit bounds on the rank
\rho_{\gamma}
of
\mathsf{B}_{\gamma}
appear to be of interest to physicists
[
BCV
,
BV
,
BBGL
,
GGHHP
,
LVWX
]
.
5
5

5

We thank Grimm for pointing us to the physics literature, specifically
[
BCV
]
.

•

In the series of papers starting from
[
Nek1
]
, Nekrasov studied the moduli space of (non-commutative) instantons on origami spacetimes in
\mathbb{C}^{4}
. Our choice of
K
-theoretic insertions for toric Calabi-Yau 4-folds in the sequels (and the choice in
[
CKM
,
KR
]
) are motivated by Nekrasov-Piazzalunga’s study of SUSY Yang-Mills theory on
\mathbb{C}^{4}

[
Nek2
,
NP
]
.

1.9.
Sequels

We briefly mention the content of the sequels to this paper:

•

In Part II we study the
\mathrm{DT}/\mathrm{PT}_{0}
correspondence. We conjecture a general correspondence and provide evidence on toric Calabi-Yau 4-folds via a vertex formalism, and on compact Calabi-Yau 4-folds via a virtual Lefschetz principle.

•

In Part III we study the
\mathrm{PT}_{0}/\mathrm{PT}_{1}
correspondence in special cases. We consider virtual projective bundles and Weierstrass 4-folds.

•

In Part IV we compute
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
invariants in some examples including local surfaces and Calabi-Yau 4-folds with a nondegenerate holomorphic 2-form.

We will also further explore the link between the variational Hodge conjecture and the reduced virtual cycles.

1.10.
Related work

Reduced virtual cycles for
curves
on hyperkähler
4
-folds were studied by Cao-Oberdieck-Toda in
[
COT1
,
COT2
]
.
For the moduli space of
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
-pairs on
X
, Theorem
1.4
was independently proven by Gholampour-Jiang-Lo
[
GJL
]
.
Correspondences for
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
invariants in the context of Joyce’s wall-crossing framework
[
GJT
,
Joy1
,
Joy2
]
will be an interesting direction to study.

There are two types of generalizations of cosection localization
[
KL
]
to Donaldson-Thomas theory of Calabi-Yau
4
-folds
[
BJ
,
OT1
]
. In
[
Sav
]
, Savvas showed vanishing of Borisov-Joyce virtual cycles with a nowhere vanishing isotropic cosection.
In
[
KiPa2
]
, Kiem and the third-named author studied cosection localization for Oh-Thomas virtual cycles
[
OT1
]
.

1.11.
Notation and conventions

We use the following notation and conventions throughout the paper.

•

All schemes and algebraic stacks are assumed to be of finite type over the field
\mathbb{C}
, unless stated otherwise.
A
variety
is a separated integral scheme.
A
point

x\in X
in a scheme or an algebraic stack
X
means a
\mathbb{C}
-valued point.

•

A
Calabi-Yau
n
-fold
is a smooth projective variety
X
of dimension
n
with
K_{X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
.

•

For a coherent sheaf
F
on a smooth quasi-projective variety
X
, we use the following notation for the various duals:

–

F^{\vee}:=R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(F,\mathcal{O}_{X})
;

–

F^{D}:=\curly Ext^{c}_{X}(F,K_{X})
, where
c
is the codimension of
F
in
X
.

•

For a smooth projective variety
X
, we denote by

–

\mathrm{Coh}(X)
the abelian category of coherent sheaves on
X
;

–

{\curly Coh}(X)
the moduli stack of coherent sheaves on
X
;

–

D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X)
the bounded derived category of coherent sheaves on
X
.

•

For a perfect complex
\mathbb{F}
on a scheme
\mathscr{X}
of tor-amplitude
(-\infty,0]
, denote by
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{F}):=h^{1}/h^{0}(\mathbb{F}^{\vee})
the associated abelian cone stack
[
BF
, Prop. 2.4]
.

•

An
orthogonal bundle
on a scheme
\mathscr{X}
is a pair of a vector bundle
E
and an isomorphism
q:E\to E^{\vee}
such that
q=q^{\vee}
. By abuse of notation, we also denote by
q:E\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathscr{X}}
the corresponding quadratic function. An
orientation
of an orthogonal bundle
E
is an isomorphism
o:\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}\cong\det(E)
such that
\det(q)=(-1)^{\frac{r(r-1)}{2}}(o\circ o^{\vee})^{-1}
where
r=\mathrm{rank}(E)
.

•

A
symmetric complex
on a scheme
\mathscr{X}
is a pair of a perfect complex
\mathbb{E}
on
\mathscr{X}
of tor-amplitude
[-2,0]
and an isomorphism
\theta:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[2]\cong\mathbb{E}
such that
\theta^{\vee}[2]=\theta
. An
orientation
of a symmetric complex
\mathbb{E}
is an isomorphism
o:\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}\cong\det(\mathbb{E})
such that
\det(\theta)=(-1)^{\frac{r(r-1)}{2}}o\circ o^{\vee}
where
r=\mathrm{rank}(\mathbb{E})
.
6
6

6

This convention is slightly different from
[
Par1
]
. In loc. cit., all symmetric complexes are assumed to be equipped with an orientation.

•

For a morphism
f:\mathscr{X}\to\mathscr{Y}
of algebraic stacks, we denote by
\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{X}/\mathscr{Y}}
the full cotangent complex
[
Ill
]
(
[
Ols
]
) and by
\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{X}/\mathscr{Y}}
the truncated cotangent complex.

•

For any algebraic stack
\mathscr{X}
, we denote by
A_{*}(\mathscr{X})
the Chow group of Kresch
[
Kre1
]
with
\mathbb{Q}
-coefficients. For any
d\in\mathbb{Q}\setminus\mathbb{Z}
, we let
A_{d}(\mathcal{X}):=0
.

•

For a morphism
f:\mathscr{X}\to\mathscr{Y}
and a coherent sheaf (resp. a perfect complex)
E
on
\mathscr{Y}
, we denote the pullback
f^{*}E
(resp. the derived pullback
Lf^{*}E
) by
E|_{\mathscr{X}}
.

2.
Stable pairs

In this section, we introduce the
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
-stability conditions on 2-dimensional pairs for
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
, and present basic properties which will be used throughout the paper and its sequels. The
\mathrm{PT}_{-1}
-stable pairs coincide with
\mathrm{DT}
-stable pairs (i.e. ideal sheaves),
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
-stable pairs are
\mathrm{PT}
-stable pairs (i.e. Le Potier stable pairs
[
Pot1
,
Pot2
,
PT1
]
), and
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
-stable pairs is a new intermediate notion

\mathrm{DT}:=\mathrm{PT}_{-1}\leadsto\mathrm{PT}_{0}\leadsto\mathrm{PT}_{1}=:\mathrm{PT}.

Any
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair
(F,s)
on a smooth variety
X
has an associated complex

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}:=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s}F]

in
D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X)
— the bounded derived category of coherent sheaves on
X
.
We study
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs through these associated complexes. We provide descriptions of these associated complexes under some various assumptions on the singularities of the support of
F
.
The results in this section provide some insights into the geometric meaning of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
-stability. They will also play an important role in the sequels to this paper.

Throughout this section, we fix a smooth projective variety
X
of dimension
n
over the complex field
\mathbb{C}
.

2.1.
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
-stability

For any integer
q\geq-1
, consider the two full subcategories

\displaystyle\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq q}(X)

\displaystyle:=\{F\in\mathrm{Coh}(X):\dim(F)\leq q\},

\displaystyle\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq q+1}(X)

\displaystyle:=\{F\in\mathrm{Coh}(X):T_{q}(F)=0\}.

Definition 2.1
.

Let
F
be a coherent sheaf on
X
with 2-dimensional support and let
s:\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F
be a section.
For
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
, we say that the pair
(F,s)
is
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
-stable
if

F\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq q+1}(X)Q:=\operatorname{coker}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s}F)\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq q}(X).

Abbreviating, we also refer to
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
-stable pairs as
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs
.

Example 2.2
.

The
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
-stability conditions for the following two extreme cases are well-known.

DT)

A pair
(F,s)
is
\mathrm{PT}_{-1}
-stable if and only if
s:\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F
is surjective. Hence
\mathrm{PT}_{-1}
pairs on
X
correspond to 2-dimensional closed subschemes of
X
(which may have irreducible components of dimension 0 and 1). Thus we refer to
\mathrm{PT}_{-1}
-stability as
\mathrm{DT}
-stability.

PT)

A pair
(F,s)
is
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
-stable if and only if
F
is pure and
\dim(Q)\leq 1
. Hence
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pairs are exactly the 2-dimensional stable pairs in the sense of Le Potier
[
Pot1
, Def. 4.2]
.
7
7

7

Equivalently,
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pairs are the 2-dimensional
\alpha
-stable pairs in the sense of
[
Pot2
, Def. 4.4]
for sufficiently big
\alpha(t)\in\mathbb{Q}[t]
.
Their 1-dimensional analogs
are used in
[
PT1
,
CMT2
]
(and many other places). Thus we sometimes refer to
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
-stability as
\mathrm{PT}
-stability.

We present some elementary properties of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs. We begin with an observation on the supports.

Lemma 2.3
.

Given a
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair
(F,s)
on
X
, the scheme theoretic support of the coherent sheaf
F
is the scheme theoretic support of the section
s
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to
[
PT1
, Lem. 1.6]
.
Let
\mathrm{Ann}(s)\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{X}
be the ideal sheaf of the support of
s
. Away from the support of
Q=\operatorname{coker}(s)
, the sheaf
F
is generated by the section
s
. Hence
\mathrm{Ann}(s)\cdot F\subseteq F
is set-theoretically supported in the support of
Q
, and thus
\mathrm{Ann}(s)\cdot F\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq q}(X)
. Since
F\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq q+1}(X)
, we obtain
\mathrm{Ann}(s)\cdot F=0
.
∎

We note that the
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs have limit descriptions.

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
W\subseteq Z\subseteq X
be closed subschemes such that

\mathcal{O}_{Z}\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq q+1}(X)
and
\mathcal{O}_{W}\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq q}(X)
. Suppose
W
is reduced
and denote its ideal by
\mathfrak{I}\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{Z}
.
Then
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs
(F,s)
with support
Z
and cokernel
Q
satisfying
\mathrm{Supp}(Q)^{\mathrm{red}}\subseteq W
are equivalent to coherent subsheaves of
\varinjlim\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathfrak{I}^{r},\mathcal{O}_{Z})/\mathcal{O}_{Z}
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to
[
PT1
, Prop. 1.8]
. Choose a
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair
(F,s)
. By Lemma
2.3
, we can form a short exact sequence

(4)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where
Z
is the support of
F
and
Q
is the cokernel of
s
. Since
\mathcal{O}_{Z}\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq q+1}(X)
and
Q\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq q}(X)
, we have
\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(Q,\mathcal{O}_{Z})=0
. Hence the short exact sequence (
4
) gives us an inclusion

\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(F,\mathcal{O}_{Z})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{Z},\mathcal{O}_{Z})=\mathcal{O}_{Z},

which is an isomorphism away from the support of
Q
. Hence
\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(F,\mathcal{O}_{Z})
is an ideal such that
\mathfrak{I}^{r}\subseteq\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(F,\mathcal{O}_{Z})
for some integer
r
. Since
F\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq q+1}(X)
, we have

F\subseteq\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(F,\mathcal{O}_{Z}),\mathcal{O}_{Z})\subseteq\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathfrak{I}^{r},\mathcal{O}_{Z})\subseteq\varinjlim_{r\to\infty}\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathfrak{I}^{r},\mathcal{O}_{Z}),

where the last inclusion map follows from
\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\frac{\mathfrak{I}^{r}}{\mathfrak{I}^{r+1}},\mathcal{O}_{Z})=0
.

On the other hand, choose a coherent subsheaf
F\subseteq\varinjlim\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathfrak{I}^{r},\mathcal{O}_{Z})
containing
\mathcal{O}_{Z}
. Since
F
is coherent, we have
F\subseteq\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathfrak{I}^{r},\mathcal{O}_{Z})
for some sufficiently large
r
. Let
s:\mathcal{O}_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{Z}\hookrightarrow F
be the composition. Consider a subsheaf
G\subseteq F
of dimension
\leq q
. By adjunction, the inclusion map
G\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathfrak{I}^{r},\mathcal{O}_{Z})
corresponds to a map
G\otimes\mathfrak{I}^{r}\to\mathcal{O}_{Z}
, which is zero since
\mathcal{O}_{Z}\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq q+1}(X)
. Hence
G=0
. This means that
F\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq q+1}(X)
. Therefore
(F,s)
is a
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
-stable pair.
∎

Remark 2.5
.

Some results in this paper hold for
d
-dimensional pairs. For a coherent sheaf
F
on
X
with a
d
-dimensional support, a section
s:\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F
, and any
q\in\{-1,0,\ldots,d-1\}
, we define the pair
(F,s)
to be
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
-stable
if

F\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq q+1}(X)Q:=\operatorname{coker}(\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s}F)\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq q}(X).

Then Lemma
2.3
and Proposition
2.4
hold for
d
-dimensional
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs as well (as is immediately obvious from the proofs). However, most parts of the paper require
d=2
, so we restrict to this case.

2.2.
Special cases

Given a
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair
(F,s)
on an
n
-dimensional smooth projective variety
X
, we may consider the following two special cases:

(1)

(F,s)
is additionally
\mathrm{DT}
-stable, i.e.
s:\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F
is surjective.

(2)

(F,s)
is additionally
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
-stable, i.e.
F
is pure.

The first case (1) is very clear. It consists of 2-dimensional subschemes
Z\subset X
with no (possibly embedded)
0
-dimensional components. The second case (2) is more interesting. This case depends on the singularities of the support.

Proposition 2.6
.

Let
S
be a
pure
2-dimensional subscheme of
X
. Then the following three conditions are equivalent:

\mathrm{(i)}

S
is not Cohen-Macaulay.

\mathrm{(ii)}

\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X})\neq 0
.

\mathrm{(iii)}

There exists a
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair
(F,s)
with support
S
and non-zero cokernel
Q
.

Furthermore, for any
m\geq 0
, the
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs
(F,s)
with support
S
and
\chi(Q)=m
are in bijective correspondence with the closed points of the Quot scheme

{\curly Quot}_{X}(\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X}),m)

parametrizing length
m
quotients of the 0-dimensional sheaf
\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X})
.

Proof.

Since
S
is pure,
\curly Ext^{i}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X})=0
for
i\neq n-2,n-1
and
\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X})
is 0-dimensional
[
HL
, Prop. 1.1.6, 1.1.10]
. Let
i:S\hookrightarrow X
denote the inclusion map. Since
R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X})=Ri_{*}i^{!}K_{X}
, we have
\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X})=0
if and only if
S
is Cohen-Macaulay.

For any 0-dimensional sheaf
Q
, we have

(5)

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(Q,\mathcal{O}_{S})\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X}),Q^{D})

where
Q^{D}:=\curly Ext^{n}_{X}(Q,K_{X})
. Hence (iii) implies (ii). On the other hand, if
\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X})\neq 0
, we can always find a closed point
P\in X
in the support of
\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X})
and a non-zero map
\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X})\to\mathcal{O}_{P}
. By (
5
), we find a non-split extension in
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{P}^{D},\mathcal{O}_{S})\neq 0
from which we obtain a
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair
(F,s)
with non-zero cokernel. By Lemma
2.3
, the support of this
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair is
S
. This proves (ii)
\implies
(iii).

Finally, it follows from (
5
) that there exists a bijection between the collections

\Big(\bigsqcup_{Q}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(Q,\mathcal{O}_{S})\Big)/\cong,\quad\quad\Big(\bigsqcup_{Q}\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},K_{X}),Q)\Big)/\cong

where the union is over all 0-dimensional sheaves on
X
and we divide out by isomorphisms. This bijection is obtained by dualizing the short exact sequence
0\to\mathcal{O}_{S}\to F\to Q\to 0
. Since
F
is pure if and only if
\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(F,K_{X})
is 0-dimensional and
\curly Ext^{n}_{X}(F,K_{X})=0

[
HL
, Prop. 1.1.10]
, it follows that the short exact sequences with
F

pure
correspond precisely to the
surjective
morphisms. The result follows.
∎

Now consider a
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pair
(F,s)
. We can similarly consider the following two special cases:

(3)

(F,s)
is additionally
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
-stable, i.e.
Q
is 0-dimensional.

(4)

F
is additionally reflexive.

Case (3) is exactly case (2) above. Case (4) is a “dual” version of case (1) above, in the sense of the following proposition:

Proposition 2.7
.

Let
(F,s)
be a pair of a coherent sheaf
F
on
X
and a section
s:\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F
.

(1)

If
(F,s)
is
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
-stable and
\chi(F)
is minimal among all
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs with Chern characters
\operatorname{ch}_{n-2}(F)
and
\operatorname{ch}_{n-1}(F)
, then
(F,s)
is
\mathrm{DT}
-stable.

(2)

If
(F,s)
is
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
-stable and
\chi(F)
is maximal among all
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pairs with Chern characters
\operatorname{ch}_{n-2}(F)
and
\operatorname{ch}_{n-1}(F)
, then
F
is reflexive.

In Proposition
2.14
, we will show that
\chi(F)
is bounded below for
\mathrm{DT}
and
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pairs
(F,s)
with fixed Chern characters
\operatorname{ch}_{n-2}(F)
and
\operatorname{ch}_{n-1}(F)
, and bounded above in the
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
case.

Proof of Proposition
2.7
.

(1) Form the short exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where
Z
is the support of
F
and
Q
is the cokernel of
s
. Then we have

\operatorname{ch}_{n-2}(\mathcal{O}_{Z})=\operatorname{ch}_{n-2}(F),\quad\operatorname{ch}_{n-1}(\mathcal{O}_{Z})=\operatorname{ch}_{n-1}(F),\chi(\mathcal{O}_{Z})=\chi(F)-\chi(Q).

Since
(\mathcal{O}_{Z},1:\mathcal{O}_{X}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Z}
) is a
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair, and

\chi(F)
is minimal, we have
Q=0
. Therefore
(F,s)
is a
\mathrm{DT}
pair.

(2) Consider the canonical map

\theta_{F}:F\to F^{DD}.

The purity of
F
implies
\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(F,K_{X})\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq 0}(X)
and
\curly Ext^{n}_{X}(F,K_{X})=0
by
[
HL
, Prop. 1.1.10]
. Moreover, the spectral sequence

\curly Ext^{p}_{X}(\curly Ext^{-q}_{X}(F,K_{X}),K_{X})\Rightarrow F

in
[
HL
, Lem. 1.1.8]
gives us a short exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F^{DD}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\curly Ext_{X}^{n-1}(F,K_{X})^{D}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where
\curly Ext_{X}^{n-1}(F,K_{X})^{D}\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq 0}(X)
. Hence
(F^{DD},\theta_{F}\circ s)
is also a
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pair such that

\chi(F^{DD})=\chi(F)+\chi(\curly Ext^{n-1}_{X}(F,K_{X})^{D})\geq\chi(F).

Since
\chi(F)
is maximal, we have
F=F^{DD}
.
∎

2.3.
Associated complexes

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety of dimension
n
. Given a
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair
(F,s)
on
X
, we can form an associated complex

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}:=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s}F],

with
\mathcal{O}_{X}
in degree zero. We view
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
as an element of
D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X)
— the bounded derived category of coherent sheaves on
X
. In this subsection, we analyze
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs in terms of the associated complex
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
.

We first note that a
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair is equivalent to its associated complex.

Lemma 2.8
.

Assume
X
is a smooth projective variety of dimension
n\geq 4
.
Consider two
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs
(F_{1},s_{1})
and
(F_{2},s_{2})
. Then the two associated complexes
I_{1}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}:=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s_{1}}F_{1}]
and
I_{2}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}:=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s_{2}}F_{2}]
are quasi-isomorphic if and only if the two
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs
(F_{1},s_{1})
and
(F_{2},s_{2})
are isomorphic.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to
[
PT1
, Prop. 1.21]
. Since

\operatorname{Ext}^{\leq 1}_{X}(F_{1},\mathcal{O}_{X})\cong H^{\geq n-1}(X,F_{1}\otimes K_{X})^{*}=0,

by
F_{1}\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq 2}(X)
, the map

\mathbb{C}=\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathcal{O}_{X})\to\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}_{1},\mathcal{O}_{X})

is an isomorphism. Therefore, any quasi-isomorphism
\phi:I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}_{1}\to I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}_{2}
can be completed to an isomorphism of distinguished triangles

\textstyle{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s_{1}}
\scriptstyle{c}
\textstyle{F_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi}
\textstyle{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{s_{2}}
\textstyle{F_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

for some dotted arrows
c
and
\psi
. Since
c\in\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}^{*})=\mathbb{C}^{*}
is a non-zero constant,
c^{-1}\cdot\psi:F_{1}\to F_{2}
is an isomorphism of sheaves with
\psi(s_{1})=s_{2}
.
∎

We show that a
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair, for which the pure part of its scheme theoretic support is Cohen-Macaulay, can be expressed by a map from a
\mathrm{DT}
pair to a
1
-dimensional pure sheaf.

Proposition 2.9
.

For any
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair
(F,s)
on
X
such that
S=\mathrm{Supp}(F)^{\mathrm{pure}}
is Cohen-Macaulay, we have a natural quasi-isomorphism

(6)

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\cong[I_{S/X}\to T_{1}(F)],

where
T_{1}(F)
is zero or pure 1-dimensional and
I_{S/X}
denotes the ideal sheaf of
S
in
X
.

In particular, when
\dim(X)=3
, we have
I_{S/X}=\mathcal{O}_{X}(-S)
and

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\cong[\mathcal{O}_{X}\to T_{1}(F)(S)](-S)

which, for
T_{1}(F)\neq 0
, is a 1-dimensional stable pair on
X
as in
[
PT1
]
.

Proof.

We may assume
T_{1}(F)\neq 0
, because otherwise the result is immediate from Proposition
2.6
. Note that
F/T_{1}(F)
is a pure 2-dimensional sheaf and
T_{1}(F)
is a pure 1-dimensional sheaf. Hence the support of the
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
-stable pair
\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F/T_{1}(F)
is also pure. Let
Z=\mathrm{Supp}(F)
, so
S=Z^{\mathrm{pure}}
. Since
T_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{Z})=I_{S/Z}
by definition,
the support of the
\mathrm{PT}_{0}
pair
\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F/T_{1}(F)
is
S
. By Proposition
2.6
above, we obtain the desired isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{S}\cong F/T_{1}(F)
. Moreover, since the commutative square

\textstyle{I_{S/X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{1}(F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F}

is cartesian and cocartesian, we have the desired quasi-isomorphism (
6
).
∎

Dually,
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pairs (with Cohen-Macaulay support) can be expressed by maps between
1
-dimensional pure sheaves and
\mathrm{DT}
pairs.

Proposition 2.10
.

For any
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pair
(F,s)
on
X
with Cohen-Macaulay support
S
, we have a natural quasi-isomorphism

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\cong\mathrm{cone}(Q[-2]\to I_{S/X}),

where the cokernel
Q
is zero or pure 1-dimensional.

Proof.

The quasi-isomorphism follows from the canonical distinguished triangle

\textstyle{I_{S/X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{I_{S/X}[1].}

Thus it suffices to assume
Q\neq 0
and show it is pure 1-dimensional.
Applying
\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(T_{0}(Q),-)
to the short exact sequence
0\to\mathcal{O}_{S}\to F\to Q\to 0
, we obtain a long exact sequence

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 6.75pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise
