8

8

Proposition
3.17
for the
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
-stability is not a consequence of the results in
[
Pot1
]
, since the parameter space
\mathcal{M}
in this paper is
larger
than the parameter space in
[
Pot1
]
(see Remark
3.12
).

Proof of Proposition
3.17
for
\mathrm{DT}
-stability.

(2) Assume that
(F,\rho)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}
and
(F,s)
is a
\mathrm{DT}
pair. Choose a proper non-zero subspace
V^{\prime}\subseteq V
and let
F^{\prime}=\rho(V^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(-m))\subseteq F
be the induced subsheaf.

We first prove the inequality (
18
). Note that the leading coefficient of the right-hand side of (
18
), as a polynomial of
l
, is negative. On the other hand, the leading coefficient of the left-hand side of (
18
) is always positive. Since the collection
\{F^{\prime}\}
for fixed
X
,
v
,
m
is bounded by
[
Gro2
, Prop. 1.2(i)]
, the set
\{P_{F^{\prime}}(t)\}
is finite. Hence we have (
18
) for sufficiently large
l
.

We then prove the second inequality (
19
) when
s\in V^{\prime}\otimes R_{m}^{*}
. Indeed, we can form the commutative square (
21
) and the commutative diagram (
22
) where the left square is cartesian. Hence we have
s\in\Gamma(X,F^{\prime})
. Since
(F,s)
is a
\mathrm{DT}
pair, the coherent sheaf
F
is generated by the section
s
. Therefore
F^{\prime}=F
. We claim that
V^{\prime}=V
, which then leads to a contradiction. Indeed, since
s\in V^{\prime}\otimes R_{m}^{*}
, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathbb{C}\cong\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma(X,F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V^{\prime}\otimes R_{m}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma(X,F(m))\otimes R_{m}^{*}}

for some dotted arrow. By adjunction, we can form a commutative diagram

\textstyle{R_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma(X,F)\otimes R_{m}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{V^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Gamma(X,F(m)).}

The collection
\{\mathcal{I}_{Z/X}:\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{Z})=v\}
is bounded, so for
m
sufficiently large (with lower bound only depending on
X,v
), we may assume that the map

R_{m}=\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X}(m))\to\Gamma(X,F(m))

is surjective. Therefore,
V^{\prime}\hookrightarrow V=\Gamma(X,F(m))
is also surjective, which proves the claim.

(1) We first claim that

(F,\rho,s)\in\mathcal{M}^{ss}(\mathcal{L}^{(-1)})|_{\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}}\implies\text{$(F,s)$ is a $\mathrm{DT}$ pair}

for sufficiently large
m
and
l
. Indeed, choose
(F,\rho,s)\in\mathcal{M}^{ss}(\mathcal{L}^{(-1)})|_{\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}}
and let
Z
be the support of
s
and
Q
be the cokernel of
s
. Let
V^{\prime}=\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{Z}(m))\subseteq V\cong\Gamma(X,F(m))
and let
F^{\prime}=\rho(V^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(-m))\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{Z}\subseteq F
. Then clearly
s\in V^{\prime}\otimes R_{m}^{*}
. Thus we have the inequality (
19
). Also note

(23)

-\frac{a}{\dim(V^{\prime})}<-\frac{a}{\dim(V)}.

As above, for fixed
X,v,m
the collection of
\{F^{\prime}\}
is bounded, so there are finitely many
\{P_{F^{\prime}}(t)\}
. Taking
l
sufficiently large, (
19
) contradicts (
23
). Hence
V^{\prime}=V
and thus
F^{\prime}=F
. Therefore
\mathcal{O}_{Z}=F
and
(F,s)
is a
\mathrm{DT}
pair.

By the results in (2) and the previous paragraph, we have open embeddings

\left[\mathcal{M}^{ss}|_{\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}}(\mathcal{L}^{(-1)})/\mathrm{PGL}(V)\right]\subseteq{\curly Pair}^{(-1)}(X,v)\subseteq\left[\mathcal{M}^{st}(\mathcal{L}^{(-1)})/\mathrm{PGL}(V)\right]

which induces an open dense embedding

{\curly Pair}^{(-1)}(X,v)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{st}(\mathcal{L}^{(-1)})/\!/\mathrm{PGL}(V)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{ss}(\mathcal{L}^{(-1)})/\!/\mathrm{PGL}(V).

Since
{\curly Pair}^{(-1)}(X,v)
is proper and
\mathcal{M}^{ss}(\mathcal{L}^{(-1)})/\!/\mathrm{PGL}(V)
is separated, the above open embedding is an isomorphism. Hence
\mathcal{M}^{st}(\mathcal{L}^{(-1)})=\mathcal{M}^{ss}(\mathcal{L}^{(-1)})
and
{\curly Pair}^{(-1)}(X,v)=[\mathcal{M}^{st}(\mathcal{L}^{(-1)})/\mathrm{PGL}(V)]
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.17
for
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
-stability.

(2) Assume that
(F,\rho)\in\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}
and
(F,s)
is a
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pair. Choose a proper non-zero subspace
V^{\prime}\subseteq V
and let
F^{\prime}=\rho(V^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(-m))\subseteq F
be the induced sheaf.

We first prove the inequality (
18
). Indeed, since
F
is pure, the subsheaf
F^{\prime}
is also 2-dimensional. Since
\delta<\frac{1}{2}
and
\dim(V^{\prime})<\dim(V)
, we have an inequality

\frac{\alpha_{2}(F^{\prime})}{2\dim(V^{\prime})}>\frac{\delta}{\dim(V)},

between the leading coefficients of the both sides of (
18
), as polynomials of
l
. For fixed
X,v,m
, the collection
\{F^{\prime}\}
is bounded by
[
Gro2
, Prop. 1.2(i)]
, and hence the set
\{P_{F^{\prime}}(t)\}
is finite. Therefore we have (
18
) for sufficiently large
l
.

We then prove the inequality (
19
) when
s\in V^{\prime}\otimes R_{m}^{*}
. Indeed, we can form the commutative square (
21
). We can also form the commutative diagram (
22
) where the left square is cartesian. Hence we have
s\in\Gamma(X,F^{\prime})
.
Since
(F,s)
is a
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pair,
\alpha_{2}(F)=\alpha_{2}(F^{\prime})
. Since
\dim(V^{\prime})<\dim(V)
, we have an inequality

\frac{\delta}{\dim(V^{\prime})}>\frac{\delta}{\dim(V)}

between the leading coefficients of the both sides of (
19
), as polynomials of
l
. Since the collection
\{F^{\prime}\}
is bounded, we have (
19
) for sufficiently large
l
.

(1) We will show that

(24)

(F,\rho,s)\in\mathcal{M}^{ss}(\mathcal{L}^{(1)})|_{\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}}\implies\text{$(F,s)$ is a $\mathrm{PT}_{1}$ pair}

for sufficiently large
m
and
l
. Choose
(F,\rho,s)\in\mathcal{M}^{ss}(\mathcal{L}^{(1)})|_{\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}}
. Let
Q=\operatorname{coker}(s:\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F)
be the cokernel and
\mathcal{O}_{S}=\operatorname{im}(s:\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F)
the image.

We first claim that
Q\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq 1}(X).
Let
V^{\prime}=\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{S}(m))\subseteq\Gamma(X,F(m))\cong V
and let
F^{\prime}=\rho(V^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(-m))\subseteq\mathcal{O}_{S}\subseteq F
. Then clearly
s\in V^{\prime}\otimes R_{m}^{*}
and in particular
V^{\prime}\neq 0
. Hence we have inequality (
19
). If
\alpha_{2}(F^{\prime})<\alpha_{2}(F)
, then the leading term of the left-hand side of (
19
) is negative since
\delta<\frac{1}{2}
. Since the collection
\{F^{\prime}\}
is bounded by
[
Gro2
, Prop. 1.2(i)]
for fixed
X,v,m
, this leads to a contradiction for sufficiently large
l
. Hence
\alpha_{2}(F^{\prime})=\alpha_{2}(F)
and thus
\alpha_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{S})=\alpha_{2}(F)
. This proves the claim.

We then claim that
F
is pure. Consider the torsion subsheaf
TF\subseteq F
. Let
V^{\prime}=\Gamma(X,TF(m))\subseteq V
and let
F^{\prime}=\rho(V^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(-m))\subseteq TF\subseteq F
. If
V^{\prime}\neq 0
, then we have the inequality (
18
). Since the degree of the left-hand side of (
18
), as polynomial of
l
, is
\leq 1
and the degree of the right-hand side of (
18
) is 2, and the collection
\{F^{\prime}\}
is bounded, the inequality (
18
) leads to a contradiction for sufficiently large
l
. Hence we deduce that

(25)

\Gamma(X,TF(m))=0.

Consider the short exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{TF\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F/TF\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Define
\mathcal{O}_{Z}=\mathrm{im}(\mathcal{O}_{S}\to F\to F/TF)
.
Then we obtain a short exact sequence

(26)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F/TF\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{R\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

where
R=\operatorname{coker}(\mathcal{O}_{Z}\to F/TF)\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq 1}(X)
. Then both
F/TF
and
\mathcal{O}_{Z}
are pure 2-dimensional sheaves such that
\alpha_{2}(F/TF)=\alpha_{2}(\mathcal{O}_{Z})=\alpha_{2}(F)
. In particular, (
26
) defines a
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
pair. Moreover,
\alpha_{1}(F/TF)
is bounded above by
\alpha_{1}(F)
and bounded below
\alpha_{1}(\mathcal{O}_{Z})
, which is bounded below by a constant depending on
X,\alpha_{2}(F)
but not on
m
by
[
Pot1
, Cor. 2.13]
. In particular,
\alpha_{1}(F/TF)
takes finitely many values.
By Proposition
2.14
(2),
\chi(F/TF)
is bounded above by a constant depending on
X,v
but not on
m
. Since
\chi(TF)=\chi(F)-\chi(F/TF)
, we have

(27)

\chi(TF)\geq C(X,v)

for a constant
C(X,v)
depending on
X,v
but independent of
m
. By the Riemann-Roch formula, we have

\chi(TF(m))=r\cdot m+\chi(TF)

where
r=\alpha_{1}(F)-\alpha_{1}(F/TF)\geq 0
since
TF\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq 1}(X)
. Moreover,
r\geq 0
takes finitely many values independent of
m
. We deduce from (
25
) and (
27
) that, for all
F
as above, we have
r=0
for sufficiently large
m
, which means
TF
is 0-dimensional. Hence
TF
is globally generated and thus (
25
) implies
TF=0
. Therefore
F
is pure.

Combining the results in (2) and (
24
), we have open embeddings

\left[\mathcal{M}^{ss}|_{\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}}(\mathcal{L}^{(1)})/\mathrm{PGL}(V)\right]\subseteq{\curly Pair}^{(1)}(X,v)\subseteq\left[\mathcal{M}^{st}(\mathcal{L}^{(1)})/\mathrm{PGL}(V)\right]

which induces an open dense embedding

{\curly Pair}^{(1)}(X,v)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{st}(\mathcal{L}^{(1)})/\!/\mathrm{PGL}(V)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}^{ss}(\mathcal{L}^{(1)})/\!/\mathrm{PGL}(V).

Since
{\curly Pair}^{(1)}(X,v)
is proper and
\mathcal{M}^{ss}(\mathcal{L}^{(1)})/\!/\mathrm{PGL}(V)
is separated, the above map is an isomorphism. Consequently we have
\mathcal{M}^{st}(\mathcal{L}^{(1)})=\mathcal{M}^{ss}(\mathcal{L}^{(1)})
and
{\curly Pair}^{(1)}(X,v)=[\mathcal{M}^{st}(\mathcal{L}^{(1)})/\mathrm{PGL}(V)]
.
∎

Theorem
3.10
is a direct corollary of Proposition
3.17
.

Proof of Theorem
3.10
.

Since the stacks

{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(X,v),\quad[\mathcal{M}^{st}(X,\mathcal{L}^{(q)})/\mathrm{PGL}(V)],\quad[\mathcal{M}^{ss}(X,\mathcal{L}^{(q)})/\mathrm{PGL}(V)]

are open substacks of
{\curly Pair}(X,v)_{m}=[\mathcal{M}|_{\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}}/\mathrm{PGL}(V)]=[\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\circ}}(R\pi_{*}\mathbb{F})/\mathrm{PGL}(V)]
, it suffices to compare the
\mathbb{C}
-points. Proposition
3.17
completes the proof.
∎

We end this subsection with the following question:

Question 3.18
.

Does Theorem
3.10
holds for
d
-dimensional
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs with
d>2
as defined in Remark
2.5
?

3.3.
Moduli of complexes

In this subsection, we prove that the moduli spaces of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs are open subschemes of the moduli space of perfect complexes. We basically follow the reasoning in
[
PT1
]
, but the actual proof requires a finer argument.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety and let
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
.
Recall
[
Ina
,
Lie
]
that there exists a moduli space

{\curly Perf}(X,v)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}^{\mathrm{spl}}

of simple perfect complexes
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
on
X
with
\det(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
and
\operatorname{ch}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})=v
, as an algebraic space.

Theorem 3.19
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety of dimension
n\geq 4
,
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
, and
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
. Then we have an open embedding

(28)

{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(X,v)\hookrightarrow{\curly Perf}(X,v^{\prime})_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}^{\mathrm{spl}}:(F,s)\mapsto I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}:=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F]

where
v^{\prime}:=1-v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Theorem
3.19
. Throughout this subsection, we use the notation in Theorem
3.19
.

To any
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair
(F,s)
on
X
, we can associate a perfect complex

I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}:=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s}F]

with
\det(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
. We first show that
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
is
simple
.

Lemma 3.20
.

Given a
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair
(F,s)
on
X
, we have

\operatorname{Ext}^{<0}_{X}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})=0\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})=\mathbb{C}

for the associated perfect complex
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}:=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s}F]
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous
[
PT1
, Lem. 1.15]
. Consider the two canonical distinguished triangles

\textstyle{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F}
\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q[-1]}

where
Z
is the support of
F
and
Q
is the cokernel of
s
. We can form the following three induced distinguished triangles

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 37.37474pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-37.37474pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(F,\mathcal{O}_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 61.37474pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 61.37474pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathcal{O}_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 168.79579pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 168.79579pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},\mathcal{O}_{X})}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 40.30423pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-40.30423pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(Q,F)[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 64.30423pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 64.30423pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},F)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 159.81268pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 159.81268pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(\mathcal{I}_{Z},F)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces,

\displaystyle\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 36.52505pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-36.52505pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 60.52505pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 60.52505pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},\mathcal{O}_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 161.92734pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 161.92734pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},F)}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

The distinguished triangle (
3.3
) implies

(35)

\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},\mathcal{O}_{X})=\mathbb{C}\operatorname{Ext}^{<0}_{X}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},\mathcal{O}_{X})=0

since
\operatorname{Ext}^{\leq 1}_{X}(F,\mathcal{O}_{X})=H^{\geq n-1}(X,F)^{*}=0
. On the other hand, the distinguished triangle (
3.3
) implies

(36)

\operatorname{Ext}^{<0}_{X}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},F)=0

since
\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(Q,F)=0
by
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
-stability. Then the distinguished triangle (
3.3
) and equations (
35
) and (
36
) prove that
I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
is simple.
∎

Since
{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(X,v)
is a
fine
moduli space, there exists a universal family

\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}=[\mathcal{O}_{{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(X,v)\times X}\xrightarrow{}\mathbb{F}],

which defines a map

(37)

{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(X,v)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}{\curly Perf}(X,v)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}^{\mathrm{spl}}

between the moduli spaces by Lemma
3.21
below.

Lemma 3.21
.

Let
f:\mathscr{X}\to\mathscr{Y}
be a smooth projective morphism of schemes. Let
\mathcal{F}
be a
\mathscr{Y}
-flat coherent sheaf on
\mathscr{X}
. If
\mathrm{codim}(\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{F}_{y}))\geq 2
for all
y\in\mathscr{Y}
, then
\det(\mathcal{F})\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}
.

Proof.

This follows directly from
[
HK
, Prop. 3.5]
.
∎

We show that the above map (
37
) satisfies the
infinitesimal lifting property
.

Proposition 3.22
.

Let
B_{0}\subseteq B
be an nilpotent extension of 0-dimensional schemes with a unique closed point
b\in B_{0}
. Let
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
be a perfect complex on
X\times B
with trivial determinant.
Let
i:X\times B_{0}\hookrightarrow X\times B
be the inclusion map.
If
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}|_{X\times B_{0}}\cong[\mathcal{O}_{X\times B_{0}}\xrightarrow{s_{0}}F_{0}]
for a
B_{0}
-flat family
(F_{0},s_{0})
of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs over
B_{0}
, then there exists a
B
-flat family
(F,s)
of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs over
B
such that
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\ \cong[\mathcal{O}_{X\times B}\xrightarrow{s}F]
and
(F,s)|_{X\times B_{0}}=(F_{0},s_{0})
.

Proof.

We first take
q=0,1
. The (easier) case
q=-1
is discussed at the end of the proof. Let
(F_{b},s_{b})
be the fibre of
(F_{0},s_{0})
over the point
b\in B_{0}
. Since
F_{b}\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq 1}(X)
, we have
\mathrm{depth}_{\mathcal{O}_{X,x}}((F_{b})_{x})\geq 1
for all closed points
x\in\mathrm{Supp}(F_{b})
.
By the Auslander-Buchsbaum formula, we have

\mathrm{pd}_{\mathcal{O}_{X,x}}((F_{b})_{x})\leq n-1

for all
x\in X
.
Then
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}|_{X\times\{b\}}\cong[\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s_{b}}F_{b}]
has tor-amplitude
[-(n-2),1]
, and thus we have a quasi-isomorphism with a complex of locally free sheaves

(38)

I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}|_{X\times\{b\}}\cong[F^{-(n-2)}\to\cdots\to F^{0}\to F^{1}].

Since
X\times B
is quasi-projective,
I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
is quasi-isomorphic to a bounded complex of locally free sheaves. Using Nakayama’s lemma, and the fact that a morphism between locally free sheaves is injective on the fibres if and only if it is injective with locally free cokernel, we see from (
38
) that there exists a quasi-isomorphism with a complex of locally free sheaves

I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\cong[E^{-(n-2)}\to\cdots\to E^{0}\to E^{1}].

Moreover since
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
has amplitude
[0,1]
by
[
Lie
, Lem. 2.1.4]
, we have

I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\cong[A\to E^{1}]

where
A=\operatorname{coker}(E^{-1}\to E^{0})
is
B
-flat.

We claim that the canonical map

h^{0}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\to h^{0}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})^{**}

is injective. Indeed, let
K
be the kernel of the above map
h^{0}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\to h^{0}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})^{**}
. Note that
h^{0}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
is a vector bundle away from the support of
F_{0}
by
[
Lie
, Lem. 2.1.4]
.
Hence
\dim(K)\leq 2<n
.

The support
W
of
h^{1}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
is of dimension
\leq q
. This follows from the fact that
i^{*}h^{1}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\cong h^{1}(I_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
and Nakayama’s lemma.
Away from
W
, the coherent sheaf
h^{0}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
is
B
-flat because
A
is
B
-flat.
Since the ideal sheaf
h^{0}(I_{b}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
is torsion free, all non-zero subsheaves of
h^{0}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})|_{X\times B\setminus W}

are
n
-dimensional by Lemma
3.23
below. Therefore
K
is (set-theoretically) supported in
W
. Thus we have
\dim(K)\leq\dim(W)\leq q.

Consider the local-to-global spectral sequence

H^{i}(X\times B,\curly Ext^{j}_{X\times B}(A,K\otimes\omega_{X}))\Rightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{i+j}_{X\times B}(A,K\otimes\omega_{X}).

Since

\dim(K\otimes\omega_{X})\leq q\leq 1

and there is a resolution
A\cong[E^{-(n-2)}\to\cdots\to E^{0}]
of length
n-2
, we have

\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{X\times B}(A,K\otimes\omega_{X})=0.

By Serre duality
R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(K,A)\cong R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(A,K\otimes\omega_{X}))^{\vee}[-n],
we have

\operatorname{Hom}_{X\times B}(K,A)^{*}\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{n}_{X\times B}(A,K\otimes\omega_{X})=0,

where the dual is taken as a
\Gamma(B,\mathcal{O}_{B})
-module.
Since
K\subseteq A
, we deduce
K=0
.
This proves the claim.

Since the rest of the proof is exactly the same as
[
PT1
]
, we will only sketch the remaining arguments. By
[
Kol1
, Lem. 6.13]
,
h^{0}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})^{**}
is a vector bundle away from the support of
Q=h^{1}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
.
In the proof of
[
Kol1
, Lem. 6.13]
, it is also shown that a reflexive rank 1 sheaf which is locally free outside a subset of codimension
n-1\geq 3
, is in fact locally free everywhere. Therefore
h^{0}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})^{**}
is locally free.

Next, we have
\det(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\cong\mathcal{O}_{X\times B}
and
W\ \subset\mathrm{Supp}(F_{0})
, thus

h^{0}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})^{**}|_{(X\times B)\setminus\mathrm{Supp}(F_{0})}\cong h^{0}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})|_{(X\times B)\setminus\mathrm{Supp}(F_{0})}\cong\mathcal{O}_{(X\times B)\setminus\mathrm{Supp}(F_{0})}.

Since
\mathrm{Supp}(F_{0})
has codimension
n-2\geq 2
, we find
h^{0}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})^{**}\cong\mathcal{O}_{X\times B}
globally and
\mathcal{I}_{Z}\cong h^{0}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
is an ideal sheaf of a closed subscheme
Z\subseteq X\times B
.
The distinguished triangle

\textstyle{\mathcal{I}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q[-1]}

defines an element

\alpha\in\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X\times B}(Q,\mathcal{I}_{Z})\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X\times B}(Q,\mathcal{O}_{Z})

since
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X\times B}(Q,\mathcal{O}_{X\times B})=\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X\times B}(Q,\mathcal{O}_{X\times B})=0
(recall
q\leq 1
and
n\geq 4
). Hence we have a short exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{Z}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Q\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

of coherent sheaves on
X\times B
. Therefore we have an isomorphism

I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\cong[\mathcal{O}_{X\times B}\xrightarrow{s}F]

of perfect complexes. Since
F
and
F_{0}
are 2-dimensional,
\operatorname{Hom}_{X\times B}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},\mathcal{O}_{X\times B})\cong\Gamma(X\times B,\mathcal{O}_{X\times B})\cong\Gamma(B,\mathcal{O}_{B})
and
\operatorname{Hom}_{X\times B_{0}}(I_{0}^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},\mathcal{O}_{X\times B_{0}})\cong\Gamma(B_{0},\mathcal{O}_{B_{0}})
. As in
[
PT1
]
, we therefore have
Li^{*}F\cong F_{0}
. Hence
F
is flat over
B
by
[
PT1
, Lem. 2.5]
, which completes the proof.

For
q=-1
,
I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
is only quasi-isomorphic to a bounded complex of locally free sheaves
[E^{-(n-1)}\to\cdots\to E^{1}]
. However, this time
W=\emptyset
, so
h^{0}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\to h^{0}(I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})^{**}
is obviously injective (Lemma
3.23
). The rest of the proof proceeds as in the previous paragraph.
∎

We need the following lemma to complete the proof of Proposition
3.22
.

Lemma 3.23
.

Let
(A,\mathfrak{m})
be a local Artinian
\mathbb{C}
-algebra and let
X
be a quasi-projective scheme. Consider an
A
-flat family
F
of sheaves on
X\times\operatorname{Spec}\,(A)
whose fibre
F_{0}=F\otimes_{A}A/\mathfrak{m}
is a pure sheaf. Then
F
is also a pure sheaf, i.e., for all non-zero subsheaves
G\subseteq F
, we have
\dim(G)=\dim(F)
.

Proof.

Since
A
is Artinian, we may take a sequence of ideals
[
Eis
, Prop. 3.7]

0=\mathfrak{m}_{N}\subset\cdots\subset\mathfrak{m}_{2}\subset\mathfrak{m}_{1}=\mathfrak{m},\quad\text{such that}\quad\mathfrak{m}_{i}/\mathfrak{m}_{i+1}\cong A/\mathfrak{m}\,.

We let
\mathfrak{m}_{0}=A
. Suppose we have a subsheaf
G\subset F
such that
\dim(G)<\dim(F)
. Since
F
is flat over
A
, we have a short exact sequence

(39)

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{m}_{i+1}\cdot F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{m}_{i}\cdot F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

of sheaves on
X\times\operatorname{Spec}\,(A)
, where
\mathfrak{m}_{i}\otimes_{A}F=\mathfrak{m}_{i}\cdot F
. Consider the induced morphism

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G\cap\mathfrak{m}\cdot F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\frac{G}{G\cap\mathfrak{m}\cdot F}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{m}\cdot F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{F_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

of short exact sequences. Note that

\dim(\tfrac{G}{G\cap\mathfrak{m}\cdot F})\leq\dim(G)<\dim(F)=\dim(F_{0}).

Since
F_{0}
is pure, we have
\frac{G}{G\cap\mathfrak{m}\cdot F}=0
. Therefore we deduce that

G\subseteq\mathfrak{m}\cdot F.

Moreover, applying this argument to (
39
), we deduce that

G\subseteq\mathfrak{m}_{i}\cdot F\implies G\subseteq\mathfrak{m}_{i+1}\cdot F.

Therefore we obtain
G\subseteq\mathfrak{m}_{N}\cdot F=0
by induction on
i
.
∎

Now we can simply deduce Theorem
3.19
from Lemma
2.8
and Proposition
3.22
as follows:

Proof of Theorem
3.19
.

By Lemma
2.8
, the map

{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(X,v)\xrightarrow{\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}}{\curly Perf}(X,v)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}^{\mathrm{spl}}

in (
37
) is injective on
\mathbb{C}
-points. Since
\mathbb{C}
is algebraically closed, the above map is universally injective. In order to verify that the above map is formally smooth it is enough to check the lifting property along extensions of local Artinian
\mathbb{C}
-algebras
[
Sta
, 02HW]
which is done in Proposition
3.22
. Since
{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(X,v)
is of finite type, the above map is étale. Moreover the above map is universally injective and étale, thus it is an open embedding (
[
Sta
, tag 02LC]
).
∎

Remark 3.24
.

The open embedding (
28
) in Theorem
3.19
induces a derived enhancement of the moduli space
{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(X,v)
of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs. The tangent complex at
(F,s)\in{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(X,v)
is given as

\mathbb{T}|_{(F,s)}=R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})_{0}[1]

where
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s}F]
. This derived structure (of perfect complexes) is
different
from the derived structure (of pairs) in Remark
3.9
.

Remark 3.25
.

For
\mathrm{PT}_{1}
-stability, Theorem
3.19
was independently proved by Gholampour-Jiang-Lo in
[
GJL
, Prop. 2.18]
using
[
Lo
, Lem. 5.12]
.

Remark 3.26
.

In the proof of Proposition
3.22
we only used the fact that
T_{0}(F)=0
and
Q\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq 1}(X)
, and the statement can be generalized as follows.
Let
{\curly Perf}(X)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}
be the moduli stack of universally gluable perfect complexes
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
on
X
with
\det(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
which is an algebraic stack locally finite type over
\mathbb{C}

[
Lie
, Thm. 4.2.1]
. By the proof of Lemma
3.7
we have an open substack

{\curly Pair}^{[0,1]}(X):=\Big\{(F,s)\in{\curly Pair}(X):T_{0}(F)=0Q\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq 1}(X)\Big\}

inside
{\curly Pair}(X)
. For
q=0,1
, we have

\textstyle{{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{{\curly Pair}^{[0,1]}(X)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\epsilon}
\textstyle{{\curly Pair}(X)\,,}
\textstyle{{\curly Perf}(X)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}}

where the horizontal morphisms are open embedding and the morphism
\epsilon
is formally smooth by Proposition
3.22
.
We claim that the map

\epsilon:{\curly Pair}^{[0,1]}(X)\to{\curly Perf}(X)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}

is an open embedding.
Indeed, it suffices to show that the induced map
\epsilon(\mathbb{C})
on the groupoids of
\mathbb{C}
-valued points is fully faithful. By the same argument as for Lemma
2.8
, the induced map
\pi_{0}(\epsilon(\mathbb{C}))
on the sets of isomorphism classes is injective. By (
3.3
) (in fact by (
81
)), we have an exact sequence

0\to\operatorname{Ext}_{X}^{-1}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},F)\to\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})_{0}\to\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(F,\mathcal{O}_{X})=0

for
(F,s)\in{\curly Pair}^{[0,1]}(X)
and hence
\epsilon(\mathbb{C})
preserves stabilizers. This proves the claim.

3.4.
Polynomial Bridgeland stability

In this subsection, we prove that the
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
-stability conditions can be realized as Bayer’s polynomial Bridgeland stability conditions
[
Bri
,
Bay
]
on the derived category.
The results of this subsection, which are inspired by
[
GJL
]
, are not used elsewhere in the paper. We use a different torsion pair compared to
[
GJL
]
, which allows us to incorporate 2-dimensional (Gieseker) stable sheaves as well.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety of dimension
n\geq 4
. Let
D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X)
be the bounded derived category of coherent sheaves on
X
.
We consider the perversity function

p:\{0,1,\ldots,n\}\to\mathbb{Z},\quad p(0)=p(1)=p(2)=0,\quad p(3)=\ldots=p(n)=-1.

The perversity function defines a bounded
t
-structure on
D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X)

[
AB
]
and this
t
-structure coincides with the one associated to the torsion pair

(\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq 2}(X),\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq 3}(X)),

[
HRS
, Cor. 2.2]
. We denote the heart by

\mathcal{A}:=\mathcal{A}^{p}:=\langle\,\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq 2}(X),\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq 3}(X)[1]\,\rangle\,.

The abelian category
\mathcal{A}
consists of the objects
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\in D^{[-1,0]}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X)
satisfying

h^{-1}(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq 3}(X)\quad\textrm{and}\quad h^{0}(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq 2}(X).

A sequence

0\to A^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\to E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\to B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\to 0
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{A}
if and only if the associated sequence in
D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X)
is a distinguished triangle. For any
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\in\mathcal{A}
, the following sequence is exact in
\mathcal{A}

0\to h^{-1}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})[1]\to E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\to h^{0}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\to 0.

For any
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair
I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
on
X
, we have
I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}[1]\in\mathcal{A}
. Throughout this subsection, we fix
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
such that

v_{\leq n-3}=0
and
v_{n-2}\neq 0
.

Denote the semi-closed upper half plane by

\mathbb{H}=\{z\in\mathbb{C}\,:\,z\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}\cdot e^{i\pi\phi(z)}\quad 0<\phi(z)\leq 1\},

where
\phi(z)
denotes the phase of
z
. Denote by
\mathbb{H}^{\circ}\subset\mathbb{H}
the open upper half plane. Recall
[
Bay
]
that a Bayer stability condition (also known as a polynomial Bridgeland stability condition) is determined by a
stability vector
, i.e., a sequence

\rho=(\rho_{0},\ldots,\rho_{n})\in(\mathbb{C}^{*})^{n+1}

such that
\rho_{d}/\rho_{d+1}\in\mathbb{H}^{\circ}
for
0\leq d\leq n-1
and
(-1)^{p(d)}\rho_{d}\in\mathbb{H}
for all
d
. We fix a polarization
H
of
X
.
Define the central charge
Z\colon K^{0}(X)\to\mathbb{C}[x]
as

Z(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})(x)=\sum_{d=0}^{n}x^{d}\rho_{d}\int_{X}H^{d}\operatorname{ch}(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\mathrm{td}(X),

for any
E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\in K^{0}(X)
.
For
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\in\mathcal{A}
, let
\phi
be the polynomial phase function, i.e.,

Z(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})(x)\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}\cdot e^{\pi i\phi(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})(x)},\quad x\gg 0,

for some continuous function germ
\phi\in(\phi_{0},\phi_{0}+1]
for some
\phi_{0}
. We say
\phi<\psi
if and only if
\phi(x)<\psi(x)
for all
0\ll x<+\infty
, and similarly for
\phi\leq\psi
.

An object
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
in
\mathcal{A}
is called
Bayer stable
(resp.
Bayer semi-stable
) with respect to the stability vector
\rho
if for all proper non-zero subobjects
D^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\subset E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
, we have
\phi(D^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})<\phi(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
(resp.
\phi(D^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\leq\phi(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
).

Definition 3.27
.

Consider stability vectors
\rho^{(q)}
for
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
satisfying the following:

(40)

\rho^{(-1)}

\phi(-\rho_{n})>\phi(\rho_{0})>\phi(\rho_{1})>\phi(\rho_{2})

\rho^{(0)}

\phi(\rho_{0})>\phi(-\rho_{n})>\phi(\rho_{1})>\phi(\rho_{2})

\rho^{(1)}

\phi(\rho_{0})>\phi(\rho_{1})>\phi(-\rho_{n})>\phi(\rho_{2})
.

From the condition on the stability vector, we have

(41)

\phi(-\rho_{3}),\ldots,\phi(-\rho_{n-1})>\phi(-\rho_{n}).

The following proposition is a minor modification of
[
Bay
, Prop. 6.1.1]
.

Proposition 3.28
.

Fix
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
such that
v_{\leq n-3}=0
and
v_{n-2}\neq 0
.
Let
E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\in D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(X)
with
\operatorname{ch}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})=v-1
and
\det(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\cong\mathcal{O}_{X}
. Let
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
. Then
E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
is a
\rho^{(q)}
-semistable object of
\mathcal{A}
if and only if
E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\cong I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}[1]
for some
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair
(F,s)
where
I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s}F]
is the associated complex. Moreover,
E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
cannot be strictly
\rho^{(q)}
-semistable.

Proof.

Let
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
be a
\rho^{(q)}
-semistable object of
\mathcal{A}
satisfying the above conditions. We first show that
h^{-1}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
is a rank one torsion free sheaf. Since
h^{0}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq 2}(X)
,
h^{-1}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
has rank
1
and
h^{-1}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq 3}(X)
. Suppose
h^{-1}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
has a non-trivial torsion subsheaf. Let
T\subset h^{-1}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
be the maximal torsion subsheaf. Then
0\to T[1]\to h^{-1}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})[1]\to(h^{-1}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})/T)[1]\to 0
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{A}
and hence
T[1]
is a subobject of
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
in
\mathcal{A}
. Then (
41
) leads to a contradiction. Therefore
h^{-1}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
is a rank one torsion free sheaf with trivial determinant.
Next we show that
h^{0}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\leq 1}(X)
. By assumption
h^{0}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
has dimension at most two. Because
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
is
\rho^{(q)}
-semistable,
h^{0}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
has dimension at most one, otherwise,
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\twoheadrightarrow h^{0}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
destabilizes
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
.
By a standard argument (see the last two paragraphs of the proof of Proposition
3.22
), we have
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\cong I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}[1]
,
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F]
for some 2-dimensional coherent sheaf
F
.
For
q=1
, we show that
F
is pure. Let
T\subset F
be the maximal torsion subsheaf of
F
. Then
0\to T\to I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}[1]\to[\mathcal{O}_{X}\to F/T][1]\to 0
is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{A}
and hence
T
is a subobject of
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
.
By the stability vector (
40
), we have
T=0
. For
q=0
, a similar argument shows that
F
has no non-trivial 0-dimensional subsheaf and
h^{0}(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
is 0-dimensional. For
q=-1
we have
h^{0}(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})=0
.

Conversely, suppose
E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\cong I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}[1]
for some
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair
(F,s)
where
I^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s}F]
. Then
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\in\mathcal{A}
. We prove
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
is a
\rho^{(q)}
-stable object.
Take any short exact sequence

0\to A^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\to E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\to B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\to 0

in
\mathcal{A}

with
A^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\neq 0
.
Consider its long exact sequence

0\to h^{-1}(A^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\to h^{-1}(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\to h^{-1}(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\to h^{0}(A^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\to h^{0}(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\to h^{0}(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\to 0.

We distinguish three cases:

Case 1:

0\neq h^{-1}(A^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\subsetneq h^{-1}(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
. Then
h^{-1}(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
has rank 0. If
h^{-1}(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\neq 0
, then it has dimension in
\{3,\ldots,n-1\}
so we obtain
\phi(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})<\phi(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
from (
41
). Suppose
h^{-1}(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})=0
, then
B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\cong h^{0}(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
and since
h^{0}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
is at most 1-dimensional,
h^{0}(B^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
has dimension
\leq 1
. For
q=1
, (
40
) implies that
\phi(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})<\phi(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
. For
q=0
, we have
h^{0}(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
is 0-dimensional so we also find
\phi(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})<\phi(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
. Finally, for
q=-1
we have
h^{0}(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})=0
contradicting the assumption
B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\neq 0
.

Case 2:

h^{-1}(A^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})=h^{-1}(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
. Then
h^{-1}(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\subset h^{0}(A^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
so
h^{-1}(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})=0
and
B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\cong h^{0}(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
. Then
\phi(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})<\phi(B^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
follows as in Case 1.

Case 3:

h^{-1}(A^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})=0
. Then
A^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\cong h^{0}(A^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
has dimension
\leq 2
and hence
\operatorname{Hom}(A^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})\cong\operatorname{Hom}(A^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet},F)
. Since
A^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
is a non-zero subobject of
E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}
and
(F,s)
is a
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair, we deduce that
A^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}\cong h^{0}(A^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
is 2-dimensional. Then (
40
) implies
\phi(A^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})<\phi(E^{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet})
.
∎

We characterize rank zero
\rho^{(q)}
-semistable objects in
\mathcal{A}
.

Proposition 3.29
.

Let
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
and
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
such that
v_{\leq n-3}=0
and
v_{n-2}\neq 0
.
An object
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\in\mathcal{A}
with
\operatorname{ch}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})=v
is
\rho^{(q)}
-stable (resp.
\rho^{(q)}
-semistable) if and only if
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\cong F
for some Gieseker stable (resp. semistable) sheaf
F
on
X
with
\operatorname{ch}(F)=v
.

Proof.

Let
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\in\mathcal{A}
be a
\rho^{(q)}
-(semi)stable object with
\operatorname{ch}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})=v
. Since
h^{-1}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\in\mathrm{Coh}_{\geq 3}(X)
, we have
h^{-1}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})=0
and hence
E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\cong h^{0}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
. Then
h^{0}(E^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
is Gieseker (semi)stable
[
Bay
, Sect. 2.1]
. Conversely, it is easy to show that any Gieseker (semi)stable sheaf
F
on
X
with
\operatorname{ch}(F)=v
determines a
\rho^{(q)}
-(semi)stable object in
\mathcal{A}

[
Bay
, Sect. 2.1]
.
∎

3.5.
Relative version

We now generalize the main results in this section to the relative setting.

We first fix some notation.
Let

f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}

be a smooth projective morphism of schemes of relative dimension
n
.
Consider the
algebraic de Rham complex

\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}:=\left[\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{d_{DR}}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\xrightarrow{d_{DR}}\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\xrightarrow{d_{DR}}\cdots\xrightarrow{d_{DR}}\Omega^{n}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\right],

as in
[
Gro1
,
Del1
]
. Define the
Hodge bundles
as

(42)

\mathcal{H}^{k}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}):=R^{k}f_{*}\left(\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}^{\bullet}\right).

When
\mathcal{B}
is an affine scheme, we write
H_{DR}^{k}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}):=\Gamma(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}^{k}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})).

Note that there is a a natural spectral sequence

E_{1}^{pq}=R^{q}f_{*}\Omega^{p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\Rightarrow\mathcal{H}^{p+q}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})

given by the stupid truncations

\Omega^{\geq p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}:=\left[0\to\cdots\to 0\to\Omega^{p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\xrightarrow{d_{DR}}\Omega^{p+1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\xrightarrow{d_{DR}}\cdots\xrightarrow{d_{DR}}\Omega^{n}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\right].

By
[
Del1
]
, the spectral sequence degenerates at
E_{1}
. Hence we have a filtration

F^{p}\mathcal{H}^{p+q}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}):=\operatorname{im}\left(R^{p+q}f_{*}\big(\Omega^{\geq p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\big)\to R^{p+q}f_{*}\left(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\right)\right)

of the Hodge bundles
\mathcal{H}^{p+q}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
such that

(43)

F^{p}\mathcal{H}^{p+q}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\cong R^{p+q}f_{*}\left(\Omega^{\geq p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\right)\frac{F^{p}\mathcal{H}^{p+q}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})}{F^{p+1}\mathcal{H}^{p+q}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})}\cong R^{q}f_{*}\Omega^{p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}.

Moreover, the Hodge bundles
\mathcal{H}^{p+q}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
, the filtrations
F^{p}\mathcal{H}^{p+q}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
, and the quotients
R^{q}f_{*}\Omega^{p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}
are vector bundles and stable under base change, by
[
Del1
, Thm. 5.5]
.

Consider the
Gauss-Manin connection

\nabla:\mathcal{H}^{k}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\to\mathcal{H}^{k}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\otimes\Omega_{\mathcal{B}}^{1}.

As in
[
KO
]
, the Gauss-Manin connection
\nabla
can be defined via the canonical filtration of
\bigwedge^{p}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}}
given by the short exact sequence

\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 5.5pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&&&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-5.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 29.5pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{f^{*}\Omega_{\mathcal{B}}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 83.38202pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 83.38202pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega_{\mathcal{X}}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 127.00705pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 127.00705pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 180.20853pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 180.20853pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{0}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces.

Indeed, the first two terms of the filtration give us a map

\Omega^{p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\to\Omega^{p-1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\otimes f^{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}[1].

Then by gathering the above maps for all
p
and taking the derived pushforward
R^{q}f_{*}
, we obtain a flat connection
\nabla
called the Gauss-Manin connection.

Following
[
Blo
]
, we define the notion of
horizontal sections
of Hodge bundles.

Definition 3.30

(cf.
[
Blo
, Rem. 3.9]
)
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a smooth projective morphism of schemes of constant relative dimension.
We say that a section
\widetilde{v}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}^{k}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}))
is
horizontal
if

\nabla(\widetilde{v})=0\in\Gamma(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}^{k}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}).

Roughly speaking, the horizontal sections
\widetilde{v}
of
\mathcal{H}^{k}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
are locally constant families of cohomology classes
\widetilde{v}_{b}\in H^{*}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathbb{C})
on the fibres
\mathcal{X}_{b}:=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\{b\}
. The following remark justifies this interpretation.

Remark 3.31
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a smooth projective morphism of schemes of constant relative dimension.

(1)

By considering the associated analytic spaces, we have

\mathcal{H}_{DR}^{k}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})^{\mathrm{an}}\cong R^{k}f^{\mathrm{an}}_{*}\mathbb{C}\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{an}}}\nabla^{\mathrm{an}}\cong\operatorname{id}\otimes d_{\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{an}}}

when
\mathcal{B}
is smooth
[
KO
]
.Thus a section
\widetilde{v}
of
\mathcal{H}^{k}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
is horizontal if and only if

\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{an}}=v\otimes 1

for some
v\in\Gamma(\mathcal{B},R^{k}f^{\mathrm{an}}_{*}\mathbb{C})
.

(2)

(a)

Firstly, assume that
\mathcal{B}=\operatorname{Spec}\,(A)
for a local Artinian
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
A
. Then we have isomorphisms

H^{k}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\cong H^{k}_{DR}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}A

by
[
Blo
, Prop. 3.8]
, where
X
is the fibre of
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
over the unique closed point and
H^{k}_{DR}(X):=H^{k}_{DR}(X/\operatorname{Spec}\,(\mathbb{C}))
.
Then the horizontal sections are exactly the sections of the form

v\otimes 1\in H^{k}_{DR}(X)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}A

for some
v\in H^{k}_{DR}(X)\cong H^{k}(X,\mathbb{C})
.

(b)

Secondly, for general
\mathcal{B}
, a section
\widetilde{v}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}^{k}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}))
is horizontal if and only if its pullbacks

\widetilde{v}|_{\mathcal{A}}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{H}^{k}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{A}}/\mathcal{A}))

are horizontal for all morphisms
\mathcal{A}=\operatorname{Spec}\,(A)\to\mathcal{B}
from local Artinian
\mathbb{C}
-algebras
A
. Here
\mathcal{X}_{\mathcal{A}}:=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{A}
.
This can be shown by the Krull intersection theorem.

We have a relative moduli space of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs on the fibres of
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
.

Theorem 3.32
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a quasi-projective scheme and
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
a smooth projective morphism of relative dimension
n
. Let
\widetilde{v}\in\bigoplus_{p}\Gamma(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}))
be a horizontal section
and
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
.
Let
{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})
be the moduli stack consisting of triples
(b,F,s)
of a morphism
b:T\to\mathcal{B}
, a
T
-flat coherent sheaf
F
on
\mathcal{X}_{T}:=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{B}}T
, and a section
s\in\Gamma(\mathcal{X}_{T},F)
such that for all
t\in T(\mathbb{C})
the pair
(F_{t},s_{t})
is a
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair with
\operatorname{ch}(F_{t})=\widetilde{v}_{t}\in H^{*}(\mathcal{X}_{t},\mathbb{C})
. Then we have the following properties:

(1)

The canonical map

{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})\to\mathcal{B},\quad(b,F,s)\mapsto b

is a projective morphism of schemes.

(2)

If
n\geq 4
, then the canonical map

{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})\to{\curly Perf}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{\mathrm{spl}},\quad(b,F,s)\mapsto(b,[\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}_{T}}\xrightarrow{s}F])

is an open embedding.

Proof.

Fix a polynomial
P(t)\in\mathbb{Q}[t]
and let
{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},P)
be the moduli stack of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs on the fibres of
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
with Hilbert polynomial
P(t)
with respect to some relative very ample line bundle.
Note that we can find a closed embedding
\mathcal{X}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathcal{B}}
over
\mathcal{B}
since
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
is projective and
\mathcal{B}
is quasi-projective.
Consider the canonical maps

{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},P)\hookrightarrow{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathcal{B}}/\mathcal{B},P)\xrightarrow{\cong}{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(\mathbb{P}^{N},P)\times\mathcal{B}

where the first map is a closed embedding, the second map is an isomorphism, and the last term is a projective scheme over
\mathcal{B}
by Theorem
3.10
.
Indeed, as a functor over
{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathcal{B}}/\mathcal{B},P)
,
{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},P)
is the zero locus of the canonical map

\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathcal{B}}}\otimes\mathbb{F}\to\mathbb{F}

where
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathcal{B}}}
is the ideal sheaf of
\mathcal{X}\subseteq\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathcal{B}}
and
(\mathbb{F},s)
is the universal pair on
{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathcal{B}}/\mathcal{B},P)\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathcal{B}}
.
By
[
EGA3
, Cor. 7.7.8]
, the map
\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathcal{B}}}\otimes\mathbb{F}\to\mathbb{F}
can be represented by the abelian cone of a coherent sheaf on
{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(\mathbb{P}^{N}_{\mathcal{B}}/\mathcal{B},P)
since
\mathbb{F}
is flat.
Hence the desired map is a closed embedding since the abelian cone is separated over the base. Therefore we have (1) since
{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})
is an open and closed subscheme of the projective scheme
{\curly Pair}^{(q)}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},P)
(for appropriate
P
). This proves (1).

All the arguments in subsection
3.3
also works in the relative setting. This proves (2).
∎

We end this subsection with a technical proposition stating that the relative Chern character of a perfect complex is horizontal. This fact will be used several times in section
4
and
5
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a smooth projective morphism of schemes of constant relative dimension.
Let
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
be a perfect complex on
\mathcal{X}
. Let

\operatorname{At}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}):I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\to I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}[1]

be the (relative) Atiyah class of
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
.
Let

\operatorname{At}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}^{p}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}):=\operatorname{At}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\wedge\cdots\wedge\operatorname{At}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}):I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\to I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\otimes\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}^{p}[p]

be the induced map.
We define the (relative)
Chern character

\operatorname{ch}_{p}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
of
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
as

(44)

H^{p}(\mathcal{X},\Omega^{p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})\to\Gamma(\mathcal{B},R^{p}f_{*}\Omega^{p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}),\quad\frac{1}{p!}\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{At}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}^{p}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}))\mapsto\operatorname{ch}_{p}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}).

The Chern character
\operatorname{ch}_{p}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
in (
44
) is horizontal. More precisely, we have the following:

Proposition 3.33
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a smooth projective morphism of schemes of constant relative dimension.
Let
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
be a perfect complex on
\mathcal{X}
.
Then there exists a section

\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}_{p}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\in\Gamma(\mathcal{B},F^{p}\mathcal{H}_{DR}^{2p}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}))

satisfying the following properties:

(1)

Under the projection map

F^{p}\mathcal{H}^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\twoheadrightarrow R^{p}f_{*}\Omega^{p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}
given by (
43
),
the section
\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}_{p}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
maps to the Chern character
\operatorname{ch}_{p}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
in (
44
).

(2)

Under the inclusion map
F^{p}\mathcal{H}_{DR}^{2p}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\hookrightarrow\mathcal{H}^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
given by (
43
),
the section
\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}_{p}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
maps to a horizontal section.

(3)

Under the restriction map
\Gamma(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{H}_{DR}^{2p}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}))\to H_{DR}^{2p}(\mathcal{X}_{b}/\operatorname{Spec}\,(\mathbb{C}))
for any
\mathbb{C}
-point
b\in\mathcal{B}
, the section
\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}_{p}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
maps to the usual Chern character
\operatorname{ch}_{p}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}_{b})\in H^{2p}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathbb{C})
.

The section
\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}_{p}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
is uniquely determined by properties
(2)
and
(3)
.

Remark 3.34
.

We note that an analogous statement for the fundamental class
[\mathcal{Z}]
of a local complete intersection
\mathcal{Z}\subseteq\mathcal{X}
was shown in
[
Blo
, Prop. 5.6]
.

We briefly summarize how we prove Proposition
3.33
.
We use the theory of Hochschild homology and cyclic homology, see Loday
[
Lod
]
for the case of rings and Weibel
[
Wei2
,
Wei3
]
for the case of schemes.
9
9

9

By Keller
[
Kel
]
, the cyclic homologies defined in
[
Wei3
]
coincide with the cyclic homologies defined in McCarthy
[
McC
]
via the dg category of perfect complexes.

By the Hochschild-Kostant-Rosenberg (HKR) theorem
[
HKR
]
, we may identify the Hodge bundles, the filtrations, and the associated quotients in (
42
) and (
43
) with the periodic cyclic homology, the negative cyclic homology, and the Hochschild homology, respectively. Moreover, the Chern character in (
44
) can be identified with the Dennis trace map.
Then the key to proving Proposition
3.33
is Goodwillie’s lift of the Dennis trace map to the negative cyclic homology
[
Goo2
]
.
10
10

10

In the language of
[
PTVV
]
, Chern characters lift to
p
-shifted
closed

p
-forms
[
TV2
,
TV3
]
. By
[
HSSS
]
, the Chern character of
[
TV3
]
matches with the classical Dennis trace.

The proof was inspired by Pridham
[
Pri
]
.

Proof of Proposition
3.33
.

Note that the uniqueness of the section
\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}_{p}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
follows immediately from the properties (2) and (3) in Proposition
3.33
and Remark
3.31
(2). Hence it suffices to consider the statement locally on the base
\mathcal{B}
. Thus we may assume that
\mathcal{B}
is an affine scheme. Then (
44
) is an isomorphism.

By the HKR theorem in
[
Wei3
, Thm. 3.3]
, we have natural isomorphisms

(45)

\displaystyle\mathsf{HN}^{i}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{p}F^{p}H_{DR}^{2p+i}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}),

\displaystyle\mathsf{HP}^{i}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{p}H^{2p+i}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}),

\displaystyle\mathsf{HH}^{i}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{p}R^{p+i}f_{*}(\Omega^{p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}),

\displaystyle\mathsf{HC}^{i}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})

\displaystyle\cong\bigoplus_{p}\frac{H^{2p+i}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})}{F^{p+1}H^{2p+i}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})},

where
\mathsf{HN}
,
\mathsf{HP}
,
\mathsf{HH}
, and
\mathsf{HC}
denote the negative cyclic homology, the periodic cyclic homology, the Hochschild homology, and the cyclic homology, respectively, with the cohomological convention. More specifically, the isomorphisms (
45
) are induced by the left inverse (
\pi
in
[
Lod
, Lem. 1.3.14]
and
e
in
[
Wei1
, Cor. 9.4.4]
) of the antisymmetrization map.

The relative Chern character
\operatorname{ch}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}):=(\operatorname{ch}_{p}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}))_{p}
in (
44
) comes from the absolute Chern character. More precisely, we have a commutative diagram

(46)

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ch}_{\mathcal{X}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{p}\mathbb{L}^{p}_{\mathcal{X}}[p]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ch}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{p}\Omega^{p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}[p]}

in the derived category of
\mathcal{X}
, where
\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{X}}
denotes Illusie’s cotangent complex
[
Ill
]
of
\mathcal{X}
,
\mathbb{L}^{p}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\bigwedge^{p}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{X}}:=\operatorname{Sym}^{p}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{X}}[1]])[-p]
denotes the derived wedge product of
\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{X}}
, and
\operatorname{ch}_{\mathcal{X}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
denotes the absolute Chern character defined as the trace of the exponential of the absolute Atiyah class
\operatorname{At}_{\mathcal{X}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}):I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\to I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{X}}[1]

[
Ill
]
.

Under the HKR ismorphism, the Chern character
\operatorname{ch}_{\mathcal{X}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
can be identified with the Dennis trace
\operatorname{ch}^{D}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
.
More precisely, we have a commutative diagram

(47)

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ch}^{D}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ch}_{\mathcal{X}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})}
\textstyle{L\widetilde{\Delta}^{*}\widetilde{\Delta}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{p}\mathbb{L}^{p}_{\mathcal{X}}[p]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{L\Delta^{*}\Delta_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{HKR}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{p}\Omega^{p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}[p]}

in the derived category of
\mathcal{X}
where
\widetilde{\Delta}:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}
and

are the diagonal maps.
The horizontal isomorphisms are given by the exponentials of the universal Atiyah classes as in Buchweitz-Flenner
[

, Thm. 6.2.1]
(see Markarian
[

The hypercohomology of the left column of (

) computes the Hochschild homology,

\mathbb{H}^{i}(\mathcal{X},L\widetilde{\Delta}^{*}\widetilde{\Delta}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})\cong\mathsf{HH}^{i}(\mathcal{X}),\qquad\mathbb{H}^{i}(\mathcal{X},L\Delta^{*}\Delta_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})\cong\mathsf{HH}^{i}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})

by Yekutieli
[

, Prop. 3.3]
.
The lower horizontal isomorphism in (
47
)
is compatible with the corresponding isomorphism in (
45
),
by the arguments in
[
Cal
, Prop. 4.4]
since
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
is smooth.
The map
\operatorname{ch}^{D}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
in (

) is the Euler map of

in the sense of
[
BNT
, 4.3]
, which agrees with the Dennis trace map for the dg category
\mathrm{Perf}(\mathcal{X})

[
McC
]
, as desribed in
[
BNT
, 4.5, 4.7]
.

As explained in
[

, 3.8]
, the Dennis trace map

can be extended to the Goodwillie Chern character

[

]
via descent
[
WG
]
,

(48)

\textstyle{K^{0}(\mathcal{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ch}^{D}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{ch}^{G}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{HH}^{0}(\mathcal{X})}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathsf{HN}^{0}(\mathcal{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{HP}^{0}(\mathcal{X}).}

Consider the canonical commutative diagram

(49)

\textstyle{\mathsf{HH}^{0}(\mathcal{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{HN}^{0}(\mathcal{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{HP}^{0}(\mathcal{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{HH}^{0}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

\textstyle{\mathsf{HN}^{0}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{HKR}}
\textstyle{\mathsf{HP}^{0}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

\textstyle{\bigoplus_{p}R^{p}f_{*}\Omega^{p}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{p}F^{p}H^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{p}H^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}).}

Denote by

(50)

\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}_{p}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\in F^{p}H^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})

the image of
\operatorname{ch}^{G}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})\in HN_{0}(\mathcal{X})
under the vertical maps in (
49
).
We now show that
\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}_{p}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
in (
50
) satisfies the three properties in Lemma
3.33
separately.

(1)

Clearly
\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}_{p}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
maps to the Chern character
\operatorname{ch}_{p}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
in (
44
). This follows from the commutative diagrams (
46
), (
47
), (
48
), and (
49
).

(2)

We now claim that
\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}_{p}}(I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
is horizontal. Indeed, we may assume that
\mathcal{B}=\operatorname{Spec}\,(A)
for a local Artinian
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
A
(Remark
3.31
). Let
X:=\mathcal{X}_{0}
be the fibre over the
\mathbb{C}
-point
0\in\mathcal{B}
.
By the Feigin-Tsygan theorem
[
FT
, Thm. 5]
(see also
[
Emm
, Thm. 2.2]
,
[
Wei3
, Thm. 3.4]
), we have a commutative square with bijective horizontal arrows

(51)

\textstyle{\mathsf{HP}^{0}(\mathcal{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{p}\mathbb{H}^{2p}(\widehat{\mathcal{Y}},\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\bullet})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathsf{HP}^{0}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\bigoplus_{p}\mathbb{H}^{2p}(\mathcal{X},\Omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}^{\bullet})}

where
\bigoplus_{p}\mathbb{H}^{2p}(\widehat{\mathcal{Y}},\widehat{\Omega}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{\bullet})
is Hartshorne’s algebraic de Rham cohomology
[
Har
]
given by an embedding
\mathcal{X}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{Y}
into a smooth scheme
\mathcal{Y}
.
Here the lower isomorphism in (

) coincides with the corresponding isomorphism in (

) since they both are given by the same left inverse (
\mu
in
[

]
and

in
[

]
) of the antisymmetrization map.
By
[

, Thm. IV.1.1]
(and
[
