\mathsf{KS}_{\curly P\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\circ\operatorname{At}_{\curly P\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})=0.

We deduce that the big square in (
76
) commutes (up to sign)

(77)

\mathsf{KS}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\circ\operatorname{At}_{\curly P\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{X}/\curly P}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})=-\mathsf{KS}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}\circ\operatorname{At}_{\curly P\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{X}}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}).

The commutativity of (
75
) follows from (
77
) by adjunction.
∎

Remark 4.22
.

The main limitation of the algebraic twistor family approach in this subsection is that the reduced obstruction theory is given as a
composition
, but not as a
factorization
,

(78)

\textstyle{\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\mathrm{red}}_{V}}
\scriptstyle{\operatorname{id}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{?}
\textstyle{\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)}.}

In classical reduced theory, e.g.
[
KT1
]
, this was sufficient to construct reduced Behrend-Fantechi virtual cycles
[
BF
]
.
However, in DT4 theory, this is not sufficient to construct reduced Oh-Thomas virtual cycles
[
OT1
]
since we need to know whether the reduced obstruction theory
\phi^{\mathrm{red}}_{V}
satisfies the
isotropic condition

\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\not\exists}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}_{{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
algebraic twistor
\scriptstyle{?}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{V}).}

Thus we additionally need the cone reduction property in the previous subsection (Proposition
4.15
) to construct a reduced virtual cycle from
\phi^{\mathrm{red}}_{V}
.

To conclude, the cone reduction property in Proposition
4.15
and the reduced obstruction theory in Proposition
4.19
give us a commutative diagram

(79)

\textstyle{(\mathfrak{C}_{{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)})_{\mathrm{red}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
cone reduction
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{V})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}_{{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
algebraic twistor
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{V}).}

This proves Theorem
1.7
.

In Appendix
A
, we will construct a diagonal arrow
\mathfrak{C}_{{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{V})
in commutative diagram (
79
) using derived algebraic geometry, see Remark
A.16
.

Remark 4.23
.

By Proposition
4.20
, the standard derived enhancement on
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(\mathcal{X}_{V}/\mathcal{A}_{V})
gives us a
reduced derived enhancement
on
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
.
However, this derived enhancement is not necessarily homotopically finitely presented over
\operatorname{Spec}\,(\mathbb{C})
and does not give us the reduced virtual cycle in Theorem
4.5
.
In Appendix
A
, we will construct a different reduced derived enhancement on
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
that has a
(-2)
-shifted symplectic structure and induces the desired reduced virtual cycle.

4.6.
Reduced virtual dimension

In this subsection, we present various methods to compute the number
\rho_{\gamma}
and the reduced virtual dimension.

Local complete intersection surfaces.

Let
X
be a Calabi-Yau 4-fold. We take
i:S\hookrightarrow X
a
local complete intersection
(lci) surface,
v=\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{S})=(0,0,\gamma,\beta,\chi(\mathcal{O}_{S})-\gamma\cdot\mathrm{td}_{2}(X))\in H^{*}(X)
. Our goal is to make the key diagram from Proposition
4.9
more explicit and determine the reduced virtual dimension
\mathrm{rvd}=\chi(\mathcal{O}_{S})-\tfrac{1}{2}\gamma^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\rho_{\gamma}
.
The normal sheaf
N_{S/X}
is locally free because
S
is lci. Since
X
has trivial canonical bundle, we have

K_{S}\cong N_{S/X}\wedge N_{S/X}

and the Serre duality pairing gives
a non-degenerate symmetric bilinear pairing

\mathsf{SD}_{S}:H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})\cong H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})^{\vee}.

The space
H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})
maps to the obstruction space of
I_{S/X}
. From the short exact sequence

(80)

0\to I_{S/X}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}\to 0\,,

we get an exact triangle

R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I_{S/X},\mathcal{O}_{S})\to R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I_{S/X},I_{S/X})[1]\to R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I_{S/X},\mathcal{O}_{X})[1]\,.

By taking cones of maps from
R\Gamma(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})[1]
to the second and the third factor, we get an exact triangle

(81)

R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I_{S/X},\mathcal{O}_{S})\to R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I_{S/X},I_{S/X})_{0}[1]\to R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{X})[2]\,.

Consider the map

(82)

\theta_{S}:H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(I_{S/X},\mathcal{O}_{S})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(I_{S/X},I_{S/X})_{0}\,,

where the first map is defined by adjunction.

We give local descriptions of the obstruction map (
60
) and the semi-regularity map (
59
) for local complete intersection surfaces.
The map defined by

\mathsf{ob}_{S/X}:H^{1}(X,T_{X})\to H^{1}(S,T_{X}|_{S})\to H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})

is the obstruction map for deforming
S\subset X
along a deformation of
X

[
Blo
, Prop. 2.6]
. Let
d_{S}:\mathcal{O}_{S}\to I_{S/X}[1]
be the map induced by (
80
). Let
\alpha:\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(I_{S/X},\mathcal{O}_{S})\to H^{3}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})
be the map defined by the trace of

I_{S/X}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}[1]\xrightarrow{\operatorname{At}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}\mathcal{O}_{S}\otimes\Omega_{X}^{1}[2]\xrightarrow{d_{S}\otimes\operatorname{id}}I_{S/X}\otimes\Omega_{X}^{1}[3]\,.

The local semi-regularity map is defined by

(83)

\mathsf{sr}_{S/X}:H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})\to\operatorname{Ext}_{X}^{1}(I_{S/X},\mathcal{O}_{S})\xrightarrow{\alpha}H^{3}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1}).

The main result of this subsection consists of the following compatibilities.

Proposition 4.24
.

Let
i:S\hookrightarrow X
be a local complete intersection surface inside a Calabi-Yau 4-fold
X
.

(1)

The map (
82
) is an isomorphism between two vector spaces with non-degenerate symmetric bilinear forms

\theta_{S}:\left(H^{1}(S,N_{S/X}),\mathsf{SD}_{S}\right)\xrightarrow{\cong}\left(\operatorname{Ext}_{X}^{2}(I_{S/X},I_{S/X})_{0},\mathsf{Y}\right).

(2)

The following diagram commutes

\textstyle{H^{1}(X,T_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{ob}_{S/X}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{ob}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sr}_{S/X}}
\scriptstyle{\theta_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{(2^{\prime})}
\scriptstyle{(2^{\prime\prime})}
\textstyle{H^{3}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1}).}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(I_{S/X},I_{S/X})_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{sr}}

Proof.

To simplify the notation, denote
I:=I_{S/X}
. We first prove that the map
\theta_{S}
is an isomorphism. The compatibility of the two bilinear forms will be proved at the end. The exact triangle (
81
) gives a long exact sequence

(84)

\displaystyle\begin{split}0&\to\underbrace{\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{X})}_{\cong H^{0}(S,K_{S})}\xrightarrow{\textup{(i)}}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})\xrightarrow{\textup{(ii)}}\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(I,I)_{0}\\
&\to\underbrace{\operatorname{Ext}^{3}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{X})}_{\cong H^{1}(S,K_{S})}\xrightarrow{\textup{(iii)}}\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})\xrightarrow{\textup{(iv)}}\operatorname{Ext}^{3}_{X}(I,I)_{0}\\
&\to\operatorname{Ext}^{4}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{X})\xrightarrow{\textup{(v)}}\operatorname{Ext}^{3}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})\to 0\end{split}

since
\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(I,I)_{0}
is an isomorphism (Theorem
3.19
).
The map (v) is Serre dual to

\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},I)\xrightarrow{\textup{(v')}}\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathcal{O}_{S}).

Applying
\curly Ext^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},-)
to (
80
), we have

\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},I)=0,\quad\curly Ext^{1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},I)\cong\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{S})

since
\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{X})=\curly Ext^{1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{X})=0
. Therefore
(v’) can be identified with the map induced by the isomorphism
\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{S})\cong\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{X},\mathcal{O}_{S})
and (iv) is surjective.

Consider the local-to-global spectral sequence

H^{p}(X,\curly Ext^{q}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S}))\Rightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S}).

Since
S
is lci, there are isomorphisms

\curly Ext^{q}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})\cong\curly Ext^{q+1}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{S})\cong\bigwedge^{q+1}N_{S/X},\quad\forall q\geq 0

by
[
SGA6
, Prop.VII.2.5]
.
Since
\curly Ext^{q}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})=0
, for all
q\geq 2
, the local-to-global spectral sequence collapses to
the long exact sequence

(85)

\displaystyle\begin{split}0&\to H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})\xrightarrow{}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})\xrightarrow{(a)}\underbrace{H^{0}(S,N_{S/X}\wedge N_{S/X})}_{H^{0}(S,K_{S})}\\
&\to H^{2}(S,N_{S/X})\xrightarrow{}\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})\xrightarrow{(b)}\underbrace{H^{1}(S,N_{S/X}\wedge N_{S/X})}_{H^{1}(S,K_{S})}\to 0.\end{split}

Combined with (
84
), we obtain morphisms

\displaystyle H^{0}(S,K_{S})\xrightarrow{\textup{(i)}}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})\xrightarrow{(a)}H^{0}(S,K_{S}),

\displaystyle H^{1}(S,K_{S})\xrightarrow{\textup{(iii)}}\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})\xrightarrow{(b)}H^{1}(S,K_{S}).

We show that the composition is an isomorphism. Since
\mathcal{O}_{S}
is Cohen-Macaulay, we have
\curly Ext^{q}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{X})=0
, for
q=3,4
(Lemma
2.6
). Therefore we have
H^{p}(X,\curly Ext^{2}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{X}))\cong\operatorname{Ext}^{p+2}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{X})
for all
p
. By functoriality of the local-to-global spectral sequence, the map
(a)\circ(i)
is induced by

\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}_{X}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{X})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
(i)
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(a)}
\textstyle{H^{0}(X,\curly Ext^{2}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{X}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{(\star)}
\textstyle{H^{0}(X,\curly Ext^{2}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{S}))}

where the map
(\star)
is induced by applying
\curly Ext^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},-)
to (
80
). By the fundamental local isomorphism,
(\star)
is induced by

\curly Ext_{X}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{X})\cong K_{S}\otimes i^{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}\cong\curly Ext^{2}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{S})\,.

Hence the composition is an isomorphism. Similar arguments show that the second composition is also an isomorphism. Consequently, (
84
) and (
85
) split into two short exact sequences and hence the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{S}:H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(I,I)_{0}}
(ii)

is an isomorphism.

The commutativity
(2^{\prime\prime})
follows from the compatibility of
\operatorname{At}(I)
and
\operatorname{At}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
along
d_{S}
. To prove the commutativity
(2^{\prime})
, we first show that the following diagram commutes

(86)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(X,T_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(S,N_{S/X}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{ob}_{S/X}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{ob}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{S}^{\prime}}

Here the map
\theta_{S}^{\prime}
is obtained similarly to (
82
). Consider the composition

\theta_{S}^{\prime\prime}:H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})\xrightarrow{\cong}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{S}(I/I^{2},\mathcal{O}_{S})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(i_{*}I/I^{2},i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{S})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{S})\,,

where the second map is the pushforward and the third map is induced by the exact sequence

(87)

0\to I/I^{2}\to\mathcal{O}_{X}/I^{2}\to\mathcal{O}_{S}\to 0\,.

The map
\theta_{S}^{\prime}
can be identified with
\theta^{\prime\prime}_{S}
by comparing (
80
) and (
87
).
Therefore the commutativity of (
86
) follows from the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces I/I^{2}[1]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{S}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}[1]\,,}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{At}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}

where
e_{S}
is induced from (
87
) and
\delta
is defined by the conormal sequence. By the naturality of Atiyah classes, the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(X,T_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(I,I)_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{3}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},I)\,.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{ob}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{ob}(\mathcal{O}_{S})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{S}}

The right vertical map is injective because it is dual to (ii). Therefore if suffices to check
(2^{\prime})
after post-composing with
d_{S}
. Since (
86
) commutes, this follows from the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(I,I)_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{3}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},I).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{S}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d_{S}}

Finally we prove that the map
\theta_{S}
preserves pairings. We first show that
\theta_{S}^{\prime}
preserves pairings. Let
i^{!}
be the right adjoint of
i_{*}
. Then
\theta_{S}^{\prime}
decomposes as

\displaystyle\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{S}(I/I^{2},\mathcal{O}_{S})\to\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{S}(I/I^{2},i^{!}i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{S})\xrightarrow{\cong}\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(i_{*}I/I^{2},i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{S})\xrightarrow{e_{S}}\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{S},i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{S})

where the first map is the adjoint, the second map is Grothendieck duality, and the third map is induced by (
87
). By the functoriality of Serre duality, we are reduced to checking commutativity of the diagram

\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{S}(I/I^{2},i^{!}i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{SD}_{S}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(i_{*}I/I^{2},i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{S})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{SD}_{X}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{3}_{S}(Li^{*}i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{S},I/I^{2})^{\vee}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Ext}^{3}_{X}(i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{S},i_{*}I/I^{2})^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}

where the bottom isomorphism is induced by the adjunction
Li^{*}\dashv i_{*}
.
Since
S
is lci, we have
i^{!}=\mathsf{SD}_{S}\circ Li^{*}\circ\mathsf{SD}_{X}^{-1}
. Therefore the above diagram commutes and
\theta_{S}^{\prime}
preserves pairings.
The pullbacks of quadratic forms on
\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(I,I)_{0}
and
\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{S})
to
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})
coincide because
\operatorname{tr}(d_{S}\circ u\circ d_{S}\circ u)=\operatorname{tr}(u\circ d_{S}\circ u\circ d_{S})
for
u\in\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})
. Since
\theta_{S},\theta_{S}^{\prime}
both factor through
\operatorname{Ext}^{1}_{X}(I,\mathcal{O}_{S})
, the compatibility of quadratic forms under pullback along
\theta_{S}
follows from compatibility under pullback along
\theta_{S}^{\prime}
.
∎

Remark 4.25
.

The map (
83
) coincides with Bloch’s semi-regularity map
[
BuFl1
, Prop. 8.2]
. Furthermore, we have a map

\mathsf{sr}^{\prime}=\operatorname{tr}(\operatorname{At}(\mathcal{O}_{S})\circ-):\operatorname{Ext}_{X}^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{S})\to H^{3}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1}).

By Proposition
4.24
(2^{\prime\prime})
,
compatibility of Atiyah classes, and cyclicity of trace, the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Ext}^{2}_{X}(\mathcal{O}_{S},\mathcal{O}_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{3}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{sr}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\theta_{S}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{sr}_{S/X}}

The following simple observation is useful for computing the reduced virtual dimension.

Remark 4.26
.

Let
J
be a complex invertible symmetric
m\times m
matrix. In general, a complex
m\times n
matrix
A
does not satisfy
\operatorname{rk}(A^{T}JA)=\operatorname{rk}(A)
. This equality holds if and only if
J
is non-degenerate on
\operatorname{im}(A)
.

We now compute the reduced virtual dimension when
v=\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
for some lci surfaces.

Corollary 4.27
.

Let
i:S\hookrightarrow X
be a local complete intersection surface inside a Calabi-Yau 4-fold
X
. If
v=\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
then

\mathrm{rvd}=\frac{1}{2}\chi(N_{S/X})+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{rk}(\mathsf{ob}_{S/X}^{\vee}\circ\mathsf{SD}_{S}\circ\mathsf{ob}_{S/X}).

In particular when
\mathsf{ob}_{S/X}
is surjective, i.e.
S
is semi-regular, we have

\mathrm{rvd}=h^{0}(S,N_{S/X}).

Proof.

By the Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch theorem we have

\chi(N_{S/X})=\int_{S}\operatorname{ch}(N_{S/X})\,\mathrm{td}(S)=2\chi(\mathcal{O}_{S})-\int_{S}c_{2}(N_{S/X})=2\chi(\mathcal{O}_{S})-[S]^{2},

where we used
c_{1}(N_{S/X})=c_{1}(\bigwedge^{2}N_{S/X})=c_{1}(K_{S})
and the self-intersection formula
i^{*}i_{*}[S]=c_{2}(N_{S/X})
. Thus the formula for the reduced virtual dimension follows from Proposition
4.24
. In the semi-regular case, we have
\mathrm{rk}(\mathsf{ob}_{S/X}^{\vee}\circ\mathsf{SD}_{S}\circ\mathsf{ob}_{S/X})=\mathrm{rk}(\mathsf{ob}_{S/X})=h^{1}(N_{S/X})
(Remark
4.26
). The result follows from the fact that
h^{0}(N_{S/X})=h^{2}(N_{S/X})
(see also Theorem
4.6
).
∎

Complete intersection Calabi-Yau 4-folds

The following result is inspired by Steenbrink
[
Ste
]
.

Corollary 4.28
.

Let
X\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}
be a complete intersection Calabi-Yau 4-fold defined by a regular section of
E_{1}=\oplus_{i=1}^{N-4}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}(e_{i})
,
e_{i}\geq 2
. Suppose
S\subset X
is a complete intersection surface of
\mathbb{P}^{N}
defined by a regular section of
E_{2}=\oplus_{j=1}^{N-2}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}(d_{j})
,
d_{j}\geq 1
. Then the ideal sheaf
I_{S/X}
is semi-regular.
Moreover if
v=\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
, then

\mathrm{rvd}=\dim(\ker(H^{0}(E_{2}|_{S})\to H^{0}(E_{1}|_{S}))

where the map is induced by the morphism
E_{2}|_{S}=N_{S/\mathbb{P}^{N}}\to N_{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}|_{S}=E_{1}|_{S}
.

Proof.

By the short exact sequence

(88)

0\to N_{S/X}\to N_{S/\mathbb{P}^{N}}\to N_{X/\mathbb{P}^{N}}|_{S}\to 0

and Proposition
4.24
, it is enough to show that
\mathsf{ob}_{S/X}
is surjective.
We note that the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(\mathbb{P}^{N},E_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(S,E_{1}|_{S})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(S,N_{S/X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(X,E_{1}|_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(X,T_{X}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{ob}_{S/X}}

Therefore it suffices to show that two top horizontal maps are surjective. Consider a Koszul resolution
\bigwedge^{\bullet+1}E_{2}^{\vee}\to I_{S/\mathbb{P}^{N}}
and the associated spectral sequence
[
Sta
, Tag 07A9]

\operatorname{Ext}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}^{p}(E_{1}\otimes\bigwedge^{q+1}E_{2}^{\vee},K_{\mathbb{P}^{N}})\Rightarrow\operatorname{Ext}^{p+q}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}(E_{1}\otimes I_{S/\mathbb{P}^{N}},K_{\mathbb{P}^{N}})\,.

Since, on line bundles,
H^{p}(\mathbb{P}^{N},-)
is nonzero only when
p=0
or
N
, this spectral sequence degenerates and we have
0=\operatorname{Ext}^{N-1}_{\mathbb{P}^{N}}(E_{1}\otimes I_{S/\mathbb{P}^{N}},K_{\mathbb{P}^{N}})\cong H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{N},E_{1}\otimes I_{S/\mathbb{P}^{N}})^{\vee}
. Therefore the first horizontal map is surjective. Similarly we have
H^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{N},E_{2}\otimes I_{S/\mathbb{P}^{N}})=0
.
Consequently
H^{1}(S,E_{2}|_{S})=H^{1}(S,N_{S/\mathbb{P}^{N}})=0
and hence the second horizontal map is surjective.
Therefore
\mathsf{ob}_{S/X}=(\mathsf{sr}_{S/X})^{\vee}
is surjective and
I_{S/X}
is semi-regular.
∎

Corollary
4.28
allows us to compute
\mathrm{rvd}
and
\rho_{\gamma}
for semi-regular cases.

Example 4.29
.

Suppose
X:=X_{e_{1},\ldots,e_{N-4}}\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}
is a complete intersection Calabi-Yau 4-fold. The possible cases are
X_{6}
,
X_{2,5}
,
X_{3,4}
,
X_{2,2,4}
,
X_{2,3,3}
,
X_{2,2,2,3}
,
X_{2,2,2,2,2}
. Consider complete intersection surfaces
S\subset\mathbb{P}^{N}
of type
(d_{1},\ldots,d_{N-2})
such that
S\subset X
. By Corollary
4.28
the ideal sheaf
I_{S/X}
is semi-regular.

(1)

Suppose for all
i
we have

(89)

-d_{i}+\sum_{j=1}^{N-2}d_{j}<N+1.

Then we claim
S\subset X
is rigid, i.e.
h^{0}(N_{S/X})=0
, so in particular
\mathrm{rvd}=0
. This can be seen by showing that
H^{0}(S,N_{S/X})^{\vee}\cong H^{2}(S,N_{S/X})=0
as follows. Using (
88
) it suffices to show that
H^{1}(S,E_{1}|_{S})=H^{2}(S,E_{2}|_{S})=0
. This follows by showing
H^{2}(\mathbb{P}^{N},E_{1}\otimes I_{S/X})=H^{3}(\mathbb{P}^{N},E_{2}\otimes I_{S/X})=0
by a Koszul calculation as in the proof of Corollary
4.28
.
E.g. for
N=5
all solutions to (
89
) are (up to permutations)

(1,1,1),(1,1,2),(1,1,3),(1,2,2);(1,2,3),(2,2,2),(1,1,4);(2,2,3).

In cases 1–4,
S
is del Pezzo. In cases 5–7,
S
is K3. In case 8,
S
is general type. Using the formula
\mathrm{rvd}=\chi(\mathcal{O}_{S})-\tfrac{1}{2}\gamma^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\rho_{\gamma}
, and determining
\chi(\mathcal{O}_{S})
,
\gamma^{2}
in terms of
d_{1},d_{2},d_{3}
, this produces the following values for
\rho_{\gamma}
in the above cases

19,32,37,54;62,92,32;106.

(2)

Suppose
X=X_{6}
is defined by a homogeneous polynomial
f\in R:=\mathbb{C}[x_{0},...,x_{5}]
of degree 6 and
S\subset X
is defined by homogeneous polynomials
(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})
of degrees
(d_{1},d_{2},d_{3})
, then
f=s_{1}t_{1}+s_{2}t_{2}+s_{3}t_{3}
for some
t_{1},t_{2},t_{3}
.
Using the Koszul resolution of
I_{S/\mathbb{P}^{5}}
, as in proof of Corollary
4.28
, it is easy to see that
H^{1}(\mathbb{P}^{5},I_{S/\mathbb{P}^{5}}(n))=0
for all
n
. If the ideal
(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\subset R
is saturated then
H^{0}(S,N_{S/X})
is the kernel of the following map

\displaystyle H^{0}(E_{2}|_{S})\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{3}\Big(R/(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\Big)_{(d_{i})}

\displaystyle\to H^{0}(E_{1}|_{S})\cong\Big(R/(s_{1},s_{2},s_{3})\Big)_{(6)}

\displaystyle(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3})

\displaystyle\mapsto u_{1}t_{1}+u_{2}t_{2}+u_{3}t_{3}

where
(-)_{(n)}
denotes the degree
n
part.
This can be implemented in
Maccaulay2
. E.g. for the Fermat sextic and certain
S
of degrees
(1,3,3),(2,3,3),(3,3,3)
, we obtain
\mathrm{rvd}=h^{0}(N_{S/X})=1,1,3
respectively. Using the formula
\mathrm{rvd}=\chi(\mathcal{O}_{S})-\tfrac{1}{2}\gamma^{2}+\tfrac{1}{2}\rho_{\gamma}
, we obtain that
\rho_{\gamma}
is
71,122,141
respectively.

The value of
\rho_{\gamma}
can also be computed by Griffiths’ residue calculus
[
Gri
]
. In the cases of the sextic 4-fold listed above, our values for
\rho_{\gamma}
reproduce
[
Mov
, Table 17.3]
.
Corollary
4.28
gives an algebraic method for determining
\rho_{\gamma}
.

Holomorphic symplectic 4-folds
When a Calabi-Yau 4-fold
X
has a non-degenerate holomorphic 2-form, the rank
\rho_{\gamma}
of
\mathsf{B}_{\gamma}
is easy to determine.
The following computation is inspired by Voisin
[
Voi1
]
.

Corollary 4.30
.

Suppose
X
has a non-degenerate holomorphic 2-form
\sigma\in H^{0}(X,\Omega_{X}^{2})
. Let
i:S\hookrightarrow X
be a (possibly disconnected) smooth lagrangian surface, i.e.,
i^{*}\sigma=0
. If
v=\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
then

\mathrm{rvd}=h^{1,0}(S)-\frac{1}{2}\dim\operatorname{coker}(i^{*})

where
i^{*}:H^{1}(X,\Omega_{X}^{1})\to H^{1}(S,\Omega_{S}^{1})
.

Proof.

We prove the case when
S
is connected. The disconnected case then follows easily.
Since
S
is a lagrangian, we have an isomorphism

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N_{S/X}^{*}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{X}|_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sigma}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\Omega_{S}^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{T_{X}|_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N_{S/X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0.}

Therefore
\mathsf{ob}_{S/X}
can be identified with
i^{*}
.
Under this identification,
\mathsf{SD}_{S}
corresponds to the cup product. By the Hodge index theorem, the cup product is non-degenerate on the image of
i^{*}
. Therefore Remark
4.26
and Corollary
4.27
imply that the reduced virtual dimension equals

\mathrm{rvd}=\frac{1}{2}\chi(\Omega^{1}_{S})+\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{rk}(i^{*}).\qed

Example 4.31
.

Suppose
X
has a non-degenerate holomorphic 2-form. Consider a lagrangian plane
\mathbb{P}^{2}\cong S\subset X
and let
v=\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
. Since
i^{*}
is non-trivial and
h^{1,1}(S)=1
,
S
is semi-regular. Moreover,
N_{S/X}\cong\Omega_{S}^{1}
implies that
S
is rigid and
\mathrm{rvd}=0
. When
\curly P_{v}^{(q)}(X)
only contains lagrangian planes,
\deg([\curly P_{v}^{(q)}(X)]^{\mathrm{red}})
is a (signed) count of lagrangian planes in
X
in class
v
.

Example 4.32
.

In
[
Sch
]
Schoen constructed minimal surfaces
S
of general type related to the Hodge conjecture for abelian 4-folds. These surfaces satisfy
h^{1,0}(S)=4
,
h^{2,0}(S)=5
, and
h^{1,1}(S)=12
.
Let
S
be a Schoen surface, then its Albanese variety
X=\mathrm{Alb}(S)
is an abelian 4-fold. The Albanese map
i:S\to X
is an embedding of
S
as a lagrangian surface (for some non-degenerate
\sigma\in H^{0}(X,\Omega_{X}^{2})
)
[
CMLR
]
, and the cohomology class
[S]\in H^{2,2}(X)\cap H^{4}(X,\mathbb{Q})
is not the product of divisor classes on
X

[
Sch
]
. Let
v=\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{S})
. From the constructions in
[
Sch
]
, one can see that there exist semi-regular Schoen surfaces
S\subset X
. Then
i^{*}
is surjective.
Therefore
\mathrm{rvd}=4
and
\rho_{[S]}=12
.
We revisit this example in future work.

Remark 4.33
.

Combining Theorem
4.6
and Corollary
4.30
, we obtain a numerical criterion for a smooth lagrangian surface
i:S\hookrightarrow X
to be non-semi-regular. Consider
i^{*}:H^{1,1}(X)\to H^{1,1}(S)
. Indeed if
\operatorname{rk}(i^{*})
is
odd
or
b_{1}(S)<\dim\operatorname{coker}(i^{*})
, then
S
is not semi-regular.

For example, let
\pi:X\to B
be a hyperkähler 4-fold with lagrangian fibration over a smooth base.
The general fibre
S=F
is a lagrangian abelian surface and the base
B
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{P}^{2}
(
[
Hwa
,
HX
]
). For
v=\operatorname{ch}(\mathcal{O}_{F})
and any
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
, we have a morphism

B\to{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X),\quad P\mapsto\pi^{*}I_{P/B}.

This is a bijection on
\mathbb{C}
-points and using the fact that
R\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{B}\oplus\Omega_{B}^{1}[-1]\oplus\Omega_{B}^{2}[-2]

[
Mat1
]
, one can also show that it is an isomorphism on tangent spaces at
\mathbb{C}
-points. Thus, since
B
is smooth, the map is an isomorphism.
Let
\Delta\subset B\times B
denote the diagonal and
\pi_{B}:X\times B\to B
projection.
Using again
R\pi_{*}\mathcal{O}_{X}\cong\mathcal{O}_{B}\oplus\Omega_{B}^{1}[-1]\oplus\Omega_{B}^{2}[-2]
, we obtain the following obstruction bundle

\curly Ext^{2}_{\pi_{B}}(\pi^{*}I_{\Delta},\pi^{*}I_{\Delta})_{0}\cong T_{B}\otimes\Omega_{B}^{1}\cong\mathcal{E}{\it{nd}}(T_{B}).

The natural pairing
T_{B}\otimes\Omega_{B}^{1}\to\mathcal{O}_{B}
is the unique cosection (up to scaling). Since
i^{*}
is non-zero, we deduce
\operatorname{rk}i^{*}=1
. A direct computation of
\operatorname{rk}i^{*}=1
can be found in
[
SY
,
Mat2
]
. We deduce
\mathrm{rvd}=\tfrac{1}{2}
so
S
is not semi-regular.

Example 4.34
.

Let
C\subset\mathrm{K3}
be a smooth curve of genus
g
on a K3 surface. Then the Hilbert square
X=\mathrm{K3}^{[2]}
is a hyperkähler 4-fold and the symmetric product
S=C^{[2]}
is a smooth lagrangian surface. Since
S
is cut out transversally by a tautological section of the vector bundle
\mathcal{O}(C)^{[2]}
, we have

\chi(\Omega^{1}_{S})=\int_{S}\operatorname{ch}(\Omega^{1}_{S})\cdot\mathrm{td}(S)=\int_{X}c_{2}(\mathcal{O}(C)^{[2]})\operatorname{ch}(\Omega^{1}_{X}-\mathcal{O}(C)^{[2]\vee})\,\frac{\mathrm{td}(X)}{\mathrm{td}(\mathcal{O}(C)^{[2]})}.

This tautological integral can be easily determined e.g. using
[
EGL
]
. The result is
-(C^{2})^{2}/4=-(g-1)^{2}
. Moreover,
\operatorname{rk}i^{*}=1
when
g=0
and
\operatorname{rk}i^{*}=2
otherwise (
[
Voi1
, Sect. 3]
).
By Corollary
4.30
we find

\mathrm{rvd}=-\frac{1}{2}(g-1)^{2}+\left\{\begin{array}[]{cc}\frac{1}{2}&\textrm{if}\ g=0\\
1&\textrm{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

This number is only non-zero for
g=0,1,2
, then
\mathrm{rvd}=0,1,\tfrac{1}{2}
respectively. In particular, for
g\geq 2
we deduce that
S
is not semi-regular (Remark
4.33
). For
g=0
,
S\cong\mathbb{P}^{2}
is a lagrangian plane.

When
X
is a product of two
K3
surfaces, we determine
\rho_{\gamma}
for diagonal classes, see also Example
5.16
.

Proposition 4.35
.

Let
X
be a product of two K3 surfaces
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
. If
\gamma=\eta_{1}\cup\eta_{2}
for some non-zero
\eta_{1}\in H^{1,1}(S_{1})
and
\eta_{2}\in H^{1,1}(S_{2})
, then we have
\rho_{\gamma}=2
.

Proof.

Let
\sigma_{1}\in H^{2,0}(S_{1})
and
\sigma_{2}\in H^{2,0}(S_{2})
be two nowhere vanishing holomorphic 2-forms. Take
\omega=\sigma_{1}\cup\sigma_{2}\in H^{0}(X,\Omega_{X}^{4})
. Consider the isomorphism

H^{1}(X,T_{X})\cong H^{1}(S_{1},T_{S_{1}})\oplus H^{1}(S_{2},T_{S_{2}}).

The symmetric bilinear form
\mathsf{B}_{\gamma}
on
H^{1}(X,T_{X})
can be related to the two rank one maps

\mathsf{A_{\ell}}:H^{1}(S_{\ell},T_{S_{\ell}})\to\mathbb{C},\,\quad\xi\mapsto\int_{S_{\ell}}\eta_{\ell}\cup\iota_{\xi}(\sigma_{\ell})=-\int_{S_{\ell}}\iota_{\xi}(\eta_{\ell})\cup\sigma_{\ell}\,,\quad\ell=0,1\,.

Namely, if
\xi_{1}\in H^{1}(S_{1},T_{S_{1}})
and
\xi_{2}\in H^{1}(S_{2},T_{S_{2}})
, then

\displaystyle\mathsf{B}_{\gamma}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{X}\iota_{\xi_{1}}\iota_{\xi_{2}}(\eta_{1}\cup\eta_{2})\cup\sigma_{1}\cup\sigma_{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{S_{1}}\eta_{1}\cup\iota_{\xi_{1}}(\sigma_{1})\cdot\int_{S_{2}}\eta_{2}\cup\iota_{\xi_{2}}(\sigma_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathsf{A_{1}}(\xi_{1})\cdot\mathsf{A_{2}}(\xi_{2})

and for
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in H^{1}(S_{1},T_{S_{1}})
or
\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in H^{1}(S_{2},T_{S_{2}})
, we have
\mathsf{B}_{\gamma}(\xi_{1},\xi_{2})=0
. Therefore the rank of
\mathsf{B}_{\gamma}
is two.
∎

Vanishing of
\rho_{\gamma}
.

Let
X
be an arbitrary Calabi-Yau 4-fold and
\gamma\in H^{2,2}(X)
.
There are interesting cases for which
\rho_{\gamma}=0
and hence the non-reduced virtual cycle equals the reduced virtual cycle.

Proposition 4.36
.

Assume that
H^{2}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})=0
. If
\gamma=\eta_{1}\cup\eta_{2}
for some
(1,1)
-classes
\eta_{1}
and
\eta_{2}
on
X
, then we have
\rho_{\gamma}=0
.

Proof.

For
\xi\in H^{1}(X,T_{X})
, we have

\iota_{\xi}(\eta_{1}\cup\eta_{2})=\iota_{\xi}(\eta_{1})\cup\eta_{2}+\eta_{1}\cup\iota_{\xi}(\eta_{2})=0

because
\iota_{\xi}(\eta_{1}),\iota_{\xi}(\eta_{2})\in H^{2}(X,\mathcal{O}_{X})
. Hence
\rho_{\gamma}=0
by definition.
∎

Proposition 4.37
.

Let
Y\subset X
be a smooth divisor with
H^{2}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y})=0
. If
\gamma=[S]
for some effective divisor
S\subset Y
with
H^{1}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}(S))=0
, then
\rho_{\gamma}=0
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.24
it is enough to show that
\operatorname{ob}_{S/X}
is zero. Consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(T_{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(N_{S/Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{ob}_{S/X}:H^{1}(T_{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(T_{X}|_{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(N_{S/X})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{ob}_{S/Y}}

where the left vertical map is surjective by the vanishing of
H^{1}(N_{Y/X})\cong H^{1}(\mathcal{O}_{Y}(Y))\cong H^{1}(K_{Y})\cong H^{2}(\mathcal{O}_{Y})^{\vee}
. By the vanishing of
H^{1}(Y,\mathcal{O}_{Y}(S))
, we have
H^{1}(N_{S/Y})=0
, therefore
\operatorname{ob}_{S/X}=0
.
∎

For example, the hypotheses of the previous proposition are satisfied for
\mathbb{P}^{3}\cong Y\subset X
and any effective divisor
S\subset Y
.

5.
Deformation invariance

In this section, we prove the deformation invariance of reduced virtual cycles.
As a corollary, we prove the variational Hodge conjecture for surface classes that carry non-zero reduced virtual cycles.

5.1.
Deformation invariance

In this subsection, we prove that the reduced virtual cycles on the moduli spaces of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs are deformation invariant along the Hodge loci.

We first fix some notation.
Let

f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}

be a morphism of schemes satisfying the following conditions:

(1)

f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
is a smooth projective morphism of relative dimension
4
with connected fibres;

(2)

\mathcal{B}
is a smooth connected affine scheme;

(3)

\omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}
is trivial, i.e., there exists an isomorphism
w:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\cong\omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}
.

We denote by
\mathcal{X}_{b}:=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\{b\}
the fibre of
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
over a point
b\in\mathcal{B}
.

Choose a horizontal section

\widetilde{v}=(\widetilde{v}_{p})_{p\geq 0}\in\bigoplus_{p\geq 0}F^{p}H^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})

in the sense Definition
3.30
.
We may regard
\widetilde{v}
as a locally constant family of cohomology classes

\widetilde{v}_{b}\in H^{*}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathbb{C})
by Remark
3.31
.
Let

\widetilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{B},R^{2}f_{*}\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})

be the family of
(2,2)
-classes induced by
\widetilde{v}_{2}\in F^{2}H^{4}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
, see (
43
).

Consider a vector bundle

\mathcal{T}:=R^{1}f_{*}(T_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})

on
\mathcal{B}
with a canonical symmetric bilinear form

\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}:\mathcal{T}\otimes\mathcal{T}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}\otimes\widetilde{\gamma}}\mathcal{T}\otimes\mathcal{T}\otimes R^{2}f_{*}(\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})\xrightarrow{}R^{4}f_{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}})\xrightarrow{w}R^{4}f_{*}\Omega^{4}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}.

If the function

b\in\mathcal{B}\mapsto\rho_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}\in\mathbb{Z}

is constant, then we can form an induced orthogonal bundle of rank
\rho:=\rho_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}
as

\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}:={\mathcal{T}}/{\ker(\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}})}.

Let
\curly P:={\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
be the moduli space of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs on the fibres of
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
, defined in Theorem
3.32
. We can form a fibre diagram

(90)

\textstyle{\curly P_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{b}}
\textstyle{\curly P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\{b\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{b}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}}

where
\curly P_{b}:={\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}_{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
is the moduli space of
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pairs on the fibre
\mathcal{X}_{b}
.
By Theorem
3.32
(2) and
[
HT
]
, we have a relative symmetric obstruction theory

(91)

\phi:\mathbb{E}\to\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}

for
p:\curly P\to\mathcal{B}
. Moreover, the induced map

\phi_{b}:\mathbb{E}_{b}:=Lj_{b}^{*}\mathbb{E}\xrightarrow{Lj_{b}^{*}\phi}Lj_{b}^{*}(\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}})\to\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\curly P_{b}}

is the standard symmetric obstruction theory for the fibre
\mathcal{X}_{b}
in (
52
).

Now we can state our main theorem in this section.

Theorem 5.1
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a smooth projective morphism of relative dimension
4
with connected fibres to a smooth connected affine scheme
\mathcal{B}
such that
\omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
. Let
\widetilde{v}\in\bigoplus_{p}F^{p}H^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
be a horizontal section,
\widetilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{B},R^{2}f_{*}\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})
the section induced by
\widetilde{v}_{2}
, and
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
.
Assume that the function
b\in\mathcal{B}\mapsto\rho_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}\in\mathbb{Z}
is constant.
For every orientation

o_{1}:\mathcal{O}_{{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})}\cong\det(\mathbb{E}),\qquad o_{2}:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}\cong\det(\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}})

of the symmetric complex
\mathbb{E}
on
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
and the orthogonal bundle
\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
on
\mathcal{B}
, respectively, there exists a cycle class

\left[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\right]^{\mathrm{red}}\in A_{\mathrm{rvd}+\dim(\mathcal{B})}\left({\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\right)

such that

\left[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}_{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})\right]^{\mathrm{red}}=i_{b}^{!}\left[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\right]^{\mathrm{red}}\in A_{\mathrm{rvd}}\left({\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}_{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})\right)

for all
b\in\mathcal{B}
. Here
i_{b}^{!}:A_{*}({\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}))\to A_{*}({\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}_{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{b}))
denotes the refined Gysin pullback
[
Ful
]
with respect to the fibre diagram (
90
) and
[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}_{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})]^{\mathrm{red}}
denotes the reduced virtual cycle in Theorem
4.5
with respect to the orientations induced by
o_{1}
and
o_{2}
.

We briefly explain how we prove Theorem
5.1
.
We use the cosection localization technique of Kiem-Li
[
KL
]
as in subsection
4.4
.
Most of the arguments are just straightforward generalizations of the proof of Theorem
4.5
to the relative setting.
However there is one key difference. Kiem-Li’s cone reduction lemma (cf. Lemma
4.16
) fails in the relative setting.
Thus as in
[
KiPa2
]
, we need to modify the relative obstruction theory/cosection for
\curly P\to\mathcal{B}
to an absolution obstruction theory/cosection for
\curly P
. In general, we need an additional condition on the cosection to do this.
In our case, this additional condition can be deduced from the fact that the Hodge locus of
\widetilde{\gamma}\in H^{4}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
in
\mathcal{B}
is equal to
\mathcal{B}
itself.

Proof of Theorem
5.1
.

Form a fibre diagram

\textstyle{\curly P\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\curly P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}}

where
\pi
and
q
denote the projection maps. Let
\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}
denote the universal complex on
\curly P\times_{B}\mathcal{X}
.

We have a relative version of the cosections in Proposition
4.12
,

\mathsf{SR}:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[1]=R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})_{0}[2]\longrightarrow\mathcal{T}|^{\vee}_{\curly P}.

Indeed, consider the composition

(92)

R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})_{0}[2]\xrightarrow{\operatorname{At}_{\curly P\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{X}/\curly P}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})}R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})[3]\xrightarrow{\operatorname{tr}^{3}}(R^{3}f_{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})|_{\curly P}

where the third trace map is defined as

\operatorname{tr}^{3}:R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}},\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})[3]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\operatorname{tr}}}{{\to}}(Rf_{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}[3])|_{\curly P}\to(R^{3}f_{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})|_{\curly P}.

Here the last arrow uses Deligne’s decomposition theorem
[
Del1
, Thm. 6.1]
,

Rf_{*}\Omega^{i}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\cong\bigoplus_{j}R^{j}f_{*}\Omega^{i}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}[-j],

which replaces footnote
19
in the relative setting.
The map
\mathsf{SR}
is then defined by composing the above map (
92
) with the Serre duality isomorphism
R^{3}f_{*}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\cong(R^{1}f_{*}T_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})^{\vee}=\mathcal{T}^{\vee}
.

Note that we can form a relative version of the commutative triangle (
61
) in Proposition
4.12
, i.e., we have a commutative triangle

(93)

\textstyle{\mathcal{T}|_{\curly P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{SR}^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}|_{\curly P}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{SR}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}|_{\curly P}^{\vee}}

where we identified
\mathbb{E}[-1]\cong\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[1]
via the symmetric form of
\mathbb{E}
.
Here we used that

\operatorname{ch}_{2}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})=p^{*}(\widetilde{\gamma})\in\Gamma(\curly P,R^{2}\pi_{*}(\Omega^{2}_{\curly P\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{X}/\curly P}))\cong\Gamma(\curly P,p^{*}(R^{2}f_{*}(\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})))

since both sections extend to horizontal sections
\widetilde{\operatorname{ch}_{2}}(\mathbb{I}^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}})
and
p^{*}(\widetilde{v}_{2})
of the vector bundle
F^{2}\mathcal{H}^{4}_{DR}(\curly P\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{X}/\curly P)
by Lemma
3.33
(see our convention (
44
)). Then the commutativity of (
93
) follows from
[
BuFl1
, Prop. 4.2]
as in the proof of Proposition
4.12
.

Since
\mathcal{B}
is affine, we can find a subbundle
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{T}
such that the composition

(94)

\textstyle{\mathcal{V}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}}

is an isomorphism. Then the restriction

\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}|_{\mathcal{V}}:\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathcal{V}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}

is non-degenerate. Thus we may regard
\mathcal{V}
as an orthogonal bundle over
\mathcal{B}
. Moreover, the orientation of
\mathcal{V}
is determined by the orientation
o_{2}
of
\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
via the isomorphism
\mathcal{V}\cong\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
in (
94
).
We may view
\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{T}
as a family of maximal non-degenerate subspaces of
H^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{b},T_{\mathcal{X}_{b}})
.

As in Definition
4.14
, the composition
\mathsf{SR}_{\mathcal{V}}:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[1]\xrightarrow{\mathsf{SR}}\mathcal{T}|_{\curly P}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{V}|_{\curly P}^{\vee}
induces a direct sum decomposition of symmetric complexes

\mathbb{E}=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}\oplus\mathcal{V}|_{\curly P}[1].

More precisely, we let
\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}:=\mathrm{cone}(\mathsf{SR}^{\vee}_{\mathcal{V}}[1])
and consider the symmetric form induced by the symmetric form of
\mathbb{E}
.

Note that the relative symmetric obstruction theory
\phi:\mathbb{E}\to\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}
in (
91
) satisfies the isotropic condition. More precisely, we have

\mathfrak{C}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}\subseteq\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E})

as substacks of
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E})
.
Indeed, this follows from the relative version of the Darboux theorem
[
BBJ
,
BG
]
for the relative
(-2)
-shifted symplectic form for
p:\curly P\to\mathcal{B}
in
[
PTVV
]
since
\widetilde{\gamma}\in F^{2}H_{DR}^{4}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
. See
[
Par2
]
and Remark
5.2
below.

We claim that the relative cone reduction property holds for
p:\curly P\to\mathcal{B}
. More precisely, we claim that we can form a commutative diagram

(95)

\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\curly P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{C}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}})_{\mathrm{red}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\mathsf{SR}_{\mathcal{V}}^{\vee}[1])}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}|_{\curly P}^{\vee}}

for some dotted arrow, where the embedding
(\mathfrak{C}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}})_{\mathrm{red}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E})
is given by the obstruction theory
\phi
as in the previous paragraph.

We first modify the relative obstruction theory
\phi:\mathbb{E}\to\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}
into an absolute one. Indeed, we can form a morphism

(96)

\textstyle{\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{abs}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m}
\scriptstyle{\phi^{\mathrm{abs}}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}|_{\curly P}[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\curly P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{KS}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}|_{\curly P}[1]}

of distinguished triangles on
\curly P
, where the lower triangle is exact since
\mathcal{B}
is smooth.
The associated long exact sequence implies that
\phi^{\mathrm{abs}}:\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{abs}}\to\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}P
is an obstruction theory. Moreover, by considering the associated abelian cone stacks, we have a cartesian diagram

(97)

\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{C}(m)}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}_{\curly P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{abs}})}

where the horizontal arrows are closed embeddings given by the obstruction theories
\phi
,
\phi^{\mathrm{abs}}
, and the vertical arrows are
T_{\mathcal{B}}
-torsors.

We then modify the relative cosection
\mathsf{SR}:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[1]\to\mathcal{T}|_{\curly P}^{\vee}
into an absolute one. We will find a map
\mathsf{SR}^{\mathrm{abs}}:(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{abs}})^{\vee}[1]\to\mathcal{T}|_{\curly P}^{\vee}
that fits into the commutative diagram

(98)

\textstyle{\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{SR}}
\scriptstyle{m^{\vee}[1]}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}|_{\curly P}^{\vee}}
\textstyle{(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{abs}})^{\vee}[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces.}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{SR}^{\mathrm{abs}}}

We need to show that the composition

(99)

\mathcal{T}|_{\curly P}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{SR}^{\vee}}\mathbb{E}[-1]\xrightarrow{\phi[-1]}\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}[-1]\xrightarrow{\mathsf{KS}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}|_{\curly P}

vanishes.

The vanishing of (
99
) is the key part of the proof of Theorem
5.1
.
By the commutative triangle (
93
) and the compatibility result in Lemma
4.21
, we have a commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{T}|_{\curly P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{SR}^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}|_{\curly P}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{SR}}
\scriptstyle{\phi[-1]}
\textstyle{\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{-\mathsf{KS}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}|_{\curly P}^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{KS}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}|_{\curly P}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}|_{\curly P}}

where
\mathsf{KS}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}
denotes the Kodaira-Spencer map. Thus it suffices to show that the composition

\mathcal{T}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}}\mathcal{T}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{KS}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}}\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}

vanishes. Since
\mathcal{B}
is smooth, it suffices to show that the composition

(100)

T_{\mathcal{B},b}\xrightarrow{\mathsf{KS}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},b}}H^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{b},T_{\mathcal{X}_{b}})\xrightarrow{\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}}H^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{b},T_{\mathcal{X}_{b}})^{\vee}

vanishes for all
b\in\mathcal{B}
.
By
[
Blo
, Prop. 4.2]
(see also Lemma
4.11
(1)), the composition (

) indeed vanishes since the horizontal section
\widetilde{v}_{2}
is contained in
F^{2}H^{4}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
.

Now we can apply Kiem-Li’s cone reduction lemma
[
KL
]
for the absolute versions of the obstruction theory
\phi^{\mathrm{abs}}
in (
96
) and cosection
\mathsf{SR}^{\mathrm{abs}}
in (
98
).
Indeed, by Lemma
4.16
, we have a commutative diagram

(101)

\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}((\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{abs}})^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\curly P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{C}_{\curly P})_{\mathrm{red}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{abs}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{l}_{\mathsf{SR}^{\mathrm{abs}}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{T}|_{\curly P}^{\vee}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{V}|_{\curly P}^{\vee}}

for some dotted arrow, where
\mathfrak{l}_{\mathsf{SR}^{\mathrm{abs}}}:=\mathfrak{C}((\mathsf{SR}^{\mathrm{abs}})^{\vee}[1])
and

(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{abs}})^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}:=\mathrm{cone}(\mathcal{V}|_{\curly P}[1]\to\mathcal{T}|_{\curly P}[1]\xrightarrow{(\mathsf{SR}^{\mathrm{abs}})^{\vee}[1]}\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{abs}}).

The existence of the dotted arrow in (
101
) tells us that the lower horizontal composition in (
101
) is zero.
Since the diagrams (
97
) and (
98
) are commutative,
the lower horizontal composition in (
95
) is also zero, which gives us the desired dotted arrow in (
95
).

We define the relative reduced virtual cycle as

[\curly P]^{\mathrm{red}}:=\sqrt{0^{!}_{\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}})}}[\mathfrak{C}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}]\in A_{*}(\curly P),

where the square root Gysin pullback
\sqrt{0^{!}_{\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}})}}
is defined as in the proof of Theorem
4.1
.
By Vistoli’s rational equivalence
[
Vis
, Lem. 3.16]
,
22
22

22

In
[
Vis
]
, it is stated for unramified maps between DM stacks, but the result holds for arbitrary DM type morphism between Artin stacks. This can be deduced from the proof of
[
Kre2
, Prop. 4]
(cf.
[
Qu
, Prop. 2.5]
).

we have

i_{b}^{!}[\mathfrak{C}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}]=[\mathfrak{C}_{\curly P_{b}}]\in A_{*}(\mathfrak{C}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}|_{\curly P_{b}})

where we denote
\curly P_{b}:={\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}_{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
.
Since Oh-Thomas’s localized square root Euler classes
[
OT1
]
are bivariant classes by
[
KiPa2
, Lem. 4.4(3)]
, the square root Gysin pullback
\sqrt{0^{!}_{\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}})}}
commutes with the ordinary Gysin pullback
i^{!}_{b}
.
Therefore, we have

i_{b}^{!}[\curly P]^{\mathrm{red}}=[\curly P_{b}]^{\mathrm{red}},

which completes the proof.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

The relative version of the Darboux theorem
[
BBJ
,
BG
]
works only under an additional assumption.
If
\mathbb{X}
is a derived scheme over a scheme
\mathcal{B}
with a
(-2)
-shifted symplectic form
\theta\in\mathsf{HN}^{-4}(\mathbb{X}/\mathcal{B})(2)\cong\mathcal{A}^{2,\mathrm{cl}}(\mathbb{X}/\mathcal{B};-2)
, we have a global Darboux chart only if
\theta
maps to zero under the canonical map

(102)

\mathsf{HN}^{-4}(\mathbb{X}/\mathcal{B})(2)\to\mathsf{HP}^{-4}(\mathbb{X}/\mathcal{B})(2)

to the periodic cyclic homology.

There is a simple example where there is no Darboux chart for a
(-2)
-shifted symplectic derived scheme. Consider the origin in the affine plane

\mathbb{X}:=\operatorname{Spec}\,(\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]/(t_{1},t_{2}))\subseteq\mathcal{B}:=\operatorname{Spec}\,(\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}])\cong\mathbb{A}^{2}.

Using the quasi-free resolution
(\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}][y_{1},y_{2}],dy_{i}=t_{i})\xrightarrow{qis}\mathbb{C}[t_{1},t_{2}]/(t_{1},t_{2})
, we can find a
(-2)
-symplectic form

\theta:=d_{DR}y_{1}\wedge d_{DR}y_{2}\in\mathsf{HN}^{-4}(\mathbb{X}/\mathcal{B})(2).

The isotropic condition fails in this case since
\mathfrak{C}_{\mathbb{X}/\mathcal{B}}=\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{X}/\mathcal{B}})=\mathbb{A}^{2}
for the induced symmetric obstruction theory
\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{X}/\mathcal{B}}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}}\mathbb{L}_{\mathbb{X}/\mathcal{B}}
.
This implies that there is no Darboux chart.
Moreover, we have
\theta\mapsto t_{1}t_{2}\neq 0\in\mathsf{HP}^{-4}(\mathbb{X}/\mathcal{B})(2)\cong\mathbb{C}[[t_{1},t_{2}]]
under the canonical map (
102
).

However, we still have the desired isotropic condition in the situation of Theorem
5.1
.
This will be shown in
[
Par2
]
, and we briefly summarize the results here.
Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a smooth projective morphism of relative dimension
4
with
\omega:\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\xrightarrow{\cong}\Omega^{4}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}
.
Let
\mathcal{M}
be any moduli space of perfect complexes on the fibres of
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
.
The isotropic condition can be detected infinitesimally so we may assume that
\mathcal{B}=\operatorname{Spec}\,(A)
for a local Artinian
\mathbb{C}
-algebra
A
.
Let
\gamma=\operatorname{ch}_{2}(E)\in H^{4}(\mathcal{X}_{0},\mathbb{C})
for any
[E]\in\mathcal{M}
and let
\widetilde{\gamma}\in H^{4}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
be the horizontal lift.
Then the
(-2)
-symplectic form on the derived enhancement of
\mathcal{M}

[
PTVV
,
ToVa
]
maps to zero under the map (
102
) if and only if

(103)

\widetilde{\gamma}\in F^{1}H^{4}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})=\mathbb{H}^{4}(\mathcal{X},\Omega^{\geq 1}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})\subseteq H^{4}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})=\mathbb{H}^{4}(\mathcal{X},\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}).

In the situation of Theorem
5.1
, the condition (
103
) is satisfied since we already assumed
\widetilde{\gamma}\in F^{2}H^{4}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
.
Moreover, condition (
103
) implies the existence of a local Darboux chart which induces the desired isotropic condition.

Heuristically, the result in
[
Par2
]
tells us that the relative isotropic condition is satisfied along the locus in the base where the deformations of
\operatorname{ch}_{2}(E)
do not have a
(0,4)
-Hodge piece.
This condition is compatible with the gauge-theoretical point of view since the holomorphic bundles are the solutions of

\int_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\alpha(E)\cup\omega=0

where
\alpha(E)=2\operatorname{ch}_{2}(E)
, see
[
OT1
, Appendix A]
.
We thank Richard Thomas for the explanation of the gauge-theory perspective and the expectation that (
103
) is the necessary additional condition to obtain deformation invariance.

Consequently, we have deformation invariance of numerical invariants.

Corollary 5.3
.

In the situation of Theorem
5.1
,
the function

b\in\mathcal{B}\mapsto\int_{[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}_{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})]^{\mathrm{red}}}{j_{b}^{*}(\star)}\in\mathbb{Q}

is constant for any insertion
\star\in A^{\mathrm{rvd}}(\curly P^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}))
.

There are two technical assumptions in Theorem
5.1
:

A1)

The function
b\in\mathcal{B}\mapsto\rho_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}\in\mathbb{Z}
is constant.

A2)

The symmetric complex
\mathbb{E}
on
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
and the orthogonal bundle
\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
on
\mathcal{B}
are orientable.

We first discuss the assumption A1 on
\rho_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}
.

Remark 5.4
.

The assumption A1 is
generically
satisfied.
Indeed, the function
b\in\mathcal{B}\mapsto\rho_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}\in\mathbb{Z}
is a lower semi-continuous function.
Hence if we let
\rho_{\max}
be the maximal value, then

\mathcal{B}^{\circ}:=\{b\in\mathcal{B}:\rho_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}=\rho_{\max}\}

is an open dense subscheme of
\mathcal{B}
.
Consequently, we can form an orthogonal bundle of rank
\rho_{\max}
on
\mathcal{B}^{\circ}
as

\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}:=(\mathcal{T}|_{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}})/\ker(\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}).

On the other hand, for the
special
cases when assumption A1 is not satisfied, we have a vanishing result by Proposition
5.5
below.

Proposition 5.5
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a smooth projective morphism of relative dimension
4
with connected fibres to a smooth connected affine scheme
\mathcal{B}
such that
\omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
. Let
\widetilde{v}\in\bigoplus_{p}F^{p}H^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
be a horizontal section and let
\widetilde{\gamma}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{B},R^{2}f_{*}\Omega^{2}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})
be the section induced by
\widetilde{v}_{2}
.
Let
\mathcal{B}^{\circ}:=\{b\in\mathcal{B}:\rho_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}=\rho_{\max}\}
as in Remark
5.4
.
If
b\in\mathcal{B}
is a point such that
b\not\in\mathcal{B}^{\circ}
, then we have

\left[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}_{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})\right]^{\mathrm{red}}=0\in A_{*}\left({\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}_{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})\right)

for any orientation.

Before we prove Proposition
5.5
, we discuss the assumption A2 on orientations.
The following simple observation shows that the orientability of the orthogonal bundle
\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
is a minor issue.

Remark 5.6
.

There exists a canonical 2-fold étale cover
u:\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}\to\mathcal{B}^{\circ}
such that
\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}}
is orientable.
Indeed, we can form a fibre diagram

\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Spec}\,(\mathbb{C})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\det(\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}})}
\textstyle{B\mu_{2}}

where the horizontal map
\mathcal{B}^{\circ}\to B\mu_{2}
corresponds to the pair of the line bundle
\det(\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}})
and the isomorphism
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}}\cong\det(\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}})^{\otimes 2}
induced by
\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
.
Then we have a canonical bijection

\{\text{orientations of $\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}$}\}\cong\{\text{sections of $\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}\to\mathcal{B}^{\circ}$}\}.

Consequently, there exists a canonical orientation of the pullback
\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{B}}}
.

On the other hand, the orientability of the symmetric complex
\mathbb{E}
is more subtle.
The orientability result of Cao-Gross-Joyce
[
CGJ
]
for the fibres does not imply the orientability for the relative case.
We suggest two strategies:

a)

Use the canonical orientation on the canonical 2-fold étale cover of the
moduli space

\curly P
as in Remark
5.6
.

b)

Find an orientation after a
base change
by a finite étale cover
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}
.

Strategy a) is sufficient for applications to the variational Hodge conjecture in the next subsection.
However, if we want to obtain the deformation invariance of numerical invariants (Corollary
5.3
),
strategy a) is not enough and we need strategy b). We expect that the following conjecture holds.

Conjecture 5.7
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a smooth projective morphism with connected fibres of relative dimension
4
to a smooth connected affine scheme
\mathcal{B}
such that
\omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
. Let
\widetilde{v}\in\bigoplus_{p}F^{p}H^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
be a horizontal section and let
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
.

(1)

Then there exists a finite étale cover
u:\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}
such that
\mathbb{E}|_{{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\mathcal{B}^{\prime})}
is orientable, where
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{v}^{\prime}=u^{*}(\widetilde{v})
.

(2)

Moreover, we can further assume that the degree of the étale cover
u:\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}
in (1) divides

|K_{\mathrm{top}}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{b}^{\mathrm{an}})_{\mathrm{tor}}|!

where
K_{\mathrm{top}}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{b}^{\mathrm{an}})_{\mathrm{tor}}\subseteq K^{0}_{\mathrm{top}}(\mathcal{X}_{b}^{\mathrm{an}})
is the torsion subgroup of the complex topological
K
-theory of
\mathcal{X}_{b}^{\mathrm{an}}
, which is finite.

We will provide evidence for Conjecture
5.7
in Appendix
C
.
Indeed, we will prove that the topological analog of Conjecture
5.7
is true (see Corollary
C.2
).

Remark 5.8
.

Assuming Conjecture
5.7
,
\mathbb{E}
is orientable in the following cases:

(1)

\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{an}}
is simply-connected.

(2)

H^{*}(\mathcal{X}_{b}^{\mathrm{an}},\mathbb{Z})
has no torsion elements for some
b\in\mathcal{B}
, e.g., all complete intersection Calabi-Yau
4
-folds in smooth projective toric varieties.

In case (2), we have
K^{0}_{\mathrm{top}}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{an}}_{b})_{\mathrm{tor}}=0
by
[
AH
, Cor. 2.5(i)]
.

We now prove Proposition
5.5
.

Proof of Proposition
5.5
.

We may assume that

\mathcal{B}^{\circ}=\mathcal{B}-\{b\}.

Indeed, we can find a smooth connected affine curve
C
with a map
g:C\to\mathcal{B}
such that
g^{-1}(\mathcal{B}^{\circ})=C-\{c\}
for some point
c\in C
. Replace
\mathcal{B}
by
C
.

We claim that there exists a non-degenerate subbundle

\mathcal{V}\subseteq\mathcal{T}

of rank
\rho_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}
,
after replacing
\mathcal{B}
by some open neighborhood of
b\in\mathcal{B}
.
Indeed, choose a maximal non-degenerate subspace
V\subseteq H^{1}(\mathcal{X}_{b},T_{\mathcal{X}_{b}})
. Then we can extend this to a map

\mathcal{V}:=V\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{B}}\to\mathcal{T}

of vector bundles.

Since its dual
\mathcal{T}^{\vee}\to\mathcal{V}^{\vee}
is surjective over
b\in\mathcal{B}
, by shrinking
\mathcal{B}
, we may assume that
\mathcal{V}
is a subbundle of
\mathcal{T}
.
Since
\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}|_{\mathcal{V}}
is non-degenerate over
b\in\mathcal{B}
, by shrinking
\mathcal{B}
, we may assume that
\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}|_{\mathcal{V}}

is non-degenerate on
\mathcal{B}
.

Let
\widetilde{\curly P}\to\curly P
be the canonical 2-fold étale cover induced by the line bundle
\det(\mathbb{E})\otimes\det(\mathcal{V})^{\vee}
and the symmetric forms of
\mathbb{E}
and
\mathcal{V}
(as in Remark
5.6
). Form a fibre diagram

\textstyle{\widetilde{\curly P}_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\curly P}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\curly P}^{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{k}
\textstyle{\curly P_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t_{b}}
\textstyle{\curly P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\curly P^{\circ}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{b\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i_{b}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

Since the symmetric complex
\mathbb{E}_{b}
on the fibre
\curly P_{b}
is orientable by
[
CGJ
]
and the vector space
\mathcal{V}_{b}
is always orientable, we have

\widetilde{\curly P}_{b}=\curly P_{b}\sqcup\curly P_{b}.

Let
t_{b}:\curly P_{b}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\curly P}_{b}
denote the inclusion to the first component.

Although the rank of
\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
jumps at
b
,
the rank of
\mathsf{B}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}|_{\mathcal{V}}
does not jump,
so the argument in Theorem
5.1
can indeed be used.
Hence there exists a cycle class

[\widetilde{\curly P}]^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}\in A_{*}(\widetilde{\curly P})

such that

(104)

i_{b}^{!}[\widetilde{\curly P}]^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}=\left([\curly P_{b}]^{\mathrm{red}},-[\curly P_{b}]^{\mathrm{red}}\right)\in A_{*}(\widetilde{\curly P}_{b})=A_{*}(\curly P_{b})\oplus A_{*}(\curly P_{b}).

Indeed, we have a decomposition of symmetric complexes

\mathbb{E}\cong\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}\oplus(\mathcal{V}|_{\curly P}[1])

for some symmetric complex
\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}
and the relative cone reduction property

(\mathfrak{C}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}})_{\mathrm{red}}\subseteq\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}).

We can define the desired cycle class as

[\widetilde{\curly P}]^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}:=\sqrt{0^{!}_{\mathfrak{Q}\left(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}|_{\widetilde{\curly P}}\right)}}\left[\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\curly P}/\mathcal{B}}\right]\in A_{*}(\widetilde{\curly P})

since the pullback
\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}|_{\widetilde{\curly P}}
is orientable by the definition of
\widetilde{\curly P}
.

Since there are additional cosections on
\curly P^{\circ}
, we claim that

(105)

k^{*}[\widetilde{\curly P}]^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}=0\in A_{*}(\widetilde{\curly P}^{\circ}).

Indeed, the orthogonal bundle
\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
is well-defined on
\mathcal{B}^{\circ}
, and the composition

\mathcal{V}|_{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{T}|_{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}

is injective on the fibres. Thus by considering a section of the surjection

\frac{\mathcal{T}|_{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}}}{\mathcal{V}|_{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}}}\twoheadrightarrow\frac{\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}}{\mathcal{V}|_{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}}}

we can find a non-degenerate subbundle
\mathcal{W}
of
\mathcal{T}|_{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}}
properly containing
\mathcal{V}|_{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}}
,

\mathcal{V}|_{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}}\subsetneq\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{T}|_{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}}.

Then we have
\mathcal{W}=\mathcal{V}|_{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}}\oplus\mathcal{U}
for the orthogonal complement
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathcal{V}|_{\mathcal{B}^{\circ}}
in
\mathcal{W}
. Moreover, we have a decomposition of symmetric complexes

\mathbb{E}|_{\curly P^{\circ}}=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{W}}\oplus(\mathcal{W}|_{\curly P^{\circ}}[1])=\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{W}}\oplus(\mathcal{V}|_{\curly P^{\circ}}[1])\oplus(\mathcal{U}|_{\curly P^{\circ}}[1])

for some symmetric complex
\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{W}}
. Hence we have

(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}})|_{\curly P^{\circ}}\cong\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{W}}\oplus(\mathcal{U}|_{\curly P^{\circ}}[1]).

By shrinking
\mathcal{B}
further, we find a section
w\in\Gamma(\mathcal{B}^{\circ},\mathcal{U})
such that
w^{2}
is nowhere vanishing.
Consequently, we can find a cosection

\sigma:\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}|_{\curly P^{\circ}}[1]\to\mathcal{O}_{\curly P^{\circ}}

such that
\sigma^{2}
is nowhere vanishing. Lemma
5.9
below proves the claim.

By the localization sequence

\textstyle{A_{*}(\widetilde{\curly P}_{b})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{j_{*}}
\textstyle{A_{*}(\widetilde{\curly P})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{k^{*}}
\textstyle{A_{*}(\widetilde{\curly P}^{\circ})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0,}

and the vanishing (
105
), we have

[\widetilde{\curly P}]^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}=j_{*}(\alpha)

for some
\alpha\in A_{*}(\widetilde{\curly P}_{b})
. By the self-intersection formula, we have

i^{!}_{b}[\widetilde{\curly P}]^{\mathrm{red}}_{\mathcal{V}}=i_{b}^{!}\circ j_{*}(\alpha)=e(N_{b/\mathcal{B}})\cdot\alpha=0.

Then the formula (
104
) proves the desired vanishing result.
∎

We need the following lemma to complete the proof of Proposition
5.5
.

Lemma 5.9

(cf.
[
KiPa2
, Thm. 8.12]
)
.

Let
\mathbb{E}
be any symmetric complex on a quasi-projective scheme
\mathscr{X}
. Let
\sigma:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[1]\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}
be a cosection such that
\sigma^{2}\in\Gamma(\mathscr{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}})
is nowhere vanishing. Consider a fibre diagram

\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E}_{\sigma})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{l}
\textstyle{\mathscr{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{0}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{l}_{\sigma}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{\mathscr{X}}}

where
\mathbb{E}_{\sigma}:=\mathrm{cone}(\sigma^{\vee}[1]:\mathcal{O}_{\mathscr{X}}\to\mathbb{E})
.
Then the composition

A_{*}(\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E}_{\sigma}))\xrightarrow{l_{*}}A_{*}(\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E}))\xrightarrow{\sqrt{0^{!}_{\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E})}}}A_{*}(\mathscr{X})

vanishes.

Proof.

We omit the proof. See
[
KiPa2
, Sect. 8.3]
.
∎

5.2.
Variational Hodge conjecture

As an application of the deformation invariance in the previous subsection, we prove that the
variational Hodge conjecture
holds for surface classes with non-zero reduced virtual cycles.

Fix a smooth projective variety
X
over
\mathbb{C}
.

Definition 5.10
.

Let
\gamma\in H^{2p}(X,\mathbb{C})
be a cohomology class on
X
.

(1)

We say that
\gamma
is a
Hodge class
if

\gamma\in Hdg^{p}(X):=H^{2p}(X,\mathbb{Q})\cap H^{p}(X,\Omega^{p}_{X})\subset H^{2p}(X,\mathbb{C}).

(2)

We say that
\gamma
is an
algebraic
cohomology class if
\gamma
contained in the image of the cycle class map,

\gamma\in\operatorname{im}(\mathrm{cl}:A^{p}(X)_{\mathbb{Q}}\to Hdg^{p}(X)).

The
Hodge conjecture
predicts that every Hodge class on
X
is algebraic.
Grothendieck’s
variational Hodge conjecture

[
Gro1
, footnote 13]
(cf.
[
Blo
]
) predicts that a deformation of an algebraic cohomology class as a Hodge class is algebraic.

Thus we make the following definition:

Definition 5.11
.

Let
X
be a smooth projective variety and
\gamma\in H^{2p}(X,\mathbb{C})
an algebraic cohomology class.
We say that the
variational Hodge conjecture
holds for the pair
(X,\gamma)
if the following condition is satisfied:
for any smooth projective morphism
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
to a smooth connected scheme
\mathcal{B}

and any horizontal section
\widetilde{v}_{p}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{B},F^{p}\mathcal{H}^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}))

such that
\mathcal{X}_{0}\cong X
and
(\widetilde{v}_{p})_{0}=\gamma
for some
0\in\mathcal{B}
, the cohomology classes
(\widetilde{v}_{p})_{b}\in H^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
are algebraic for all
b\in\mathcal{B}
.

Remark 5.12
.

In some of the literature, the variational Hodge conjecture is stated for any horizontal section of the Hodge bundle
\mathcal{H}_{DR}^{2p}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
instead of the filtration
F^{p}\mathcal{H}_{DR}^{2p}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
. Deligne’s global invariant cycle theorem
[
Del2
, (4.1.1)]
implies that the two versions are equivalent (see also
[
CS
, Prop. 11.3.5]
).

We now state the main theorem of this subsection.

Theorem 5.13
.

Let
X
be a Calabi-Yau 4-fold and let
\gamma\in H^{2}(X,\Omega^{2}_{X})
.
If

[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)]^{\mathrm{red}}\neq 0\in A_{\mathrm{rvd}}({\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X))

for some
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{C})
with
v_{2}=\gamma
and
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
, then the variational Hodge conjecture holds for
(X,\gamma)
.

For Calabi-Yau 4-folds, we would like to regard Theorem
5.13
as a
virtual
generalization of the results of Bloch and Buchweitz-Flenner
[
Blo
,
BuFl1
]
since the semi-regular case is heuristically the
ideal
case (see Theorem
4.6
). In particular, for Calabi-Yau 4-folds, we recover the result of Buchweitz-Flenner as an immediate corollary.

Corollary 5.14
.

Let
X
be a Calabi-Yau 4-fold and let
\gamma\in H^{2}(X,\Omega^{2}_{X})
. If there exists a
\mathrm{PT}_{q}
pair
(F,s)
on
X
such that
\operatorname{ch}_{2}(F)=\gamma
, for some
q\in\{-1,0,1\}
, and the associated complex
I^{{\scriptscriptstyle\bullet}}:=[\mathcal{O}_{X}\xrightarrow{s}F]
is semi-regular, then the variational Hodge conjecture holds for
(X,\gamma)
.

Proof.

By Theorem
4.6
, there exists a non-empty smooth open subset
U\subseteq{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)
such that

[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)]^{\mathrm{red}}|_{U}=\pm[U]\in A_{*}(U).

Hence
[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{v}(X)]^{\mathrm{red}}\neq 0
and Theorem
5.13
completes the proof.
∎

We now prove our main theorem in this subsection.

Proof of Theorem
5.13
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a smooth projective morphism to a smooth connected scheme
\mathcal{B}
and
\widetilde{\gamma}
be a horizontal section of
F^{2}\mathcal{H}_{DR}^{4}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
.
Assume that
\mathcal{X}_{0}\cong X
and
\widetilde{\gamma}_{0}=\gamma
for some point
0\in\mathcal{B}
.

Heuristically, Theorem
5.13
follows from the deformation invariance in Theorem
5.1
. However, to obtain a rigorous proof, we need to overcome the following technical issues:

(1)

existence of a horizontal lift
\widetilde{\beta}\in\Gamma(\mathcal{B},F^{3}\mathcal{H}_{DR}^{6}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}))
of
\beta=v_{3}
;

(2)

f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
may not be relatively Calabi-Yau (i.e.,
\omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
);

(3)

the function
b\in\mathcal{B}\mapsto\rho_{\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}}
may not be constant;

(4)

orientability of the orthogonal bundle
\mathcal{T}_{\widetilde{\gamma}}
on
\mathcal{B}
;

(5)

orientability of the virtual cotangent complex
\mathbb{E}
of
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\to\mathcal{B}
;

(6)

the reduced virtual cycles
[{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}_{b}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})]^{\mathrm{red}}
live in the Chow groups of different schemes for each
b\in\mathcal{B}
.

We first resolve issue (1). Denote by
P_{v}(t)
the Hilbert polynomial determined by
v
with respect to some polarization on
X
. We will consider a small variation on the moduli space
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
, namely

\curly P:=\curly P^{(q)}_{\widetilde{\gamma},P_{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})=\left\{(F,s,b):\begin{matrix}\text{$(F,s)$ is a $\mathrm{PT}_{q}$ pair on the fibre $\mathcal{X}_{b}$ of $b\in\mathcal{B}$}\\
\text{ such that $\operatorname{ch}_{2}(F)=\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}$ and $P_{F}(t)=P_{v}(t)$}\end{matrix}\right\}.

The basic properties of
{\curly P}^{(q)}_{\widetilde{v}}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
in Theorem
3.32
also holds for
\curly P
. More precisely, the projection map

p:\curly P\to\mathcal{B}

is projective and the canonical map

\curly P\hookrightarrow{\curly Perf}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}}^{\mathrm{spl}}

is an open embedding. Consequently, we have a symmetric obstruction theory

\phi:\mathbb{E}\to\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\curly P/\mathcal{B}}

for
p:\curly P\to\mathcal{B}
that satisfies the isotropic condition. To prove Theorem
5.13
, it suffices to show that the projection map
p:\curly P\to\mathcal{B}
is surjective. Since
p:\curly P\to\mathcal{B}
is projective, it suffices to show that the image of
p:\curly P\to\mathcal{B}
contains a non-empty open subset of
\mathcal{B}
.

Secondly, we resolve the issue (2). We claim that there exists a nonempty open neighborhood
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
of
0\in\mathcal{B}
such that
