) is independent of the choice of the symmetric resolution (
122
), as in
[
OT1
, Sect. 4.2]
.
This part is quite technical so we will only focus on the main difference with
[
OT1
]
.
By a deformation argument as in
[
OT1
]
and adding an acyclic symmetric complex of the form

[T_{1}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}}T_{1}\to 0\to T_{1}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{id}}T_{1}^{\vee}]\oplus[0\to T_{2}\xrightarrow{(1,0)}T_{2}\oplus T_{2}^{\vee}\xrightarrow{(0,1)}T_{2}^{\vee}\to 0],

it suffices to consider the following special case.
Consider a commutative diagram

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{N\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}
\textstyle{S\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{e}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{d}
\textstyle{E}

where the upper row is exact and the vertical arrows are injective. We will compare the two symmetric resolutions

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\cong[S\xrightarrow{e}B\xrightarrow{d}E\xrightarrow{d^{\vee}}B^{\vee}\xrightarrow{e^{\vee}}S^{\vee}]

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\cong[(S/M)\xrightarrow{e}(B/N)\xrightarrow{d}(K^{\perp}/K)\xrightarrow{d^{\vee}}(B/N)^{\vee}\xrightarrow{e^{\vee}}(S/M)^{\vee}].

Consider the fibre diagram

\textstyle{C(D)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{K^{\perp}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C(G)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{K^{\perp}/K\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left[\nicefrac{{(K^{\perp}/K)}}{{\left[\nicefrac{{(B/N)}}{{(S/M)}}\right]}}\right]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\left[\nicefrac{{E}}{{\left[\nicefrac{{B}}{{S}}\right]}}\right]}

where
G:=\operatorname{coker}((B/N)\to(K^{\perp}/K))
.
Let
\widetilde{\tau}\in\Gamma(C(G),K^{\perp}/K|_{C(G)})
be the tautological section.
Then the formula

\sqrt{e}(E|_{C(D)},\tau)=\sqrt{e}(K^{\perp}/K|_{C(G)},\widetilde{\tau})\circ t^{*}

in
[
KiPa2
, Cor. 4.7]
proves the claim.

Step 4.

Finally, we prove (
121
). Consider the factorization
\mathscr{X}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{Y}^{\prime}
induced by the base change of (
124
) along
i:\mathscr{Y}^{\prime}\to\mathscr{Y}
. By Vistoli’s rational equivalence
[
Vis
,
Kre2
]
(cf.
[
Qu
, Prop. 2.5]
), we have a commutative square

\textstyle{A_{*}(\mathcal{Z})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{sp}_{\mathscr{X}/\mathscr{Z}}}
\scriptstyle{i^{!}}
\textstyle{A_{*}(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathscr{X}/\mathscr{Z}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{!}}
\textstyle{A_{*}(\mathcal{Z}^{\prime})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{sp}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}/\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}}}
\textstyle{A_{*}(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}/\mathscr{Z}^{\prime}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A_{*}(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathscr{X}/\mathscr{Z}}|_{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}}).}

Since the localized Edidin-Graham class (
123
) commutes with the refined Gysin pullback
i^{!}
by
[
KiPa2
, Lem. 4.4(3)]
, we obtain the desired formula (
121
).
∎

Next, we generalize Kiem-Li’s cone reduction lemma
[
KL
]
to Artin stacks.

Theorem B.3
.

Let
\mathscr{X}
be an algebraic stack.
Let
\phi:\mathbb{E}\to\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{X}}
be an obstruction theory.
Let
\Sigma:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[1]\to F
be a map to a vector bundle
F
.
Then

(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathscr{X}})_{\mathrm{red}}\subseteq\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E}_{\Sigma})

as substacks of
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E})
where
\mathbb{E}_{\Sigma}:=\mathrm{cone}(\Sigma^{\vee}[1]:F^{\vee}[1]\to\mathbb{E})
.

Proof.

Choose a smooth surjection
\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}\to\mathscr{X}
from an affine scheme
\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}
. We claim that we can form a morphism of distinguished triangles

(127)

\textstyle{\mathbb{E}|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\widetilde{\phi}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}/\mathscr{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}}[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi[1]}
\textstyle{\tau^{\geq-1}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{X}}|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}/\mathscr{X}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\tau^{\geq-1}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{X}}|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}})[1]}

in the underlying triangulated category
D(\mathscr{X}):=[\mathsf{D}(\mathscr{X})]
.
Indeed, the dotted arrow exists since
\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}
is affine and
\mathrm{cone}(\phi[1])\in D(\widetilde{\mathscr{X}})
has amplitude
(-\infty,-3]
.
Then
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}
and
\widetilde{\phi}
can be defined by the axioms of triangulated categories.

The long exact sequence associated to (
127
) ensures that
\widetilde{\phi}
is an obstruction theory.
Moreover we can define a map
\widetilde{\Sigma}
as the composition

\widetilde{\Sigma}:\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\vee}[1]\to\mathbb{E}|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}}^{\vee}[1]\xrightarrow{\Sigma|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}}}F|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}}.

Then we can apply Kiem-Li’s cone reduction lemma (see Lemma
4.16
) for the scheme
\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}
. Hence we have

(\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}})_{\mathrm{red}}\subseteq\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}})

as substacks of
\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}})
, where
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}}:=\mathrm{cone}(\widetilde{\Sigma}^{\vee}[1]:F|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}}^{\vee}[1]\to\widetilde{\mathbb{E}})
.
By (
127
), we have a fibre diagram

\textstyle{(\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}})_{\mathrm{red}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}_{\widetilde{\Sigma}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathscr{X}}|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}})_{\mathrm{red}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}_{\mathscr{X}}|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\mathbb{E}|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}})}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}((\mathbb{E}_{\Sigma})|_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

where the horizontal maps are closed embeddings and the vertical maps are
BT_{\widetilde{\mathscr{X}}/\mathscr{X}}
-torsors by
[
AP
, Lem. 5.12(1), Thm. 6.3(2)]
. Hence we can deduce the desired property via descent.
∎

Finally, we generalize the construction of reduced Oh-Thomas virtual cycles for non-degenerate cosections in this paper (cf.
[
KiPa2
]
) to Artin stacks.

Theorem B.4
.

In the situation of Theorem
B.1
, consider a map
\Sigma:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[1]\to F
to a special orthogonal bundle
(F,\mathfrak{q}_{F},o_{F})
on
\mathscr{X}
such that
\Sigma^{2}=\mathfrak{q}_{F}
.
We further assume that
\mathscr{Y}
is smooth scheme
and the composition

(128)

F^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\Sigma^{\vee}}\mathbb{E}[-1]\xrightarrow{\phi}(\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{X}/\mathscr{Y}})[-1]\xrightarrow{\mathsf{KS}}\Omega^{1}_{\mathscr{Y}}|_{\mathscr{X}}

is zero.
Then there is a canonical reduced square root virtual pullback

\sqrt{f^{!}_{\mathrm{red}}}:A_{*}(\mathscr{Y})\to A_{*}(\mathscr{X})

such that
\sqrt{f^{!}}=\sqrt{e}(F)\circ\sqrt{f^{!}_{\mathrm{red}}}
.
Moreover, for any cartesian square

\textstyle{\mathscr{X}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f^{\prime}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\textstyle{\mathscr{Y}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i}
\textstyle{\mathscr{Y}}

where
i
is a closed embedding of smooth schemes, then we have

(129)

i^{!}\circ\sqrt{f^{!}_{\mathrm{red}}}=\sqrt{(f^{\prime})^{!}_{\mathrm{red}}}\circ i^{!}:A_{*}(\mathscr{Y})\to A_{*}(\mathscr{X}^{\prime}).

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{E}\cong\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}\oplus F[1]
be a decomposition of symmetric complexes induced by
\Sigma^{2}=\mathfrak{q}_{F}
.
By a standard argument as in Theorem
5.1
, we can modify the relative obstruction theory
\phi
into an absolute obstruction theory.
The vanishing of the composition (
128
) ensures that the relative cosection
\Sigma
can be lifted to the absolute obstruction theory.
Hence we can apply the cone reduction lemma in Theorem
B.3
.
As in Theorem
5.1
, we now have a closed embedding
(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathscr{X}/\mathscr{Y}})_{\mathrm{red}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}})
. Then we can define the reduced square root virtual pullback
\sqrt{f^{!}_{\mathrm{red}}}
as in Theorem
B.1
using the data
(\mathfrak{C}_{\mathscr{X}/\mathscr{Y}})_{\mathrm{red}}\hookrightarrow\mathfrak{Q}(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}})
. Here we consider the orientation of
\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}}
induced by the canonical isomorphism
\det(\mathbb{E}^{\mathrm{red}})\cong\det(\mathbb{E})\otimes\det(F[1])^{\vee}
.
The argument in Theorem
B.1
also proves formula (
129
).
∎

Remark B.5
.

We hope to remove the global quotient hypothesis in Theorem
B.1
and Theorem
B.4
.
At least when
\mathscr{X}
has reductive stabilizers and quasi-affine diagonal, then the authors expect that this will not be very difficult.
Indeed, we sketch the following argument.
By Alper-Hall-Rydh
[
AHR
]
, there exists an étale cover
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
by a global quotient stack
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
.
By the Chow lemma
[
BP
]
(cf.
[
LMB
]
), we can form a proper surjection
\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
from a global quotient stack
\mathcal{X}^{\prime\prime}
using
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
.
Since we have a Kimura sequence
[
AKLPR
, Cor. 1.23]
for the Chow groups of higher Artin stacks developed by Khan
[
Kha
]
, we can reduce the situation to the global quotient case.

If this expectation works, then we can apply Theorem
B.4
to any open substack of the moduli stack
{\curly Perf}(X)
of a perfect complexes on a Calabi-Yau
4
-fold
X
which has a good moduli space
[
Alp
]
.

B.2.
Moduli stacks of semi-stable sheaves

In this subsection, we apply the results in the previous subsection to the moduli
stack
of sheaves.

We first consider the
rigidified
version. Let
(X,H)
be a polarized Calabi-Yau
4
-fold. Let
{\curly Coh}(X)
be the moduli stack of coherent sheaves on
X
. Then there exists a natural
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
{\curly Coh}(X)
. Let
{\curly Coh}(X)\!\!\!\fatslash\mathbb{G}_{m}:=[{\curly Coh}(X)/B\mathbb{G}_{m}]
be the quotient stack.
26
26

26

Since the
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
{\curly Coh}(X)
is
free
, i.e., the induced
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on the stabilizers
\operatorname{Aut}(E)
are free for all
E\in{\curly Coh}(X)
, we can alternatively use the
rigidification
of classical
1
-Artin stacks, see
[
AOV
, Thm. A.1]
.
Let

\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)\subseteq{\curly Coh}(X)\!\!\!\fatslash\mathbb{G}_{m}

be the open substack of (Gieseker)
H
-semi-stable sheaves with Chern character
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
. Here semi-stability is an open condition by
[
HL
, Prop. 2.3.1]
.

Theorem B.6
.

Let
(X,H)
be a polarized Calabi-Yau
4
-fold and
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
.
Then, for any choice of orientation, there exists a canonical reduced virtual cycle

[\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)]^{\mathrm{red}}\in A_{1-\frac{1}{2}\chi(v,v)+\frac{1}{2}\rho_{\gamma}}(\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X))

where
\gamma:=v_{2}
and
\chi(v,v):=\int_{X}v^{\vee}\cdot v\cdot\mathrm{td}(X)
.
Moreover, if
[\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)]^{\mathrm{red}}\neq 0
, then the variational Hodge conjecture holds for
(X,\gamma)
.

Proof.

We first fix some notation. Let
\mathcal{M}:=\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)
and
\lambda:\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\to\mathcal{M}
be the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-gerbe induced by
{\curly Coh}(X)\to{\curly Coh}(X)\!\!\!\fatslash\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
Let
\mathscr{E}
be the universal sheaf on
X\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
and
\pi:X\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\to\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
be the projection map.
Note that we have a weight decomposition

D(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})\cong\prod_{w\in\mathbb{Z}}D(\mathcal{M})

by Bergh-Schnürer
[
BS
]
, where
D(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}):=[\mathsf{D}(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})]
and
D(\mathcal{M}):=[\mathsf{D}(\mathcal{M})]
are the underlying triangulated categories of the stable
\infty
-categories.
27
27

27

For an
1
-Artin stack, the derived category defined via descent in the context of derived algebraic geometry
[
TV1
]
and the derived category defined as a full subcategory of sheaves of modules on the lisse-étale site in
[
LMB
,
Ols
]
coincide by Hall-Rydh
[
HR
, Prop. 1.3]
.

Moreover,

\lambda^{*}:D(\mathcal{M})\to D(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})

is fully faithful and the essential image consists of weight zero objects.

We describe the canonical symmetric obstruction theory on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
as follows. Let
R{\curly Coh}(X)
be the derived moduli stack of coherent sheaves on
X
. Then the canonical map
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}\xrightarrow{\mathscr{E}}R{\curly Coh}(X)
induces an open embedding on the classical truncations. Hence we have an induced symmetric obstruction theory

\widetilde{\phi}:\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}:=\mathbb{L}_{R{\curly Coh}(X)}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}=(R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{E})[1])^{\vee}\xrightarrow{\operatorname{At}(\mathscr{E})}\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}},

which satisfies the isotropic condition by
[
BBBBJ
]
and is orientable by
[
CGJ
]
.
Since
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\in D(\widetilde{\mathcal{M}})
is a weight zero object, we have
\lambda^{*}(\lambda_{*}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}})\cong\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}
for
\lambda_{*}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\in D(\mathcal{M})
.

We note that the canonical obstruction theory on
\mathcal{M}
given by the standard derived enhancement is
not
symmetric.
Indeed, the
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
{\curly Coh}(X)
extends to
R{\curly Coh}(X)
and the canonical map
\mathcal{M}\to[R{\curly Coh}(X)/B\mathbb{G}_{m}]
induces an open embedding on the classical truncations. Hence we have an induced obstruction theory

\phi_{1}:\mathbb{E}_{1}:=\mathbb{L}_{[R{\curly Coh}(X)/B\mathbb{G}_{m}]}|_{\mathcal{M}}\to\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{M}}

where the virtual cotangent complex
\mathbb{E}_{1}
fits into the distinguished triangle

\textstyle{\mathbb{L}_{[R{\curly Coh}(X)/B\mathbb{G}_{m}]}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{L}_{R{\curly Coh}(X)}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{L}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\lambda^{*}\mathbb{E}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{t}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}[-1].}

Here
t^{\vee}:\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}[1]\to\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\vee}=R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{E})[1]
is given by the identity
\operatorname{id}_{\mathscr{E}}:\mathscr{E}\to\mathscr{E}
since the induced
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on the derived loop space
\mathcal{L}_{R{\curly Coh}(X),E}\subseteq\mathrm{Tot}(R\operatorname{Hom}_{X}(E,E))
at
[E]\in R{\curly Coh}(X)
is the homothety.

We then
symmetrize
the obstruction theory
\phi_{1}
via reduction operation in
[
Par1
, Lem. C.2]
.
Indeed, since

\operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathcal{M})}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[3],\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[-2])=0,

we can form a reduction diagram (in the sense of
[
Par1
, Lem. C.2]
)

(130)

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[3]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[3]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{*}t^{\vee}[2]}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\vee}[2]}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{\vee}[2]}
\textstyle{\lambda_{*}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{*}t}
\scriptstyle{\alpha}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[-1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\vee}[2]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[-1]}

in the triangulated category
D(\mathcal{M})
where
\mathbb{E}
is a
symmetric
complex. Since

\operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathcal{M})}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[3],\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{M}})=\operatorname{Hom}_{D(\mathcal{M})}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[4],\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{M}})=0,

there exists a unique symmetric obstruction theory

\phi:\mathbb{E}\to\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{M}}

which fits into the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[3]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{\vee}[2]}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists\phi}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[4]}
\textstyle{\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{M}}}

as the dotted arrow.
The isotropic condition for
\phi
follows from the one for
\widetilde{\phi}
. Indeed, we can form a commutative diagram of (higher) cone stacks on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}

\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\lambda^{*}\mathbb{E}_{1}^{\vee}[2])\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\lambda^{*}\alpha)}
\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\lambda^{*}\beta)}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{M}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\lambda^{*}\mathbb{E})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\mathfrak{C}(\lambda^{*}\mathbb{E}_{1})}

where the horizontal maps are closed embeddings and the vertical maps are
\mathrm{Tot}(\mathbb{T}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}/\mathcal{M}})\cong B\mathbb{G}_{a}
-torsors. From the reduction diagram (
130
), we can deduce that the two quadratic functions
\mathfrak{q}_{\mathbb{E}}
and
\mathfrak{q}_{\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}}
restrict to the same function on
\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}
. Since the composition

\mathfrak{C}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{M}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{C}_{\mathcal{M}}

is smooth surjective, we obtain the desired isotropic condition via descent.

Moreover, an orientation of
\mathbb{E}
can be induced by one of
\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}
since we have

\det(\mathbb{E})\cong\lambda_{*}\det(\widetilde{\mathbb{E}})

by the reduction diagram (
130
).

We will now form a cosection
\mathsf{SR}:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[1]\to H^{1}(X,T_{X})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}
for the obstruction theory
\phi
on
\mathcal{M}
. Firstly, define a cosection for the obstruction theory
\widetilde{\phi}
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}
as the composition

\widetilde{\mathsf{SR}}:\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}^{\vee}[1]=R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{E})[2]\xrightarrow{\operatorname{At}(\mathscr{E})}R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{E}\otimes\mathbb{L}_{X\times\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}})[3]\\
\xrightarrow{}R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{E}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X})[3]\xrightarrow{\operatorname{tr}}R\Gamma(X,\Omega^{1}_{X})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}[3]\\
\xrightarrow{}H^{3}(X,\Omega^{1}_{X})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}\xrightarrow{\cong}H^{1}(X,T_{X})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}.

Then we have
\widetilde{\mathsf{SR}}^{2}=\mathsf{B}_{\gamma}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}}}
by the argument in Proposition
4.12
. Then there exists a unique cosection

\mathsf{SR}:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[1]\to H^{1}(X,T_{X})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}

which fits into the commutative diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[3]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\beta^{\vee}[2]}
\scriptstyle{\alpha^{\vee}[2]}
\textstyle{\mathbb{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\exists\mathsf{SR}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[4]}
\textstyle{\lambda_{*}\widetilde{\mathbb{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\lambda_{*}\widetilde{\mathsf{SR}}}
\textstyle{H^{1}(X,T_{X})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[1]}

since

\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[3],H^{1}(T_{X})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[1])=\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{M}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[4],H^{1}(T_{X})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[1])=0.

Moreover, we have
\mathsf{SR}^{2}=\lambda_{*}\widetilde{\mathsf{SR}}^{2}=\mathsf{B}_{\gamma}\otimes 1_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}}
by the reduction diagram (
130
).

Therefore, if we choose a maximal non-degenerate subspace
V\subseteq H^{1}(T_{X})
, then we have a non-degenerate cosection
\mathsf{SR}_{V}:\mathbb{E}^{\vee}[1]\xrightarrow{\mathsf{SR}}H^{1}(T_{X})^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[1]\to V^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}}[1]
for the symmetric obstruction theory
\phi:\mathbb{E}\to\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{M}}
. Hence we can apply Theorem
B.4
and define a reduced Oh-Thomas virtual cycle

[\mathcal{M}]^{\mathrm{red}}\in A_{*}(\mathcal{M})

where
\mathcal{M}
is a global quotient stack
[
HL
]
.

Finally, we show that
[\mathcal{M}]^{\mathrm{red}}
is deformation invariant and
[\mathcal{M}]^{\mathrm{red}}\neq 0
implies the variational Hodge conjecture. Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a family of Calabi-Yau
4
-folds (as in Theorem
5.1
). Let
\widetilde{\gamma}\in F^{2}H^{4}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
be a horizontal section and let
P_{v}(t)\in\mathbb{Q}[t]
be the Hilbert polynomial determined by
v
. Then we have a relative moduli stack
\mathcal{M}^{ss}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
of semi-stable sheaves
E
on the fibres
\mathcal{X}_{b}
of
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
over
b\in\mathcal{B}
with
\operatorname{ch}_{2}(E)=\widetilde{\gamma}_{b}
and
P_{E}(t)=P_{v}(t)
as a global quotient open substack of
{\curly Coh}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})

[
HL
]
.
Then all the arguments above also work for the relative setting so that we have a relative symmetric obstruction theory
\phi_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}:\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\to\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})}
and a cosection
\mathsf{SR}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}:\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}^{\vee}[1]\to(R^{1}f_{*}T_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})|_{\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})}^{\vee}
.
As in Lemma
4.21
, we have an analogous commutative square

\textstyle{\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{SR}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}}
\textstyle{(R^{1}f_{*}T_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}})|_{\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})}^{\vee}[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{KS}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}}
\textstyle{\tau^{\geq-1}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{KS}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})/\mathcal{B}}}
\textstyle{\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}|_{\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})}[1]}

since
\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})}[4],\Omega^{1}_{\mathcal{B}}|_{\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})}[1])=0
. Then, based on Theorem
B.4
, all the remaining arguments in Theorem
5.1
work in this setting.
Moreover, the analog of Theorem
5.13
also works in this setting, since there is a good moduli space of
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
which is proper over
\mathcal{B}
.
∎

Remark B.7
.

In the proof of Theorem
B.6
, if we restrict to the moduli
scheme

\mathcal{M}^{H,st}_{v}(X)
of stable sheaves, then the reduction in (
130
) is just the truncation

\tau^{[-2,0]}(R\mathcal{H}{\it{om}}_{\pi}(\mathscr{E},\mathscr{E})[1])^{\vee},

which is used in
[
OT1
,
KiPa2
]
.

Remark B.8
.

Theorem
B.6
also holds for the non-rigidified moduli stack
\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)\subseteq{\curly Coh}(X)
.
However, we will not use this approach since this will give us a weaker statement for the application to the variational Hodge conjecture since
[\widetilde{\mathcal{M}}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)]^{\mathrm{red}}=\lambda^{*}[\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)]^{\mathrm{red}}
.

Remark B.9
.

Assuming the expectation in Remark
B.5
, then the first part of Theorem
B.6
holds for any
1
-Artin open substack
\mathcal{M}(X)\subseteq{\curly Perf}(X)\!\!\!\fatslash\mathbb{G}_{m}
with good moduli space. Moreover, if we can form a
1
-Artin open substack
\mathcal{M}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\subseteq{\curly Perf}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\!\!\!\fatslash\mathbb{G}_{m}
with proper good moduli space for any family of Calabi-Yau
4
-folds
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
, then the second part of Theorem
B.6
also holds.

Next, we consider the
fixed determinant
version.
Let
(X,H)
be a polarized Calabi-Yau
4
-fold.
Note that there is a canonical determinant map
\det:{\curly Coh}(X)\to{\curly Pic}(X)
to the moduli stack
{\curly Pic}(X)
of line bundles on
X
. Let
{\curly Coh}(X)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}:={\curly Coh}(X)\times_{\det,{\curly Pic}(X),\mathcal{O}_{X}}\operatorname{Spec}\,(\mathbb{C})
be the fibre over the trivial line bundle. Let

\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\subseteq{\curly Coh}(X)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}

be the open substack of
H
-semi-stable sheaves with Chern character
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
.

Theorem B.10
.

Let
(X,H)
be a polarized Calabi-Yau
4
-fold and
v\in H^{*}(X,\mathbb{Q})
.
Assume that
v_{0}\neq 0
and
v_{1}=0
.
Then, for any choice of orientation, there exists a canonical reduced virtual cycle

[\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}]^{\mathrm{red}}\in A_{\frac{1}{2}\chi(\mathcal{O}_{X})-\frac{1}{2}\chi(v,v)+\frac{1}{2}\rho_{\gamma}}(\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}})

where
\gamma:=v_{2}
.
Moreover, if
[\mathcal{M}^{H,ss}_{v}(X)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}]^{\mathrm{red}}\neq 0
, then the variational Hodge conjecture holds for
(X,\gamma)
.

Proof.

This follows from the arguments in Theorem
4.5
, Theorem
5.1
, and Theorem
5.13
, based on Theorem
B.4
.
This is possible by the existence of the relative moduli stack of semi-stable sheaves as a global quotient stack with proper good moduli space over the base.
We omit the details.
∎

Remark B.11
.

Assuming the expectation in Remark
B.5
, we can generalize Theorem
B.10
to any
1
-Artin open substack of
{\curly Perf}(X)_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}
with (proper) good moduli space, as in Remark
B.9
.

Appendix C
Orientations for relative moduli spaces

In this subsection, we prove that family orientations exist on the topological versions of the moduli spaces. We regard this result as heuristic evidence for Conjecture
5.7
.

C.1.
Orientations for fibrations of topological spaces

We consider the following purely topological statement.

Proposition C.1
.

Let
p:\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{B}
be a (locally trivial) fibration of topological spaces.
Assume that the base
\mathcal{B}
is path connected and locally path connected, and the fibre
\mathcal{C}_{b}:=\mathcal{C}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\{b\}
over a point
b\in\mathcal{B}
has finitely many connected components.
Let
O\to\mathcal{C}
be a principal
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-bundle.
If the fibres
O|_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}
are trivial for all
b\in\mathcal{B}
, then there exists a finite-sheeted covering
u:\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}
such that the pullback
O|_{\mathcal{C}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
is trivial.

Moreover, we can further assume that
\deg(u)=|\pi_{0}(\mathcal{C}_{b})|!
.

Proof.

Fix a point
c\in\mathcal{C}
and let
b:=p(c)\in\mathcal{B}
.
Consider the fibre diagram

\textstyle{O_{c}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{O_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{O\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{c\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}_{b}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p}
\textstyle{\{b\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}}

where
O_{b}:=O|_{\mathcal{C}_{b}}
.
Since the two maps
O\to\mathcal{C}
and
O\to\mathcal{B}
are fibrations, there are monodromy actions

(131)

m:\pi_{1}(\mathcal{C},c)\lefttorightarrow\pi_{0}(O_{c}),\qquad n:\pi_{1}(\mathcal{B},b)\lefttorightarrow\pi_{0}(O_{b}).

Moreover, the canonical map

\pi_{0}(O_{c})\to\pi_{0}(O_{b})

is compatible with the above two monodromy actions in (
131
). Since we have
O_{b}=\mathcal{C}_{b}\bigsqcup\mathcal{C}_{b}
by assumption, there is a canonical bijection

(132)

\pi_{0}(O_{b})\cong\pi_{0}(\mathcal{C}_{b})\times\pi_{0}(O_{c}).

We can form a commutative diagram of groups

(133)

\textstyle{\pi_{1}(\mathcal{C},c)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{*}}
\scriptstyle{m}
\textstyle{\pi_{1}(\mathcal{B},b)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{n}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Aut}(\pi_{0}(O_{c}))\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Aut}(\pi_{0}(O_{b}))}

where the vertical maps are given by the monodromy actions in (
131
), the upper horizontal map is the canonical pushforward for
p
, and the lower horizontal map is given by the decomposition (
132
).

To show that the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-bundle
O\to\mathcal{C}
is trivial, it suffices to show that the left vertical map
m:\pi_{1}(\mathcal{C},c)\to\operatorname{Aut}(\pi_{0}(O_{c}))
in (
133
) is trivial. Since the lower horizontal map in (
133
), given by (
132
), is injective, it suffices to show that the right vertical map
n:\pi_{1}(\mathcal{B},b)\to\operatorname{Aut}(\pi_{0}(O_{b}))
in (
133
) is trivial.

Note that
\pi_{0}(O_{b})
is finite since
\pi_{0}(\mathcal{C}_{b})
is finite by assumption and we have (
132
). Hence the kernel of the map
n:\pi_{1}(\mathcal{B},b)\to\operatorname{Aut}(\pi_{0}(O_{b}))
is a normal subgroup of
\pi_{1}(\mathcal{B},b)
of finite index. Hence there exists a covering map

u:\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}

such that the composition

\pi_{1}(\mathcal{B}^{\prime},b^{\prime})\xrightarrow{u_{*}}\pi_{1}(\mathcal{B},b)\xrightarrow{n}\operatorname{Aut}(\pi_{0}(O_{b}))

is trivial and the degree of
u
divides the order of the finite group
\operatorname{Aut}(\pi_{0}(O_{b}))
.
Then the pullback
O|_{\mathcal{C}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}}
is trivial since the commutative square (
133
) is compatible with base change.

Finally, replacing
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
by its covering, we can further assume that the degree of
u:\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}
is exactly the order of the group
\operatorname{Aut}(\pi_{0}(O_{b}))
.
∎

C.2.
Orientations for relative topological moduli spaces

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a smooth projective morphism of smooth quasi-projective schemes.
Consider the mapping space

\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}):=\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Top}_{/\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{top}}}}(\mathcal{X}^{\mathrm{top}},\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{top}}\times BU\times\mathbb{Z}),

where
(-)^{\mathrm{top}}
denotes the underlying topological space (with the analytic topology). Here we used the modified compact-open topology on the mapping space
\mathrm{Map}_{\mathrm{Top}/\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{top}}}(-,-)
which satisfies adjunction (
[
BB
]
).
Then we have a homotopy fibre diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{b\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}}

for each
b\in\mathcal{B}
, where
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b}):=\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b}/\{b\})
. Note that

\pi_{0}(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b}))=K^{0}_{\mathrm{top}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})

is the topological
K
-theory of
\mathcal{X}_{b}
.

Let
\widetilde{v}
be a horizontal section of
\bigoplus_{p}\mathcal{H}^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
.
Then
\widetilde{v}^{\mathrm{top}}
can be regarded as a section of the
\mathbb{C}
-local system
\bigoplus_{p}R^{2p}f^{\mathrm{top}}_{*}\mathbb{C}
(Remark
3.31
(1)).
Hence we can consider an open subspace

\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})\subseteq\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})

which fits into the fibre diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\widetilde{v}_{b})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\{b\}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{B}}

for each
b\in\mathcal{B}
, where
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\widetilde{v}_{b})=\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b}/\{b\},\widetilde{v}_{b})\subseteq\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
is the union of connected components
\alpha\subseteq\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
whose corresponding
K
-theory classes
[\alpha]
map to
\widetilde{v}_{b}\in H^{*}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathbb{C})
under the topological Chern character map

\operatorname{ch}^{\mathrm{top}}:K^{0}_{\mathrm{top}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})\to H^{*}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\mathbb{Q}).

Corollary C.2
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a smooth projective morphism of smooth quasi-projective schemes.
Assume that
\mathcal{B}
is connected.
Let
\widetilde{v}
be a horizontal section of
\bigoplus_{p}\mathcal{H}^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
.
Let
O\to\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})
be a principal
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-bundle.
If the fibres
O|_{\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\widetilde{v}_{b})}
are trivial for all
b\in\mathcal{B}
, then there exists a finite étale cover
u:\mathcal{B}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{B}
such that the pullback
O|_{\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}^{\prime}/\mathcal{B}^{\prime},\widetilde{v}^{\prime})}
is trivial, where
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}:=\mathcal{X}\times_{\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{B}^{\prime}
and
\widetilde{v}^{\prime}:=u^{*}(\widetilde{v})
.

Moreover, we can further assume that
\deg(u)=|K_{\mathrm{top}}^{0}(\mathcal{X}_{b})_{\mathrm{tor}}|!
where
(-)_{\mathrm{tor}}
denotes the torsion subgroup.

Proof.

By Ehresmann’s theorem
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})\to\mathcal{B}
is a locally trivial fibration.
To apply Proposition
C.1
, it suffices to show that

\pi_{0}(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\widetilde{v}_{b}))=(\operatorname{ch}^{\mathrm{top}})^{-1}(\widetilde{v}_{b})

is finite. This set is bijective to
\ker(\operatorname{ch}^{\mathrm{top}})=K^{0}_{\mathrm{top}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})_{\mathrm{tor}}
. Since
K^{0}_{\mathrm{top}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
is a finitely generated abelian group (by the Atiyah-Hirzebruch spectral sequence),

\pi_{0}(\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b},\widetilde{v}_{b}))\cong K^{0}_{\mathrm{top}}(\mathcal{X}_{b})_{\mathrm{tor}}
is also finite.

Finally, there is a natural equivalence of categories

\{\text{finite {\'{e}}tale covers of $\mathcal{B}$}\}\cong\{\text{finitely-sheeted topological coverings of $\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{top}}$}\}

by
[
SGA1
, Exposé XII, Cor. 5.2]
.
Hence the finite covering in Proposition
C.1
comes from a finite étale map.
∎

Remark C.3
.

Let
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{B}
be a smooth projective morphism of smooth quasi-projective schemes of relative dimension
4
with connected fibres. Assume that the relative canonical bundle
\omega_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B}}
is trivial and
\mathcal{B}
is connected.
Let
\widetilde{v}
be a horizontal section of
\bigoplus_{p}\mathcal{H}^{2p}_{DR}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
.
Let
{\curly Perf}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})
be the moduli stack of all perfect complexes on the fibres of
f
(which exists as a higher Artin stack) whose Chern characters are pullbacks of
\widetilde{v}
. Then there exists a canonical
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-bundle
O\to{\curly Perf}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})
given by the determinant line bundle
\det(\mathbb{E})
of the restriction
\mathbb{E}
of the cotangent complex of the derived enhancement of
{\curly Perf}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})
. We want to know whether the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-bundle
O
is trivial.

If the canonical map

p^{\mathrm{top}}:{\curly Perf}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})^{\mathrm{top}}\to\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{top}}

(given by the topological realization functor
[
Sim
,
Bla
]
) were a locally trivial fibration, then this will follow from Proposition
C.1
.
However,
p^{\mathrm{top}}
is
not
locally trivial in general.

What we can use instead is the topological mapping space. There is a canonical map

{\curly Perf}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})^{\mathrm{top}}\to\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})

and the map

c:\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B},\widetilde{v})\to\mathcal{B}^{\mathrm{top}}

which is a locally trivial fibration.

In view of Corollary
C.2
, the remaining ingredients required to prove Conjecture
5.7
are as follows:

(1)

construct a natural
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
-bundle on
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
whose fibres on
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
are the ones defined by Joyce-Tanaka-Upmeier
[
JTU
]
, and

(2)

compare the
\mathbb{Z}_{2}
bundle in (1) with the natural orientation on
{\curly Perf}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{B})
.

We note that this strategy is exactly the same as the fibrewise result of Cao-Gross-Joyce
[
CGJ
]
. They showed the orientability on
{\curly Perf}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
by (1) constructing an orientation bundle on
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
, (2) showing the compatibility of orientations of
{\curly Perf}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
and
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
, and (3) showing the orientability of
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{X}_{b})
. In their setting, (3) was the most difficult part using topological facts on
5
-manifolds. In our situation, we only need to generalize parts (1) and (2) to the relative setting.

younghan.bae@math.ethz.ch, m.kool1@uu.nl, hyeonjun93@snu.ac.kr
